Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

Tool result:
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

### Anti-Patterns Verdict
**Start here.** Pass/fail: Does this look AI-generated? List specific tells. Be brutally honest.

### Executive Summary

- Total issues found (count by severity: P0/P1/P2/P3)

### Detailed Findings by Severity

Tag every issue with **P0-P3 severity**:
- **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion — fix immediately
- **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation — fix before release
- **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists — fix in next pass
- **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact — fix if time permits

For each issue, document:
- **[P?] Issue name**
- **Location**: Component, file, line
- **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Anti-Pattern
- **Impact**: How it affects users
- **WCAG/Standard**: Which standard it violates (if applicable)
- **Recommendation**: How to fix it
- **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

### Patterns & Systemic Issues

Identify recurring problems that indicate systemic gaps rather than one-off mistakes:
- "Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens"
- "Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience"

### Positive Findings

Note what's working well — good practices to maintain and replicate.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser automation tools are available AND the target is a viewable page. The `[Human]` overlay tab is the user-facing deliverable; the critique is incomplete without it. Skip only if the target is not a viewable page (CSS-only file, non-browser target).

The overlay is a **visual aid for the user**. It highlights issues directly in their browser. Do NOT scroll through the page to screenshot overlays. Instead, read the console output to get the results programmatically.

1. **Start the live detection server**:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live &
   ```
   Note the port printed to stdout (auto-assigned). Use `--port=PORT` to fix it.
2. **Create a new tab** and navigate to the page (use dev server URL for local files, or direct URL). Do not reuse existing tabs.
3. **Label the tab** via `javascript_tool` so the user can distinguish it:
   ```javascript
   document.title = '[Human] ' + document.title;
   ```
4. **Scroll to top** to ensure the page is scrolled to the very top before injection
5. **Inject** via `javascript_tool` (replace PORT with the port from step 1):
   ```javascript
   const s = document.createElement('script'); s.src = 'http://localhost:PORT/detect.js'; document.head.appendChild(s);
   ```
6. Wait 2-3 seconds for the detector to render overlays
7. **Read results from console** using `read_console_messages` with pattern `impeccable`. The detector logs all findings with the `[impeccable]` prefix. Do NOT scroll through the page to take screenshots of the overlays.
8. **Cleanup**: Stop the live server when done:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live stop
   ```

For multi-view targets, inject on 3-5 representative pages. If injection fails, continue with CLI results only.

Return: CLI findings (JSON), browser console findings (if applicable), and any false positives noted.

### Generate Combined Critique Report

Synthesize both assessments into a single report. Do NOT simply concatenate. Weave the findings together, noting where the LLM review and detector agree, where the detector caught issues the LLM missed, and where detector findings are false positives.

Structure your feedback as a design director would:

#### Design Health Score
> *Consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)*

Present the Nielsen's 10 heuristics scores as a table:

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key Issue |
|---|-----------|-------|-----------|
| 1 | Visibility of System Status | ? | [specific finding or "n/a" if solid] |
| 2 | Match System / Real World | ? | |
| 3 | User Control and Freedom | ? | |
| 4 | Consistency and Standards | ? | |
| 5 | Error Prevention | ? | |
| 6 | Recognition Rather Than Recall | ? | |
| 7 | Flexibility and Efficiency | ? | |
| 8 | Aesthetic and Minimalist Design | ? | |
| 9 | Error Recovery | ? | |
| 10 | Help and Documentation | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/40** | **[Rating band]** |

Be honest with scores. A 4 means genuinely excellent. Most real interfaces score 20-32.

#### Anti-Patterns Verdict

**Start here.** Does this look AI-generated?

**LLM assessment**: Your own evaluation of AI slop tells. Cover overall aesthetic feel, layout sameness, generic composition, missed opportunities for personality.

**Deterministic scan**: Summarize what the automated detector found, with counts and file locations. Note any additional issues the detector caught that you missed, and flag any false positives.

**Visual overlays** (if browser was used): Tell the user that overlays are now visible in the **[Human]** tab in their browser, highlighting the detected issues. Summarize what the console output reported.

#### Overall Impression
A brief gut reaction: what works, what doesn't, and the single biggest opportunity.

#### What's Working
Highlight 2-3 things done well. Be specific about why they work.

#### Priority Issues
The 3-5 most impactful design problems, ordered by importance.

For each issue, tag with **P0-P3 severity** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md) for severity definitions):
- **[P?] What**: Name the problem clearly
- **Why it matters**: How this hurts users or undermines goals
- **Fix**: What to do about it (be concrete)
- **Suggested command**: Which command could address this (from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

#### Persona Red Flags
> *Consult [personas](personas.md)*

Auto-select 2-3 personas most relevant to this interface type (use the selection table in the reference). If `AGENTS.md` contains a `## Design Context` section from `impeccable teach`, also generate 1-2 project-specific personas from the audience/brand info.

For each selected persona, walk through the primary user action and list specific red flags found:

**Alex (Power User)**: No keyboard shortcuts detected. Form requires 8 clicks for primary action. Forced modal onboarding. High abandonment risk.

**Jordan (First-Timer)**: Icon-only nav in sidebar. Technical jargon in error messages ("404 Not Found"). No visible help. Will abandon at step 2.

Be specific. Name the exact elements and interactions that fail each persona. Don't write generic persona descriptions; write what broke for them.

#### Minor Observations
Quick notes on smaller issues worth addressing.

#### Questions to Consider
Provocative questions that might unlock better solutions:
- "What if the primary action were more prominent?"
- "Does this need to feel this complex?"
- "What would a confident version of this look like?"

**Remember**:
- Be direct. Vague feedback wastes everyone's time.
- Be specific. "The submit button," not "some elements."
- Say what's wrong AND why it matters to users.
- Give concrete suggestions, not just "consider exploring..."
- Prioritize ruthlessly. If everything is important, nothing is.
- Don't soften criticism. Developers need honest feedback to ship great design.

### Ask the User

**After presenting findings**, use targeted questions based on what was actually found. STOP and use Codex's structured user-input/question tool when available; if unavailable, ask directly in chat to clarify what you cannot infer. These answers will shape the action plan.

Ask questions along these lines (adapt to the specific findings; do NOT ask generic questions):

1. **Priority direction**: Based on the issues found, ask which category matters most to the user right now. For example: "I found problems with visual hierarchy, color usage, and information overload. Which area should we tackle first?" Offer the top 2-3 issue categories as options.

2. **Design intent**: If the critique found a tonal mismatch, ask whether it was intentional. For example: "The interface feels clinical and corporate. Is that the intended tone, or should it feel warmer/bolder/more playful?" Offer 2-3 tonal directions as options based on what would fix the issues found.

3. **Scope**: Ask how much the user wants to take on. For example: "I found N issues. Want to address everything, or focus on the top 3?" Offer scope options like "Top 3 only", "All issues", "Critical issues only".

4. **Constraints** (optional; only ask if relevant): If the findings touch many areas, ask if anything is off-limits. For example: "Should any sections stay as-is?" This prevents the plan from touching things the user considers done.

**Rules for questions**:
- Every question must reference specific findings from the report. Never ask generic "who is your audience?" questions.
- Keep it to 2-4 questions maximum. Respect the user's time.
- Offer concrete options, not open-ended prompts.
- If findings are straightforward (e.g., only 1-2 clear issues), skip questions and go directly to Recommended Actions.

### Recommended Actions

**After receiving the user's answers**, present a prioritized action summary reflecting the user's priorities and scope from Ask the User.

#### Action Summary

List recommended commands in priority order, based on the user's answers:

1. **`$command-name`**: Brief description of what to fix (specific context from critique findings)
2. **`$command-name`**: Brief description (specific context)
...

**Rules for recommendations**:
- Only recommend commands from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset
- Order by the user's stated priorities first, then by impact
- Each item's description should carry enough context that the command knows what to focus on
- Map each Priority Issue to the appropriate command
- Skip commands that would address zero issues
- If the user chose a limited scope, only include items within that scope
- If the user marked areas as off-limits, exclude commands that would touch those areas
- End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended

After presenting the summary, tell the user:

> You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.
>
> Re-run `$impeccable critique` after fixes to see your score improve.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project snapshot

LSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.

## Second brain (product memory)

Product context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.

Path sanity check first:

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`

If any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

Protocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:

- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.
- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.

Where LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):

The vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:

- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`
- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`
- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`
- A student's whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule
- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)

**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:

- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)
- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`
- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)
- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`
- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`

Rules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.

## Commands

Scripts (from `package.json`):

- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080
- `npm run build` — production build
- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags
- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle
- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)
- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)
- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode
- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`

Package manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.

- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.
- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

No Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.

## Architecture (big picture)

- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.
- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.
- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.
- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.
- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.
- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.
- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.
- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.
- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.
- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.

# Product

<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->

## Scope

App-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.

A rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.

The repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.

## Platform

web

## Users

**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.

**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.

**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.

# Activity Centre — surface brief

## Scope and mode

The Activity Centre wing: the home list (`/activities`), each Activity's intro, and the eight worlds that fill the runner's left column. Mode: **Experience** for the worlds (the student is inside the room the skill lives in), **Operate** for the answer loop on the right, which stays on the module's cream surface untouched.

## Direction (locked 2026-08-21)

**Eight worlds, eight universes.** Every Activity opens into a fully committed physical scene, saturated and material, scoped behind its wrapper class so nothing leaks into the shell. This is a deliberate, owner-approved departure from the app-wide monochrome-editorial restraint: the Centre is the one wing where the room IS the teaching instrument. The Coach and answer loop remain the constant; the room changes.

| World | Activity | Scene | Display face | Key hues |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | obsidian void split by an aurora; survey-grid floor; prismatic glass plate | Italiana | ultraviolet 275°, polar cyan 190° |
| Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | Victorian naturalist's study; green wall, parchment bench, bell jar on brass plinth | EB Garamond | botanical green 162°, brass 40°, madder 8° |
| Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | celestial cartographer's dome; nebulae, twinkling stars, gilt ribs | Marcellus | indigo 233°, gold 45°, comet cyan 187°, rose 330° |
| Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | midnight rail yard; sodium floods, fog, converging track fan, solari board | Barlow Condensed | steel-night 213°, sodium 36°, signal emerald 158° |
| Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | stained-glass workshop; lamp cone, dust, tinted panes in lead cames | Young Serif | umber 24°, lamp gold 40°, glass tints rotating |
| Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | torchlit porphyry rotunda; bronze protractor inlaid in the floor | Cinzel | porphyry 356°, torchlight 36°, bronze 30° |
| The Press | Dumb it Down | hot-metal print shop; ink rollers, iron frame, vermilion proof red | Bevan | iron 20°, paper cream 42°, vermilion 14° |
| The Loom | Spot the Callback | indigo dye-house; warp threads, linen sheets with selvage, saffron thread | Alegreya | indigo 236°, linen 43°, saffron 42° |

Statement/body text across dark worlds runs Spectral 300; micro-labels stay on the shared `--ac-mono`.

## Standing rules

- **Scene layers are furniture, not content.** Every world's atmosphere lives in one `aria-hidden` layer stack (`.po-room`, `.sl-room`, …) painted below `z-index: 2` content; tested DOM never gains or loses nodes for scenery.
- **Meaning colors survive grayscale.** Hue is atmosphere on top of geometry/weight/position, never the only carrier of a verdict. Emerald still means "earned/true" wherever it appears.
- **One orchestrated entrance per world**, durations pinned by tests (1800–2500ms), always skippable, `useReducedMotion` short-circuits to `onDone()`.
- **Reduced motion**: every world CSS ends with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block; continuous loops stop rather than speed up.
- **The home is a hall of doorways.** Each `ActivityCard` carries a pure-CSS diorama of its world (`activity-scenes.css`, keyed by slug); the card's facts stay on the module paper below the scene. The intro borrows `--act-accent` from its world via the registry.
- **Registry accents** (`worlds/registry.tsx`) are each world's signature hue and feed the card eyebrow + intro accent.

## Unresolved

- Negation Station is not in `SEED_ACTIVITIES`, so its diorama only shows once assigned.
- Progress ornaments (chips, plaques, mileposts, seals…) remain unreachable from the runner; feeding them real verdicts is its own piece of work.

## Read, Recall, Colour door (locked 2026-09-05)

The two RRC doors (`SectionLibraryLaunch`, `SectionLaunch`, both drawn by `SectionLaunchFrame`) are a committed world of their own, mode **Persuade** at the top and **Operate** in the slab. Joshua's brief: a layered, high-end landing page in the spirit of heylemon.ai (cream ground, painted panels, serif display with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, a dictation demo) with fora.so's depth-plane hero, and bold step 1 to 4 instructions.

- **World:** one dusk. The runner's indigo (`#1a1635`) lifted into a sky over a painted meadow, cream paper (`#f6f3ea`) beneath. Display is Fraunces at opsz 144; body Inter; labels IBM Plex Mono. Marigold marks the Colour step, emerald marks a carried detail, nothing else is coloured.
- **Hero:** `MeadowScene` paints five depth planes generatively in SVG (seeded ridges, seeded grass blades, feTurbulence grain) so no raster ships; each plane rides `useScroll` at its own rate and the foreground grass crosses in front of the demo card. The headline blurs and recedes on the same scroll value.
- **Signature moment:** `RecallDemo`, a looping performance of the method on a synthetic stimulus, labelled as such, whose text literally dissolves at the Recall step.
- **Steps:** four cards, each with a small drawn scene, doubling as the ARIA tablist the tests already contract (`sl-beat`, `sl-beat-tab-N`, `sl-beat-panel`). The full briefing is the second tab of the drawer.
- **Tokens:** scoped to `.sl` as `--rd-*`. The design detector reports them as outside DESIGN.md; that is the sanctioned-world exception above, not drift.

## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)

Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream, not
emerald, and not grey** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI
looking", "I don't want emerald", "it should be much more colorful"). The second
sanctioned departure from the wing's surface after the RRC door.

- **Two grounds.** A deep plum console (`oklch(0.245 0.058 312)`) holds the
  verdict, the transport and the ledger; a violet-cast white page
  (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`) holds everything she reads at length.
- **Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity.** The mechanic is
  Grammarly's, which colours by category and reserves red for outright errors.
  Four fixed tones: **Detail** plum-rose, **Read** teal, **Colour** gold,
  **Method** indigo. A miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of, which is
  what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure.
- **Signal magenta is sound, not a category.** It marks the line currently
  playing and nothing else, held at hue 328 so it separates from the
  plum-rose dropped chips it shares a line with.
- **Hues chosen from research, not taste** (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). Saturated
  right now: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and
  terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft
  cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. Green and cream are the
  owner's veto; orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used
  list. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is
  (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary), a system built to hold AA.
- **Band is fill, not hue.** The ledger's marks differ by how full they are, in
  gold; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow rose ring. It survives
  grayscale, which is what lets category own the colour.
- **One stimulus at a time.** Twenty-two accordion rows became a ledger of
  twenty-two marks plus one open stimulus in a fixed order: the passage, what
  you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.
- **No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and she has
  never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says
  "Almost all of it came back" instead (Joshua, 2026-09-08).
- **Contrast is measured, not assumed.** `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs` converts
  computed colours through a canvas (Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`) and
  every text node clears 4.5:1 on its own ground, in the default, playing and
  clean states.
- **Type ramp:** Fraunces display, Lexend body, IBM Plex Mono labels. The design
  detector reports the sizes as outside DESIGN.md; that is this sanctioned world,
  not drift, the same exception the RRC door's `--rd-*` tokens carry.
- **Harness:** `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders it at twenty-two
  stimuli without `App.tsx`. `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to
  freeze the playhead on a line. `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs` captures the matrix.

# Read, Recall, Colour section briefing page: design critique

Date: 2026-09-08. Method: `$impeccable critique` on
`src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx`, two isolated
passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, and the deterministic
detector) woven into one report. Related: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

## Design health score

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | "One take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" are not on the page |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 3 | "The Recall." reads as a typo beside three imperatives |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | Back is 44x44 and works; nothing else needed |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three micro-label styles; the two H2s render at different weights |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | No warning that the browser will ask for the microphone |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | A " +" text glyph is the only disclosure affordance |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Space is the runner's only key but does nothing here |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say the product name; foot restates the notice |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Mic-denied case has no hint on this page |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The collapsed long version is exactly right |
| **Total** | | **27/40** | **Acceptable, upper end** |

## Anti-patterns verdict

Does it look AI-made? No. Single column, four numbered steps, one primary
action, Fraunces title on cream, no hero metric, no icon grid, no gradient text,
no side stripes, no em dashes in the rendered DOM. The one drift toward generic
is the Start button: a landing-page pill with a hover lift and drop shadow,
written for the dark hero and inherited by this cream task page.

Deterministic scan: `npx impeccable --json` on the page returned zero findings.
The directory scan of `components/` returned 53 advisory findings, none touching
this page or `section-launch.css` (47 are in `activity-scenes.css`). No false
positives to triage. Browser overlay could not run: impeccable 4.0.4 ships no
`detect.js`, and the page has no CSP, so this is a tooling gap and not a page
problem. Cognitive load: 1 of 8 checks failed (working memory), which is low.

## Overall impression

The structure is right and the copy is the best on the platform. The misses are
content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout. One of them matters: the page was
edited on 2026-09-07 on the belief that the one-take fact survived in Step 4.
It did not. It lives in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page never renders.

## What's working

- A page, not a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. "Laughing is
  fine." and "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours." earn their place.
- The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper, and the comments say why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: H1 H2 H2 H3, Back 44x44, 2px focus
  rings, no live-region noise.

## Priority issues

**[P1] The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute"
only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading. The code comment at
`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63` claims Step 4 carries it. Step 4 does not.
Fix: one plain sentence beside the foot Start: "It runs in one take, about
twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer`,
which the type describes as "the last beat before the Start action" and which
nothing renders. Correct the comment. Command: `$impeccable clarify`.

**[P1] The calming notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
`.sl-notice` and `.sl-bp-list li` share fill, hairline, and radius. The one
sentence written to lower her shoulders reads as procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. A plain ink line under the CTA. Command: `$impeccable layout`.

**[P2] Step body lines run about 105 characters.** The essay caps at 68ch; the
step list has no measure. Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and
`.sl-notice`. Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] Three micro-label styles on one page, and mismatched H2s.** Mono chip,
Inter-uppercase h2, Inter-uppercase step number, while `.sl-label` is declared
the one style and goes unused. The summary's h2 inherits body weight, so the
only help on the page looks like a stray sentence. Fix: put step numbers and
h2s on `.sl-label`; give the summary the same h2 treatment and a real chevron.
Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] The lede is silently overridden.** `.sl-essay p` outranks `.rd-lede`, so
the 20px italic lede renders at 16.5px. Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede`.
Command: `$impeccable polish`.

**[P3] Landing-page button on a task page.** Drop the transform and outer
shadow on `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`; keep the fill darkening.
Command: `$impeccable quieter`.

## Persona red flags

- **Jordan (first-timer):** reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space
  button; presses Start and meets a microphone permission dialog with no warning.
- **Sam (screen reader):** an `h2` nested inside `summary` is exposed
  inconsistently; some readers drop it from the headings list, the very outline
  the h2 was added to fix.
- **Casey (mobile):** the reassurance sits at the top and the Start she presses
  is 1,000px below it. Same fix as the first P1. Mobile was reasoned from the
  CSS, not seen: the viewport resize did not take.
- **A coaching client at 9pm:** wants "how long is this and can I stop if my
  roommate walks in." The page answers neither, and nothing on it is addressed
  from her coach.

## Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is redundant here.
- The head's side-by-side layout never fits at 840px, so the CTA always wraps
  under the tagline. What ships is accidental.
- Empty tagline still renders an empty `p` with top margin.
- Foot copy repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact.
- Summary focus ring is `--rd-ink-2`, buttons are `--rd-ink`.
- `.sl-bp-meta` is defined and unused.

## Questions to consider

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's only key is Space, what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. What would be missing if the page were only the notice, the four steps, and one Start at the foot?

## Recommended order

1. `$impeccable clarify`: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot, render `closer`, fix the comment.
2. `$impeccable layout`: un-box the notice.
3. `$impeccable typeset`: 62ch measure, one label style, matching H2s, lede fix.
4. `$impeccable quieter`: the Start button's lift and shadow.
5. `$impeccable polish` last.

607:## 2026-09-08 (05:20 UTC) — RRC infrastructure audit: the 37-minute offset was an orphaned recorder plus an overwriting recovery
608-
609-Root cause of Celia's clock offset, proven from the data: her assignment was opened at 23:28:48, the recorder that stamped her rows started at 00:06:27 (37 min 39 s later), her file uploaded at 01:12:34, and storage shows the object **updated at 01:30:49**. The first sitting's recorder was never stopped on unmount (only the finish path called `stop`), so it kept the microphone open for two hours and kept writing backup chunks; when she reloaded after finishing, the recovery pass took that orphan for a crashed run and uploaded its 116-minute file over her real 66-minute recording with `upsert: true`. Both sittings were Attempt 1 because the first wrote no rows, so both named the same path.
610-
611-Fixed in the app, with red-then-green tests: the recorder hook closes both recorders and the mic tracks in its own unmount cleanup; the runner discards the backup of any run that unmounts without finishing (keyed on `saving`, because `finishing` is raised on every unmount); `discard` joins the per-run write chain and marks the run so a recorder's final chunk cannot resurrect it; recovery uploads with `upsert: false`, forgets a run on a 409, and tries newest sitting first. ADR 0034 written. Tests: 96 files / 2,674 passing, typecheck clean, one pre-existing lint warning.

619:Line-by-line pairing was cutting only at silences ≥1.2 s and dealing whole bursts to lines, so a student who retold three lines in one breath had everything filed under line 1 and lines 2–3 shown as "You did not say anything for this line" while their detail lists said she carried them (Celia, PT1.S3 Q1). Replaced with a word-level monotone split by content (`supabase/functions/_shared/rrcLines.ts`): each line takes one contiguous stretch of her words in order, chosen by DP; silence and full stops are free cut points, a comma is half price, a mid-phrase cut costs 2 hits. Six Deno tests, two built from her real stimuli (`rrcLines.test.ts`). On her run: empty lines fell to 6 of 74; the "the mathe | theory" mid-phrase cut is gone.
620-
621-Grader now has `BUILD = "2026-09-08d"` back (Lovable's 04:03 edit had replaced it with the schema version) and an admin-only `{ diagnose: true }` call that names the deploy and judge backend and proves the key with one call. Verified live at 05:35 UTC: `judge: gemini-direct`, `judge_ok: true`, transcriber Deepgram. So the judge IS on Joshua's direct Gemini key, model `gemini-3.8-flash` (Lovable's commits a222e72…6c5556a at 05:25–05:29 switched every function's model at his request). Deploys went through Lovable's agent, deploy-only, 0.4–1 credit each; Lovable's working copy had diverged from GitHub and it resynced the three grader files by hand; its HEAD is at c95c3a8 now.
622-
623-**The model switch changed the answers.** Celia re-graded on 3.8-flash: mean coverage 89 → 86, Q1 82% → 59% ("Even antibiotics that are strong still struggle to keep bacteria out" now marked as dropping "even the strong ones fail"), coloured 6 → 0, Reads given 1 → 0, decoys carried 1 → 0. Charles (graded 05:16, before the switch) is on 2.5-flash. The standard's calibration (ADR 0017/0018 census, 94.7 vs judge 93.1) was measured on 2.5-flash and does not transfer. Decision needed: re-run the standard census on 3.8-flash and re-set the bands, or pin the judge back to 2.5-flash. Until then the bands and the colour verdicts are not comparable across students.
624-
625-Not verified live in a browser: the Report Card rendering of the new lines (component unchanged; the data shape is the same).

--
629-Joshua's brief: one consistent judge, fair harshness with clear criteria, and a post-recording card where a student can replay, read, see the golden standard and see exactly what she missed and how to improve, unless she hit every cylinder. Drafts and student replies deprioritised by him.

Result on 71 of 71: mean 91.7, median 93, p10 82, 0 decoy failures (2.5-flash had 4), reads matched 68/71, coloured 71/71. `BAND_FLOOR` moved 86 → 82 (68, 51 provisional, same widths). Rows saved at `docs/adr/census-2026-09-08-gemini-direct.json`.

Grade rows now also store `stimulus_text` and `the_read`.

Em-dash removed from the recall line. Verified in the browser on the lab fixture (`/activity-lab` → report) via Playwright screenshots; 52 card tests, 2,685 Activity Centre tests passing; typecheck clean.

Both rows carry next_time on all 22. Celia's 0 coloured is about her run, not the model: the census judged Joshua's runs coloured 71/71.

Screenshots at 1180px and 390px via `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs`.

That was wrong. The card gates on measured criteria, not the empty string, so it fires correctly; Celia never triggers it because she gave no Read on any of the 22, which is the right answer. The narrower finding stands: the judge has never once left `next_time` empty.

673-## 2026-09-08 (11:20 UTC) — Ivory could not open his drill, and nothing was watching
--
675-Two mails from Ivory Johnson III. Monday: "would you know when I should be able to see the assigned work on my portal?" Tuesday, with a screenshot: "Not open yet. This part of the classroom isn't in your plan right now."
676-
677:**Not a broken link.** `has_activity_access` was `false` on his profile while `has_classroom_access` was `true`, which is why he could sign in and reach the classroom and it looked like the URL was at fault. One flag caused both mails: `/activities/:id` sits behind `ProtectedRoute flag="has_activity_access"`, and Classroom's own feed drops activity rows for anyone without it (`useClassroomHome.ts:189`), so his three RRC sections were invisible AND unreachable. He is the only one of nine students with RRC assigned who was missing it; his was a hand-granted subset (classroom, drill, chat, schedule) that missed activity. Turned it on at Joshua's go-ahead. There is no per-drill flag and there cannot be one, but the scoping was moot: `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments and he has zero non-RRC activities, so the flag opened exactly the three RRC sections and nothing else.
678-
679-**The systemic fix.** `scripts/assign.ts` now grants the flag the assigned work needs, after the write, never before, granting nothing else and revoking nothing; it fails loudly rather than reporting a clean assign. `scripts/access-audit.ts` is the read-only sweep for work arriving by other paths (admin UI, Lovable-side writes, hand-run SQL) and names problems without fixing them, because opening a feature on a student's account is Joshua's call. Currently clean: 13 students with assigned work, all able to open it. Commit `15a5a2b`.
680-
681-**Reply drafted, not sent:** Gmail draft in thread `1a07c3c954127a8c`, copy at `wiki/IVORY-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md`. Owns it in the first line, names the cause, tells him the three sections and to re-sign-in if the page is stale.
682-
683-**The lesson, which is the point of writing this down.** The product had no opinion about whether a student could open what it had just written to their account. Every route is gated and every feed filters, so a missing flag is not a locked door, it is an empty page: the failure is silent on both sides, and the only detector was a student polite enough to send a screenshot. When a surface is gated AND its feed filters on the same flag, assigning into it needs a guarantee at the write, not a hope. Next time work is assigned by any path, check the student can reach it in the same breath.

## user

Tool result:
tween sessions.

For a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.

For Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.

Success is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.

## Positioning

**A real coach is in it.**

The claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.

## Operating Context

The platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.

- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.
- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).
- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.
- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.
- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.

## Capabilities and Constraints

Confirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teacher workspace.

Technical constraints:

- React, TypeScript, Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui and Radix primitives, React Router, TanStack Query, Supabase.
- One motion library (`framer-motion`). One primary icon library (`lucide-react`). New work composes existing primitives before introducing a new dependency.
- Deployment is not a side effect of pushing to `main`. Production at lsatprep.study requires an explicit publish step.
- Database migrations in `supabase/migrations/` are an archive, not an applied ledger. Production schema changes go through their own path, and UI must not assume a table exists because a migration file does.

Terminology is not defined here. It is defined per bounded context and mapped by `CONTEXT-MAP.md`. A word can carry different weight in different contexts, and collapsing them has already caused real bugs (a **flag** is not a **wrong answer**; **Set** and **Position** were separated for this reason).

## Brand Commitments

- **Name:** LSAT U, operating under Aspiring Attorneys. Production domain lsatprep.study.
- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect reads.** Joshua does not type them. A stray em-dash reads as machine-written and costs trust. This binds computed copy, utility copy, and authored copy alike.
- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a "coach voice, not industry voice" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua's decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.

## Evidence on Hand

Real:

- A live production deployment at lsatprep.study with real enrolled students.
- Authored LSAT curriculum: bootcamp modules, guided drill banks, RC passages with per-question explanations, and practice test content.
- Real student work in the database: attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers, assignments.
- A real named instructor with a real coaching practice behind the product.

Absent, and never to be fabricated:

- No testimonials, case studies, press coverage, score-improvement statistics, or named customer references exist in this repo. Do not write them into any surface.
- No pricing, licensing, or availability claims are established here.
- No third-party or copyright framing anywhere in student-facing copy.

## Product Principles

1. **Truthfulness over performance.** Every number, badge, streak, status, and progress claim traces to real stored data. An honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops. This is the one principle carried forward unchanged from the previous record, and it is the hardest of the five.

2. **Compute the next step; do not present a grid.** A student arriving at a surface should be told what to do, not shown a menu and left to choose. A card grid is what a product offers when it does not know the student.

3. **Attempt before explanation.** The student commits to an answer before the product explains anything. Passive reading does not build the skill. Reveal copy addresses what the student actually chose, not a generic key.

4. **A measurement is never a gate.** Drills and checks measure after teaching. A miss is framed as the next thing to work on, not a grade or a failure. Nothing in the product rewards attendance rather than work.

5. **The coach is not simulated.** Where the product speaks for Joshua, it is because Joshua said it. Where he has not, the product does not fill the silence with something that sounds like him. This is the operational form of the positioning claim.

## Accessibility & Inclusion

**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.

- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
- Interactive targets meet the 44x44px minimum, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text.
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.
