import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import type { Database } from "@/integrations/supabase/types";
import { completeSectionRun, requestSectionGrading } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import { coachNote, hasSeenWalkthrough, markWalkthroughSeen } from "./coach";
import { useSectionRecorder, type RecordingSegment } from "./useSectionRecorder";
import { sliceTranscript, useSectionTranscript } from "./useSectionTranscript";
import { beginBackup, discard, putChunk, stashRows } from "./recordingBackup";
import { beginLiveRun } from "@/lib/liveRun";
import {
  advanceCursor,
  buildSectionRun,
  startCursor,
  type Accommodation,
  type SectionCursor,
} from "./section";
import "./section.css";

/**
 * The generated insert shape for one attempt row, plus `rrc`.
 *
 * Same intersection, and the same reason, as `RecallRunner`: the column was
 * added by `20260829140000_recall_responses.sql` and `types.ts` is generated
 * from the deployed schema, so it will only learn the column the next time
 * types are pulled against a database that has the migration applied. Delete
 * the intersection once they are.
 */
type ResponseInsert = Database["public"]["Tables"]["activity_item_responses"]["Insert"];

/**
 * Where a section's recording is kept. Private, and readable only by the
 * student it belongs to and the instructor; see the bucket's own migration.
 */
const RECORDING_BUCKET = "drill-recordings";

/**
 * A Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run: 22 to 26 stimuli, end to end, one clock,
 * no stopping.
 *
 * Two rules shape everything here, both from
 * `docs/specs/2026-08-31-rrc-section-mode.md` section 4, and both negative:
 *
 * - **No coach in the loop.** Nothing is judged, shown or scored until the
 *   section ends. A live verdict trains her to vet before speaking, which is
 *   the exact habit colouring exists to break.
 * - **Nothing the loop waits on.** A dropped connection must not be able to
 *   stall the student mid-section. This used to be enforced by writing nothing
 *   at all until the last stimulus, which held for twenty minutes of work in
 *   the tab's memory and lost every second of it to a crash, a closed lid or a
 *   flat battery. The rule it was protecting is that the loop must not WAIT,
 *   not that nothing may be written -- so each stimulus is now handed to the
 *   database as it finishes, fired and forgotten, from an effect rather than
 *   from the keypress. Nothing is awaited between one stimulus and the next,
 *   and a write that fails is picked up by the backstop at the end.
 *
 *   A section is still the unit of work: half a section is not a partial score
 *   and never becomes one. That is enforced where it belongs, in the grader,
 *   which grades a run only once it is marked finished. Saving and judging are
 *   different acts, and conflating them is what cost the twenty minutes.
 *
 * It records. One unbroken file for the whole section, started before the loop
 * and stopped after it, exactly as the drill requires, uploaded once at the end
 * to a private bucket only she and the instructor can read. Every phase
 * boundary is stamped as an offset into that file, so each portion of each
 * stimulus is addressable without the recorder ever having been paused. See
 * `useSectionRecorder`.
 *
 * A refused or missing microphone does not stop the drill. It says so and the
 * section continues, because the drill is still a valid drill run against a
 * handheld recorder, and twenty minutes is far too long to spend talking into
 * nothing without being told.
 */

export interface SectionRunnerProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  studentId: string;
  /** The assignment's snapshot: items, the clock, and which section it is. */
  items: unknown;
  sourceSection: string | null | undefined;
  timeGoalMinutes: unknown;
  /**
   * Which goal her time is scored against. Defaults to standard; a student with
   * 53% or 70% extra time is not slow when she takes longer, and grading her
   * against the standard goal would score her accommodation as a failure.
   */
  accommodation?: Accommodation;
  onComplete?: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
  /** Injected in tests. Real runs use the wall clock. */
  now?: () => number;
  /**
   * The ready beat, in seconds. Injected in tests so a run can be driven
   * without waiting out three real seconds; every real run takes the default.
   */
  readySeconds?: number;
}

/**
 * What one stimulus's run looked like, written once the section is over.
 *
 * Stimulus-grained, like the cursor: time with the text up, time recalling
 * from memory, and nothing per chunk, because no chunk boundary was observed
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
 */
interface StimulusRecord {
  slug: string;
  order: number;
  /** Seconds from the section's start to the end of this stimulus. */
  finishedAtSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds spent on this stimulus alone. */
  elapsedSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds with the text on screen: her reads, recalls and colouring. */
  workingSeconds: number;
  /** Seconds spent giving The Recall, text gone. */
  recallSeconds: number;
}

/** Stable identity so hooks keyed on `now` do not restart every render. */
const defaultNow = () => Date.now();

/**
 * How long the ready beat runs before the first stimulus.
 *
 * Long enough to read one line and see the level move, short enough that it is
 * never in the way of a student on her fourth section of the week.
 */
const READY_SECONDS = 3;

/** A row gets a few quiet chances while she is still working, never an unbounded loop. */
const ROW_RETRY_ATTEMPTS = 3;
const ROW_RETRY_BASE_MS = 500;
const ROW_RETRY_MAX_MS = 4_000;

export function SectionRunner({
  assignmentId,
  studentId,
  items,
  sourceSection,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  accommodation = "standard",
  onComplete,
  onExit,
  now = defaultNow,
  readySeconds = READY_SECONDS,
}: SectionRunnerProps) {
  const run = useMemo(
    () => buildSectionRun(items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes),
    [items, sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes],
  );
  if (!run) {
    return (
      <div className="ac-section">
        <div className="ac-section-body">
          <p className="ac-section-instruction">This section could not be read</p>
          {onExit ? (
            <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
              Leave
            </button>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  return (
    <RunBody
      run={run}
      assignmentId={assignmentId}
      studentId={studentId}
      accommodation={accommodation}
      onComplete={onComplete}
      onExit={onExit}
      now={now}
      readySeconds={readySeconds}
    />
  );
}

function RunBody({
  run,
  assignmentId,
  studentId,
  accommodation,
  onComplete,
  onExit,
  now,
  readySeconds,
}: {
  run: NonNullable<ReturnType<typeof buildSectionRun>>;
  assignmentId: string;
  studentId: string;
  accommodation: Accommodation;
  onComplete?: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
  now: () => number;
  readySeconds: number;
}) {
  const [cursor, setCursor] = useState<SectionCursor>(startCursor);
  const [done, setDone] = useState(false);
  /**
   * The first-run walkthrough, on for a student who has never run a section.
   *
   * Read once, at mount, and marked seen at the same moment: it is a
   * first-section thing, not a first-finished-section thing, so an abandoned
   * first run does not bring it back. She can also hide it early.
   */
  const [coachOn, setCoachOn] = useState(() => !hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId));
  useEffect(() => {
    if (coachOn) markWalkthroughSeen(studentId);
    // Deliberately once, on mount, against the value read at mount.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, []);
  /**
   * Seconds left in the ready beat, counting to zero, and null once she is
   * working.
   *
   * The microphone opens on mount and the clock used to start with it, so the
   * first thing a 25-minute unrepeatable take captured was a student still
   * arranging herself -- and the very first frame she saw was already stimulus
   * one, with no moment in between that said the recording had begun. Three
   * seconds is the whole fix: the recorder is live through it, so the level
   * indicator is moving and a dead microphone is visible BEFORE any of it
   * costs her anything, and the section's own clock does not start until it
   * ends.
   */
  const [countdown, setCountdown] = useState<number | null>(readySeconds);
  /** Confirming the way out. Leaving throws the whole run away. */
  const [confirmingExit, setConfirmingExit] = useState(false);
  /**
   * Nothing reaching the microphone. Shown rather than logged: it is the one
   * fault that otherwise ends in a perfect-looking run and a card of zeros.
   *
   * Two-way, and it was not. `setDeadInput(true)` was the only call in the
   * tree, so the warning could be raised and never lowered: one skipped Recall
   * put "Nothing is reaching the microphone" on the screen at 121s and left it
   * there for the remaining twenty-five stimuli, through four minutes of speech
   * the drill heard perfectly well. `VoiceLevel` now reports the state rather
   * than announcing the fault, and this is the setter for both directions.
   */
  const [deadInput, setDeadInput] = useState(false);
  /** The recording did not reach the server. Said plainly at the end. */
  const [uploadFailed, setUploadFailed] = useState(false);
  /** Response rows still absent after the per-row end-of-run backstop. */
  const [unsavedCount, setUnsavedCount] = useState(0);
  /** True only after a real recording has reached storage. */
  const [recordingStored, setRecordingStored] = useState(false);
  /**
   * This sitting's own name for its local backup.
   *
   * Made before anything else, because pieces of the recording start arriving
   * long before the Attempt number comes back, and every piece has to be
   * filed under something. The run record that says where the pieces belong is
   * written as soon as the Attempt is known.
   */
  /**
   * She stepped away. The section is still open and its clock is not running.
   *
   * A section is twenty-five unbroken minutes and is scored whole, which used to
   * mean the only way out of one was the way that throws it away. So a student
   * who had to answer the door had two options and both were bad: sit through
   * the interruption on the clock, or lose twenty-five minutes of work. She
   * asked for the third one.
   *
   * A pause is deliberately not a small thing. The stimulus goes off the screen
   * with it, because a paused stimulus she can still read is not a break, it is
   * unlimited untimed reading time and it would quietly make the drill
   * meaningless. The recorder pauses rather than stops, so it is still one file.
   * And how long she was away is stored on the run rather than dissolved into

    for (const timer of retryTimers.current) clearTimeout(timer);
    retryTimers.current.clear();
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      const total = seconds(startedAt.current);

      /*
       * Stop the recorder and put the file away.
       *
       * The path was decided before the section began, so the rows written
       * during the run already name this file. Uploading is the act of making
       * that name true.
       *
       * A failed upload does not fail the section. Twenty minutes of work and
       * the timings are still worth keeping, and the grader treats a file that
       * is not there as "not captured", which is the truth.
       */
      const finished = await stopRecording();
      const path = audioPath();
      let storedRecording = false;
      if (finished && finished.blob.size > 0 && path) {
        /*
         * Once, then once more, then say so.
         *
         * A failed upload used to be a console line, and its consequence
         * arrived minutes later somewhere else entirely: the grader refuses a
         * run with no recording, so a dropped connection at this exact moment
         * turned twenty-five minutes of correct work into "Not scored" with
         * nothing anywhere saying why. It is a real fault and it is hers to
         * know about, because the recovery needs her to open the section again.
         */
        let upload = (
          await supabase.storage
            .from(RECORDING_BUCKET)
            .upload(path, finished.blob, { contentType: finished.mimeType, upsert: true })
        ).error;
        if (upload) {
          console.error("[rrc] could not store the recording; retrying once", upload);
          upload = (
            await supabase.storage
              .from(RECORDING_BUCKET)
              .upload(path, finished.blob, { contentType: finished.mimeType, upsert: true })
          ).error;
        }
        if (upload) {
          console.error("[rrc] could not store the recording", upload);
          if (!cancelled) setUploadFailed(true);
        }
        // The real recording is safely away, so the local copy has done its
        // job. Deliberately only on success: an upload that failed is exactly
        // when the backup is the only copy there is, and the next open of this
        // section is what sends it.
        else {
          storedRecording = true;
          void discard(runId.current);
        }
      }
      if (!cancelled) setRecordingStored(storedRecording);

      /*
       * Her words, cut at the same boundaries as the audio.
       *
       * Both channels are stamped in milliseconds from the same start, so the
       * text for one portion and the audio for that portion are the same slice
       * of the same run rather than two things lined up by hand afterwards.
       */
      const said = transcript();
      const rows: (ResponseInsert & { rrc: unknown })[] = records.current.map((r) =>
        buildRow(r, said, total),
      );
      let missingRows = 0;

      if (rows.length > 0) {
        /*
         * The backstop, not the write.
         *
         * Every stimulus was already sent as it finished. This re-sends the
         * whole run so that anything the network dropped along the way still
         * lands, and duplicates are declined rather than treated as failures.
         * On a clean run it inserts nothing at all.
         */
        // Let every save already in flight settle first. Otherwise an initial
        // save could succeed just after a failed backstop and make the warning
        // false before it was even painted.
        await Promise.allSettled([...inFlightRows.current]);
        const stored = await Promise.all(rows.map((row) => persistRow(row as ResponseInsert)));
        missingRows = stored.filter((ok) => !ok).length;
        if (missingRows > 0) {
          console.error("[rrc] response rows still missing after the backstop", { missing: missingRows });
          if (!cancelled) setUnsavedCount(missingRows);
          /*
           * The way back, which these rows did not have.
           *
           * They live in `records.current`, in memory, in a component about to
           * unmount. She leaves this screen and the answers are gone, and
           * ADR 0027 then refuses the whole run for ever -- including the
           * recording, which may have uploaded perfectly. So the ones that did
           * not land go where the recording's own backup goes, and the next
           * entry into any section sends them.
           *
           * Not conditional on the upload. The common shape of this failure is
           * exactly the run whose audio is safely away and whose answers are
           * not.
           */
          await stashRows(
            rows
              .map((row, i) => ({ row, ok: stored[i] }))
              .filter((r) => !r.ok)
              .map(({ row }) => ({
                runId: runId.current,
                assignmentId,
                studentId,
                itemOrder: row.item_order,
                row,
              })),
          );
        }
      }

      /*
       * Close the assignment.
       *
       * Deliberately after the rows and deliberately not conditional on them:
       * `status` is the Classroom's answer to "did she do the work", and she
       * did, whether or not our write of the evidence succeeded. Left out, an
       * RRC section stayed `in_progress` for ever -- every run ever done still
       * reads as unfinished -- because this branch never called the completion
       * the free-text path calls.
       *
       * Logged rather than thrown, like every other failure in this effect. She
       * has finished twenty unbroken minutes and a write we can retry is not a
       * thing to hand her at the end of them.
       */
      try {
        await completeSectionRun(assignmentId);
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("[rrc] could not close the assignment", err);
      }

      /*
       * Ask for the grade, and genuinely do not wait on it.
       *
       * This used to be awaited, which meant the ask's whole round trip stood
       * between her and the next screen -- and that round trip is the grading
       * job. On the trial run it was a 504 after two and a half minutes, spent
       * staring at "Section finished" with a Report Card screen already built
       * and polling for exactly this result on the other side of it.
       *
       * `void` is the fix and the intent: the Report Card polls, and the
       * sweeper re-drives anything this call missed, so the ask being slow or
       * lost costs nothing that is not already recovered.
       */
      if (missingRows === 0) {
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
        if (!cancelled) onComplete?.();
      }
    })();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [assignmentId, audioPath, buildRow, done, onComplete, persistRow, seconds, stopRecording, studentId, transcript]);

  const stimulus = run.stimuli[cursor.item];

  if (done) {
    /*
     * The handover, rather than a cut.
     *
     * The Report Card replaces this screen the moment the save finishes, and
     * the two surfaces could not be further apart: a cold indigo field against
     * warm paper. `is-finishing` warms the field and lifts the chrome over
     * nine hundred milliseconds so the card arrives on ground that is already
     * the right colour. It is decoration and it is deliberately on the one
     * frame she is most likely to remember.
     */
    return (
      <div className="ac-section is-finishing">
        <main className="ac-section-field">
          <div className="ac-section-paper">
            <div className="ac-section-body">
              <p className="ac-section-finished">
                Section finished.
                <span>
                  {unsavedCount > 0 && uploadFailed
                    ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us. Your recording did not reach us either, so both are still on this computer. Open this section again from the same browser and they will be sent, and then marked.`
                    : unsavedCount > 0 && recordingStored
                      ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us, but the recording did. Open this section again from the same browser and they will be sent, and then marked.`
                      : unsavedCount > 0
                        ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us, and there was no recording of this run to mark instead. Open this section again from the same browser and the answers will be sent.`
                        : uploadFailed
                    ? `${run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your recording did not reach us, so it is still on this computer. Open this section again from the same browser and it will be sent, and then marked.`
                    : `${run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your Report Card comes next.`}
                </span>
              </p>
              {unsavedCount > 0 && onExit && (
                <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
                  Back to the Activity Centre
                </button>
              )}
            </div>
          </div>
        </main>
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase={cursor.phase}
      stimulusNumber={cursor.item + 1}
      totalStimuli={run.stimuli.length}
      /*
       * A refused microphone is the loud fault and wins the line. A live one
       * that heard nothing through a whole Recall is the quiet one, and it is
       * the one that otherwise ends in a perfect-looking run and a card of
       * zeros.
       */
      error={
        // While the beat is held on a refusal, the panel is already saying it
        // in full. Repeating it in the footer is not emphasis, it is noise.
        countdown !== null && micRefused
          ? null
          : // A break is not a fault, and the dead-input line was measured
            // against a window she was not in the room for.
            paused
            ? null
            : (micError ??
            (deadInput
              ? "Nothing is reaching the microphone. Check it is not muted, then keep going."
              : null))
      }
      onExit={onExit ? askToExit : undefined}
      elapsedSeconds={countdown === null ? elapsed : 0}
      /* "Recording" must not be on the rail while nothing is being written to
         the file. The paused mark takes its place. */
      recording={recording && !micRefused && !paused}
      paused={paused}
      /* No pausing before the section has started -- there is nothing to pause
         and nothing to lose -- and none once it is over. */
      onPause={countdown === null ? pauseRun : undefined}
      onResume={resumeRun}
      pauseError={pauseError}
      getStream={getStream}
      onDeadInput={setDeadInput}
      micRefused={micRefused}
      onRetryMic={startRecording}
      confirmingExit={confirmingExit}
      onConfirmExit={onExit}
      onCancelExit={() => setConfirmingExit(false)}
      countdown={countdown}
      /* The same act as the Space handler, for a device with no Space key. The
         stage decides whether to draw it; the runner only supplies the act. */
      onAdvance={press}
      /* First section only, first three stimuli only, and never in the loop. */
      coach={coachOn ? coachNote(cursor.item, cursor.phase) : null}
      onDismissCoach={() => setCoachOn(false)}
    />
  );
}

import type { SectionPhase, SectionStimulus } from "./section";
import type { CoachNote } from "./coach";
import { VoiceLevel } from "./VoiceLevel";
import { useNoKeyboard } from "./noKeyboard";

/**
 * Is this page running inside another page's frame?
 *
 * A preview frame is never handed the microphone: the embed carries no
 * microphone permission, so `getUserMedia` is refused before the student is
 * ever asked. That looks identical to a student saying no, and "allow the
 * microphone for this site" is advice she cannot act on. So when the drill is
 * framed, the refusal names the real cause and offers the one thing that
 * works: the same address in a tab of its own.
 *
 * A cross-origin frame throws on the read, and a throw means framed.
 */
function isEmbedded(): boolean {
  try {
    return window.self !== window.top;
  } catch {
    return true;
  }
}


/**
 * One screen of a Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run.
 *
 * Presentational, like `RecallStage`, and separate from it because the rule it
 * keeps is different. v1 hides one chunk during its recall; a section has two
 * looks and nothing between them: the whole stimulus while she works it, and
 * no stimulus at all during The Recall
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
 *
 * **It wears the real section's clothes.** The drill she is training for is sat
 * on LawHub, and `OfficialDrillFrame` already mirrors that surface: indigo
 * field, white header rail, one centred white paper card, the stimulus in
 * Fraunces at 19px. This screen replicates that contract exactly and removes
 * the answering half -- no question column, no choices, no verdict, no
 * notepad -- so the drill feels like the thing it is preparing her for rather
 * than like a separate app. The frame itself is not imported because it carries
 * auth, the notepad and the tutor toggle, none of which may exist inside this
 * loop, and because this component has to stay presentational.
 *
 * The hard requirements, all of them negative:
 *
 * - **No place marker.** The app does not track which chunk she is on, so no
 *   chunk is styled as current, done, or upcoming. She reads, recalls and
 *   colours in whatever units she likes.
 * - **The text truly vanishes for The Recall.** Not blurred, not transparent,
 *   not aria-hidden: removed. A stimulus left in the DOM would be read back to
 *   a student using a screen reader, which defeats the drill for her exactly.
 * - **One key.** No mouse in the loop. The only control here is Space, wired
 *   by the runner; the exit is outside the loop.
 * - **No answer choices, and nothing judged.** Nothing is scored, shown or
 *   corrected until the section ends, because a live verdict trains her to vet
 *   before speaking, which is the habit colouring exists to break.
 *
 * The clock counts up rather than down, and the microphone has one quiet
 * indicator beside the instruction. Both are late additions and both are
 * concessions to the same finding: a student cannot pace a 25-minute section
 * she cannot see the time on, and she cannot tell a dead microphone from her
 * own silence. Neither may ever read as a score.
 */

export interface SectionStageProps {
  stimulus: SectionStimulus;
  phase: SectionPhase;
  /** 1-based, for the quiet position line. */

  totalStimuli: number;

   * identical from her side, so saying nothing while she talks into nothing is
   * the worst version of this.

  /** Leaving mid-run. A full-screen activity with no way out is a trap. */

  /** Seconds since the section started. Omit to draw no clock at all. */
  elapsedSeconds?: number;
  /**
   * True while the microphone is open and the file is being written. Drawn as

   * which is what a run against a handheld recorder looks like.

  pauseError?: string | null;

/*

const INSTRUCTION: Record<SectionPhase, string> = {

  recalling: "Say the whole argument back from memory.",
};

const KEY_DOES: Record<SectionPhase, string> = {
  working: "recall full stimulus",

/** mm:ss, and hh:mm:ss only if a section ever ran past an hour. */
function clock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  const s = Math.max(0, Math.floor(totalSeconds));
  const mm = Math.floor(s / 60);
  const ss = String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0");
  return `${mm}:${ss}`;
}

export function SectionStage({

                  )}
                  <div className="ac-section-ready-actions">
                    <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-retry" onClick={onRetryMic}>
                      Ask again
                    </button>
                    {isEmbedded() && (
                      <button
                        type="button"
                        className="ac-section-ready-retry"
                        onClick={() =>
                          window.open(window.location.href, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")
                        }
                      >
                        Open in its own tab
                      </button>
                    )}
                    {onExit && (
                      <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-leave" onClick={onExit}>
                        Leave this section
                      </button>
                    )}
                  </div>
                </>

              ) : (
                <>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-title">Recording now</p>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                    One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus: once when
                    you have read it and said it back, once when you have given your Read.
                  </p>
                  <span className="ac-section-ready-count">
                    {countdown > 0 ? `Starting in ${countdown}` : "Starting"}
                  </span>
                </>
              )}
            </div>
          ) : (
            <div className="ac-section-body">
              {/*
                While she works, every chunk is rendered in order with nothing
                marking one out from another: the cuts guide her eye and hold the
                grader's Details, and that is all they do. During The Recall the
                stimulus is not rendered at all -- the paper stays, the text goes,
                and it does not come back until the Report Card.
              */}
              {phase === "working" ? (
                <div className="ac-section-stimulus">
                  {stimulus.chunks.map((chunk) => (
                    <span key={chunk.n} className="ac-section-chunk">
                      {chunk.text}{" "}
                    </span>
                  ))}
                </div>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          )}

          {/* The card's own footer, where the real section keeps its controls.
              What she is doing sits left, the one key that does it sits right. */}
          <div className="ac-section-foot">
            <p className="ac-section-instruction" role="status">
              {paused
                ? "Paused. Nothing is being recorded."
                : countdown !== null
                  ? micRefused
                    ? "The section has not started."
                    : "Say something to check your microphone."
                  : INSTRUCTION[phase]}
              {/* No presence bar while she is away. The microphone is still
                  open -- pausing a recorder does not close the input -- so a
                  bar left running would move to an empty room and say the one
                  thing it exists to say when it moves: that this is being
                  heard. */}
              {getStream && !paused ? (
                <VoiceLevel getStream={getStream} phase={phase} onDeadInput={onDeadInput} />
              ) : null}
            </p>
            {/* The fault and the instruction, not the fault INSTEAD of it.
                These were a ternary, so a microphone warning took away the only
                thing on the screen telling her which key advances the drill --
                on precisely the run where something has already gone wrong. The
                warning leads, because it is the new information. */}
            <div className="ac-section-foot-right">
              {/* A pause she asked for and did not get.
                  It belongs in the footer rather than on the paused card
                  precisely BECAUSE she is not paused: the card she would have
                  read it on is the one that never appeared, and a refusal with
                  nowhere to be said is a button that does nothing. */}
              {!paused && pauseError ? (
                <p className="ac-section-error" role="status">
                  {pauseError}
                </p>
              ) : null}
              {error ? (
                <p className="ac-section-error" role="status">
                  {error}
                </p>
              ) : null}
              <p className="ac-section-hint">
                <kbd>space</kbd>{" "}
                {paused
                  ? "back to it"
                  : countdown !== null
                    ? micRefused
                      ? "once the microphone is on"
                      : "in a moment"
                    : KEY_DOES[phase]}
              </p>
              {/* The same act as Space, for a device that has no Space.
                  Rendered only where there is no keyboard, so the desktop loop
                  is untouched and no mouse enters it. Disabled through the beat
                  for the same reason the hint reads "in a moment": nothing may
                  advance before the section has started. */}
              {noKeyboard && onAdvance ? (
                <button
                  type="button"
                  className="ac-section-advance"
                  onClick={onAdvance}
                  disabled={countdown !== null || paused}
                >
                  {countdown !== null
                    ? micRefused
                      ? "Waiting for the microphone"
                      : "Starting"
                    : KEY_DOES[phase] === "recall full stimulus"
                      ? "Recall full stimulus"
                      : "Next stimulus"}
                </button>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* The first-run walkthrough. Beside the paper, never on it, and it
            holds nothing up: no key of its own, nothing to acknowledge, gone
            of its own accord after the third stimulus. */}
        {coach && countdown === null && !paused ? (
          <aside className="ac-section-coach" aria-label="Walkthrough">
            <p className="ac-section-coach-step">
              Walkthrough · {coach.step} of {coach.of}
            </p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-title">{coach.title}</p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-body">{coach.body}</p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-next">
              <span>Next</span> {coach.next}
            </p>
            {onDismissCoach ? (
              <button type="button" className="ac-section-coach-hide" onClick={onDismissCoach}>
                Hide this
              </button>
            ) : null}
          </aside>
        ) : null}
      </main>
    </div>
  );
}
=====VOICELEVEL=====
import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

/**
 * How long a phase has to run before its silence means anything.
 *
 * A Recall she ends in three seconds carries no evidence either way, and
 * accusing the hardware on that is how a warning stops being believed. A window
 * shorter than this counts neither for the fault nor against it.
 */
const MIN_WINDOW_MS = 8000;

/**
 * Consecutive silent windows before the fault is raised.
 *
 * Both halves of a stimulus are spoken -- "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in
 * as you go", then "Say the whole argument back from memory" -- so a dead input
 * is silent through every window there is. A skip is silent through exactly
 * one: she had nothing for that Recall, and then she reads the next stimulus
 * aloud like all the others.
 *
 * One window was therefore the wrong unit, and it accused the hardware on the
 * most ordinary thing a student does. Two consecutive is the shortest run a skip
 * cannot produce, and it costs a genuinely dead microphone one further stimulus
 * before it is named.
 */
const SILENT_WINDOWS = 2;

/** Full deflection. Chosen so ordinary speech uses most of the bar. */
const FULL_SCALE = 0.15;

export interface VoiceLevelProps {
  /** The live input, or null when the microphone never opened. */
  getStream: () => MediaStream | null;
  phase: SectionPhase;
  /**
   * Whether nothing is reaching the microphone, called only when the answer
   * changes.
   *
   * It used to be a one-way announcement, fired once and never taken back, and
   * the runner had no setter that could clear it. One deliberate skip therefore
   * told a student her hardware was broken, and went on telling her for the
   * remaining twenty-five stimuli and four minutes of clearly heard speech.
   */
  onDeadInput?: (dead: boolean) => void;
}

export function VoiceLevel({ getStream, phase, onDeadInput }: VoiceLevelProps) {
  const bar = useRef<HTMLSpanElement>(null);
  const level = useRef(0);
  /** The loudest thing heard since the current phase began. */
  const peak = useRef(0);
  const windowStartedAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
  /** Consecutive windows that ran long enough to count and carried nothing. */
  const silentWindows = useRef(0);
  /** What the runner has been told, so it is told only when that changes. */
  const dead = useRef(false);
  const onDead = useRef(onDeadInput);
  onDead.current = onDeadInput;

  /*
   * One analyser for the life of the run.
   *
   * Opened once and closed once, like the recorder itself: an AudioContext per
   * phase would be forty-four of them across a section, and Chrome caps how
   * many a page may hold at the same time.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    let raf = 0;
    let context: AudioContext | null = null;
    let stopped = false;

    const attach = () => {
      if (stopped) return;
      const stream = getStream();
      // The recorder opens the microphone asynchronously and this mounts with
      // it, so the stream is usually a moment away rather than absent. Ask
      // again next frame; a genuinely refused microphone simply never arrives
      // and the bar stays flat, which is the truth.
      if (!stream) {
        raf = requestAnimationFrame(attach);
        return;
      }
      const Ctor =
        window.AudioContext ??
        (window as unknown as { webkitAudioContext?: typeof AudioContext }).webkitAudioContext;
      if (!Ctor) return;
      context = new Ctor();
      const analyser = context.createAnalyser();
      // Small window, because this is an amplitude reading and not a spectrum:
      // it costs less per frame and follows her voice more closely.
      analyser.fftSize = 512;
      context.createMediaStreamSource(stream).connect(analyser);
      const samples = new Float32Array(analyser.fftSize);

      const read = () => {
        analyser.getFloatTimeDomainData(samples);
        let sum = 0;
        for (let i = 0; i < samples.length; i++) sum += samples[i] * samples[i];
        const rms = Math.sqrt(sum / samples.length);
        if (rms > peak.current) peak.current = rms;
        /*
         * Recovery is immediate, and deliberately not deferred to the next
         * boundary like the fault is. The fault needs a whole window to be sure;
         * being heard again needs one frame, and a warning that outlives the
         * condition it describes is the thing being fixed. Guarded on `dead` so
         * this is a transition rather than a call at 60Hz.
         */
        if (rms >= NOISE_FLOOR && dead.current) {
          dead.current = false;
          silentWindows.current = 0;
          onDead.current?.(false);
        }
        // Rises fast and falls slowly, so the bar tracks speech rather than
        // flickering on every syllable boundary.
        level.current = rms > level.current ? rms : level.current * 0.88 + rms * 0.12;
        if (bar.current) {
          bar.current.style.transform = `scaleX(${Math.min(1, level.current / FULL_SCALE)})`;
        }
        raf = requestAnimationFrame(read);
      };
      read();
    };

    attach();
    return () => {
      stopped = true;
      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
      void context?.close();
    };
  }, [getStream]);

  /*
   * The fault, decided at the boundary rather than during a phase.
   *
   * Judging it while she is mid-sentence would fire on a long pause. Judging it
   * as a phase closes uses the whole window, which is the only reading that
   * separates "she went quiet" from "nothing is arriving".
   *
   * Every boundary is judged now, not only the end of a Recall. The working
   * phase is spoken too, so its silence is evidence of exactly the same kind,
   * and using both is what lets a run of them mean something a single one
   * cannot.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    const started = windowStartedAt.current;
    const loudest = peak.current;
    peak.current = 0;
    windowStartedAt.current = Date.now();

    // The first run of this effect opens the first window; nothing has closed.
    if (started === null) return;
    // Too short to carry evidence either way, so it neither raises the fault
    // nor clears the count towards it.
    if (Date.now() - started < MIN_WINDOW_MS) return;

    if (loudest >= NOISE_FLOOR) {
      silentWindows.current = 0;
      return;
    }
    silentWindows.current += 1;
    if (silentWindows.current >= SILENT_WINDOWS && !dead.current) {
      dead.current = true;
      onDead.current?.(true);
    }
  }, [phase]);

  return (
    <span className="ac-section-level" aria-hidden>
      <span ref={bar} className="ac-section-level-fill" />
    </span>
  );
}
=====COACH=====
/**
 * The first-run walkthrough for a Read, Recall, Colour section.
 *
 * A student's first section is the only one she does not already know the
 * shape of, and the briefing page she read a minute ago is gone by the time
 * the first stimulus is on screen. So for the first three stimuli only, a
 * quiet card sits beside the paper and says what to do now and what happens
 * next. After that it goes, and it never comes back.
 *
 * Two hard rules, both about not getting in the way:
 *
 * - **It is never in the loop.** It takes no key, blocks nothing, judges
 *   nothing and gates nothing. Space still does the same thing it always did,
 *   at the same moment. The card is text next to the drill, not a step in it.
 * - **It ends by itself.** Three stimuli, then it is gone whether or not she
 *   dismissed it, and the "seen" flag is written the first time a section
 *   starts, so a second section is bare even if the first was abandoned.
 */

/** How many stimuli the walkthrough covers before it retires itself. */
export const COACHED_STIMULI = 3;

const KEY = "rrc.section.walkthrough.seen";

function storageKey(studentId: string): string {
  return `${KEY}.${studentId}`;
}

/** Has this student already been walked through a section? */
export function hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId: string): boolean {
  try {
    return window.localStorage.getItem(storageKey(studentId)) === "1";
  } catch {
    // A browser refusing storage should not force the walkthrough on every
    // run for ever; treating it as seen is the less annoying failure.
    return true;
  }
}

/** Remember that it has been shown. Called once, when the first run starts. */
export function markWalkthroughSeen(studentId: string): void {
  try {
    window.localStorage.setItem(storageKey(studentId), "1");
  } catch {
    /* storage refused; nothing to do */
  }
}

export interface CoachNote {
  /** 1-based, of `COACHED_STIMULI`. */
  step: number;
  of: number;
  title: string;
  body: string;
  /** What happens the moment she presses Space. */
  next: string;
}

const WORKING: { title: string; body: string; next: string }[] = [
  {
    title: "Read it, out loud in pieces",
    body: "Take the stimulus a piece at a time. After each piece, say it back in your own words, then say what that piece is doing in the argument — the colour. Evidence, conclusion, someone else's view, a concession. Keep talking; nothing here is being marked as you go.",
    next: "Space hides the text and asks you to recall the whole thing.",
  },
  {
    title: "Same again, and do not vet yourself",
    body: "Second one. Say the wrong word rather than pausing to find the right one — the drill is training you to keep reading and talking at once, so a rough colour said out loud beats a perfect one thought silently.",
    next: "Space hides the text for the recall.",
  },
  {
    title: "Last one with help",
    body: "You have the rhythm now: read a piece, say it back, colour it. From the next stimulus on, this card is gone and it is just you, the paper and the clock.",
    next: "Space hides the text for the recall.",
  },
];

const RECALLING: { title: string; body: string; next: string }[] = [
  {
    title: "Now say it back from memory",
    body: "The text is gone on purpose. Recap the whole argument out loud — what was claimed, what it rested on, where it turned. It does not need to be word perfect; it needs to be the argument.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
  {
    title: "Recall it whole",
    body: "Again from memory, whole argument. If you lose a piece, say so out loud and carry on — a gap you name is worth more than a silence.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
  {
    title: "Recall it, then you are on your own",
    body: "Say the argument back one more time. After this the card retires and the section runs straight through to the end, one take, no stopping.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
];

/**
 * The note for where she is, or null once the walkthrough is over.
 *
 * `item` is 0-based, matching the cursor.
 */
export function coachNote(item: number, phase: "working" | "recalling"): CoachNote | null {
  if (item < 0 || item >= COACHED_STIMULI) return null;
  const source = phase === "working" ? WORKING : RECALLING;
  const note = source[item];
  if (!note) return null;
  return { step: item + 1, of: COACHED_STIMULI, ...note };
}
=====NOKEYBOARD=====
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

/**
 * Whether this device probably has no keyboard, so the drill's one key is out
 * of reach.
 *
 * A section advances on Space and on nothing else
 * (`SectionRunner.tsx`, "One key, and only Space"). That is deliberate: no
 * mouse in the loop, so her hands never leave the argument. It also means a
 * phone can open the drill, read stimulus 1, and never get to stimulus 2 --
 * the briefing, the door and the library all render fine at 390x844, so
 * nothing warns her before the recording is already running.
 *
 * `(pointer: coarse)` is the test rather than a width, because the question is
 * "is there a keyboard", not "is the screen small". A narrow desktop window has
 * a keyboard and must keep the pure one-key loop; a large tablet has no
 * keyboard and needs the control. Width answers neither.
 *
 * This is a hint, never a gate. Joshua's call, 2026-09-04: a phone is warned
 * and let through, not refused. So nothing here disables anything -- it adds a
 * sentence at the door and an on-screen way to advance inside the run, and
 * Space stays authoritative everywhere.
 */
export const NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY = "(pointer: coarse)";

/** Read once, outside React, so a non-browser render has a defined answer. */
export function hasNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return false;
  return window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY).matches;
}

/**
 * The same question as a hook, kept live.
 *
 * A tablet with a keyboard case attached or removed mid-run changes the answer,
 * and the control appearing or going away is the correct response to that. The
 * initial state is computed rather than defaulted to `false`, so the first
 * paint on a phone already carries the control instead of flashing a screen
 * that cannot be advanced.
 */
export function useNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  const [noKeyboard, setNoKeyboard] = useState<boolean>(hasNoKeyboard);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return;
    const mql = window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY);
    const onChange = () => setNoKeyboard(mql.matches);
    onChange();
    // `addEventListener` is not universal on MediaQueryList in older Safari,
    // which is exactly the population this query is about, so the older API is
    // the fallback rather than the assumption.
    if (typeof mql.addEventListener === "function") {
      mql.addEventListener("change", onChange);
      return () => mql.removeEventListener("change", onChange);
    }
    mql.addListener?.(onChange);
    return () => mql.removeListener?.(onChange);
  }, []);

  return noKeyboard;
}

 *
 * It is registered only when import.meta.env.DEV, so it never exists in a

const WORLDS = [
  { key: "list", label: "Activity Centre list" },
  { key: "intro", label: "Activity intro" },
  { key: "door", label: "RRC door (pick a section)" },
  { key: "section", label: "RRC section (whole section)" },
  { key: "report", label: "RRC Report Card" },
  { key: "runs", label: "RRC run history" },
  { key: "shell", label: "Runner shell" },
  { key: "switchyard", label: "The Switchyard" },
  { key: "specimen-lab", label: "The Specimen Lab" },
  { key: "press", label: "The Press" },
  { key: "atelier", label: "The Argument Atelier" },
  { key: "chamber", label: "The Debate Chamber" },
  { key: "observatory", label: "The Signal Observatory" },
  { key: "loom", label: "The Loom" },
  { key: "polarity", label: "The Polarity Chamber" },
  { key: "completion", label: "Completion + indicator ledger" },
] as const;
type World = (typeof WORLDS)[number]["key"];

function Tabs<T extends string>({
  options,
  value,
  onChange,
  label,
}: {
  options: readonly T[];
  value: T;
  onChange: (v: T) => void;
  label: string;
}) {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-1" role="group" aria-label={label}>
      {options.map((o) => (
        <button
          key={o}
          onClick={() => onChange(o)}
          data-testid={`${label.toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, "-")}-${o}`}
          className={`rounded-md px-3 py-1.5 text-sm capitalize transition-colors ${
            value === o
              ? "bg-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald))] text-white"
              : "border border-border bg-card text-foreground hover:bg-muted"
          }`}
        >
          {o.replace(/-/g, " ")}
        </button>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

export default function ActivityLab() {
  const [world, setWorld] = useState<World>("switchyard");

  return (
    <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen">
      <header className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 overflow-hidden border-b border-border px-6 py-4">
        <div className="mr-auto">
          <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
            Activity Lab · dev only
          </p>
          <h1 className="ac-display text-xl text-foreground">
            {WORLDS.find((w) => w.key === world)!.label}
          </h1>
        </div>
        <Tabs
          options={WORLDS.map((w) => w.key)}
          value={world}
          onChange={(v) => setWorld(v)}
          label="World"
        />
      </header>

      {world === "list" ? <ListPanel /> : null}
      {world === "intro" ? <IntroPanel /> : null}
      {world === "door" ? <DoorPanel /> : null}
      {world === "section" ? <SectionPanel /> : null}
      {world === "report" ? <ReportPanel /> : null}
      {world === "runs" ? <RunsPanel /> : null}
      {world === "shell" ? <ShellPanel /> : null}
      {world === "switchyard" ? <SwitchyardPanel /> : null}
      {world === "specimen-lab" ? <SpecimenLabPanel /> : null}
      {world === "press" ? <PressPanel /> : null}
      {world === "atelier" ? <AtelierPanel /> : null}
      {world === "chamber" ? <ChamberPanel /> : null}
      {world === "observatory" ? <ObservatoryPanel /> : null}
      {world === "loom" ? <LoomPanel /> : null}
      {world === "polarity" ? <PolarityPanel /> : null}
      {world === "completion" ? <CompletionPanel /> : null}
    </div>
  );
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
 * The Activity Centre list
 * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */

/**
 * The eight Activities a student can actually be assigned.
 *
 * SEED_ACTIVITIES is only seven: Negation Station predates the content pipeline
 * and is deliberately never re-seeded, so it has no file in content/. That is
 * correct for the pipeline and wrong for the lab, which was left unable to draw
 * the one Activity every student meets first -- its card art and its running
 * shell had never been looked at once. Its items come from the world's own
 * verbatim extract of the seed migration; the rest of the row is that same
=====SECTION/REPORT PANELS=====
              onRecord={async () => {}}
              onNext={() => {}}
            />
          </main>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
 * The Activity intro (the screen a student lands on before drilling)
 * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */

/**
 * The intro is the other half of the runner that auth makes impossible to look
 * at, and the exit control lives on it. Rendered here against real seeded
 * content so its placement and affordance can be checked without a login.
 */
function IntroPanel() {
  // Every Activity, because the intro's failure modes are content-shaped: the
  // worked example that is a whole paragraph is the one that breaks the orbs.
  //
  // This used to filter to `instructions.worked_example`, which quietly excluded
  // the content shape that broke hardest. An Activity with a task and NO worked
  // example renders no orbs at all, so the hero has nothing to give it height
  // and the absolutely-positioned brief lands on top of the title. Read, Recall,
  // Colour and Dumb it Down are both that shape, and both shipped broken because
  // the one harness meant to catch content-shaped failures could not draw them.
  const shapes = SEED_ACTIVITIES;
  const [slug, setSlug] = useState(shapes[0].slug);
  const a = shapes.find((x) => x.slug === slug)!;
  /*
   * A section's own JSON carries `instructions: {}` on purpose -- they are
   * authored once and filled in at seed time -- which left the one intro whose
   * copy is most easily wrong as the one intro this harness could not draw. It
   * is filled from the same file the seed builder reads, so what the lab shows
   * is what the database gets.
   */
  const instructions =
    a.mode === "section"
      ? (sectionInstructions as unknown as typeof a.instructions)
      : a.instructions;
  return (
    <div>
      <div className="border-b border-border px-6 py-3">
        <Tabs
          options={shapes.map((x) => x.slug)}
          value={slug}
          onChange={setSlug}
          label="Activity"
        />
      </div>
      {/* Mirrors the runner's intro wrapper, which carries the same attribute. */}
      <div className="act-home" data-world={worldForTitle(a.title)?.slug}>
        <ActivityIntro
          key={a.slug}
          title={a.title}
          subtitle={a.subtitle}
          instructions={instructions}
          itemCount={a.items.length}
          maxAttempts={MAX_ATTEMPTS}
          format={a.item_format}
          mode={a.mode}
          onStart={() => {}}
          onExit={() => {}}
        />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
 * The door
 * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */

/**
 * The section-library door, against real data.
 *
 * It reads her assignments and the section list from the live backend rather
 * than a fixture, because the door's whole content is that list -- a preview of
 * it with three invented rows would be a preview of nothing. The harness at
 * `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html?state=library` is the fixture version,
 * for looking at the layout without a session.
 */
function DoorPanel() {
  return <SectionLibraryPanel />;
}

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
 * The Switchyard
 * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */

const PHASES: YardPhase[] = ["arrival", "routed", "flipped"];
const YARD_VIEWS = ["yard", "entrance", "sorting", "gallery"] as const;
const FAULT_DEMOS = ["none", "reversal", "dropped-negation", "trimmed-condition"] as const;
type FaultDemo = (typeof FAULT_DEMOS)[number];

/**
 * Build a plausible student routing for each named partial so the real
 * `diagnose` is what decides the choreography, rather than the lab asserting a
 * fault the production path might never derive.
 */
function studentRoutingFor(demo: FaultDemo, credited: Routing): Routing | null {
  if (demo === "none") return null;
  if (demo === "reversal") {
    return { ifClause: credited.thenClause, thenClause: credited.ifClause };
  }
  if (demo === "dropped-negation") {
    const side = credited.ifClause.negated ? "ifClause" : "thenClause";
    return {
      ...credited,
      [side]: { ...credited[side], negated: !credited[side].negated },
    } as Routing;
  }
  const words = credited.thenClause.text.split(/\s+/);
  if (words.length < 5) return null;
  return {
    ...credited,
    thenClause: { ...credited.thenClause, text: words.slice(0, Math.ceil(words.length / 2)).join(" ") },
  };

function SectionPanel() {
  const [slug, setSlug] = useState("read-recall-colour-pt105-s2");
  const row = SEED_ACTIVITIES.find((a) => a.slug === slug);
  const run = useMemo(
    () => (row ? buildSectionRun(row.items, row.source_section, row.time_goal_minutes) : null),
    [row],
  );
  const [cursor, setCursor] = useState<SectionCursor>(() => startCursor());
  /*
   * The rail's four children, the ready beat and the confirmation, all drawn
   * here on purpose.
   *
   * The header declared three grid columns and had four children from the day
   * the clock shipped, so "Leave" wrapped to a second row and the rail grew
   * from 46px to 96px. It was live for days on the drill's only screen, because
   * the lab rendered the stage without a clock or a way out and nobody ever saw
   * the version a student sees.
   */
  const [moment, setMoment] = useState<"running" | "ready beat" | "paused" | "finishing">(
    "running",
  );
  const beat = moment === "ready beat";
  /*
   * The break, which is a whole screen a student can reach and nobody would
   * otherwise look at. The lab exists because the version she sees went days
   * without being seen; a moment left out of this list is a moment back in that
   * position.
   */
  const paused = moment === "paused";
  const [confirming, setConfirming] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => setCursor(startCursor()), [slug]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!run) return;
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.code !== "Space") return;
      e.preventDefault();
      setCursor((at) => advanceCursor(run, at) ?? at);
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [run]);

  if (!run) {
    return <p className="px-6 py-8 text-sm text-zinc-500">No section Activity in the seed content.</p>;
  }
  const stimulus = run.stimuli[Math.min(cursor.item, run.stimuli.length - 1)];

  return (
    <div>
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 border-b border-border px-6 py-3">
        <Tabs
          options={SEED_ACTIVITIES.filter((a) => a.mode === "section").map((a) => a.slug)}
          value={slug}
          onChange={(v) => setSlug(v)}
          label="Section"
        />
        <Tabs
          options={run.stimuli.map((_, i) => String(i + 1))}
          value={String(cursor.item + 1)}
          onChange={(v) => setCursor({ item: Number(v) - 1, phase: "working" })}
          label="Stimulus"
        />
        <Tabs
          options={["running", "ready beat", "paused", "finishing"] as const}
          value={moment}
          onChange={(v) => setMoment(v)}
          label="Moment"
        />
        <span className="text-xs text-zinc-500">
          {cursor.phase} · press space
        </span>
      </div>
      {moment === "finishing" ? (
        /*
         * The handover, which is a nine hundred millisecond CSS transition and
         * therefore the single hardest thing in this feature to look at: it
         * happens once, at the end of a twenty-five minute run, and then the
         * Report Card replaces it.
         */
        <div className="ac-section is-finishing">
          <main className="ac-section-field">
            <div className="ac-section-paper">
              <div className="ac-section-body">
                <p className="ac-section-finished">
                  Section finished.
                  <span>{run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your Report Card comes next.</span>
                </p>
              </div>
            </div>
          </main>
        </div>
      ) : (
      <SectionStage
        stimulus={stimulus}
        phase={cursor.phase}
        stimulusNumber={cursor.item + 1}
        totalStimuli={run.stimuli.length}
        elapsedSeconds={beat ? 0 : 754}
        countdown={beat ? 3 : null}
        paused={paused}
        onPause={beat ? undefined : () => setMoment("paused")}
        onResume={() => setMoment("running")}
        // No microphone in the lab, so the bar mounts and stays flat. That is
        // exactly what a student with a dead input sees, which is the reason
        // the indicator exists and the one state worth being able to look at.
        getStream={() => null}
        onExit={() => setConfirming(true)}
        confirmingExit={confirming}
        onConfirmExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
        onCancelExit={() => setConfirming(false)}
      />
      )}
    </div>
  );
}
=====REPORT PANEL=====
function ReportPanel() {
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState<"clean" | "faults" | "partial">("faults");

  const grade = useMemo(() => makeLabGrade(attempt), [attempt]);

  return (
    <div>
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 border-b border-border px-6 py-3">
        <Tabs
          options={["clean", "faults", "partial"] as const}
          value={attempt}
          onChange={(v) => setAttempt(v)}
          label="Run"
        />
        <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">
          clean: everything at the standard · faults: the trial run · partial: stimuli we could not
          mark
        </p>
      </div>
      <div className="px-4 py-10">
        <SectionReportCard
          grade={grade}
          /*
           * The lab has no database, so the model runs are named rather than
           * looked up. The player button appears and does nothing, which is the
           * point: a lab that faked a working player would be the one place a
           * broken one could hide.
           *
           * The walkthrough is different, and is real text rather than a stub.
           * It is the part a student actually reads, it is the part most likely
           * to look wrong at some width or some length, and a lab that showed
           * the buttons but not the document would be checking the half that
           * matters least.
           */
          loadModelRuns={async () =>
            Object.fromEntries(
              grade.stimuli.map((st) => [
                st.slug,
                {
                  audio: { bucket: "model-runs", path: `${st.slug}.mp3` },
                  walkthrough:
                    "> Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so " +
                    "that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they " +
                    "do well.\n\n" +
                    "So a management consultant is telling us how to run the company, and " +
                    "the idea is to move people around so they are doing the things they " +
                    "like and the things they are good at. Think about McKinsey walking " +
                    "into Amazon and saying, put the people who like driving on the " +
                    "delivery routes.\n\n" +
                    "> This, she says, will \"increase productivity by fully exploiting " +
                    "our available resources.\"\n\n" +
                    "So her claim is that productivity goes up because you are getting the " +
                    "absolute most out of everybody. Well, what if exploit is doing two " +
                    "jobs in this argument? Getting the most out of something, and taking " +
                    "advantage of someone, are not the same word twice.",
                },
              ]),
            )
          }
        />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
