import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { SectionLaunchFrame } from "./SectionLaunchFrame";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionLibraryLaunchProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  /** The list itself, which owns the sections and the starting. */
  children: ReactNode;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

/**
 * The door to Read, Recall, Colour: the method, and the sections it is run on.
 *
 * One drill, and every section reachable from the page that explains it. Before
 * this there were three screens between her and a run — an editorial intro
 * written for the typed Activities, a bare list, and then the section's own
 * page — and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
 *
 * It carries no stat strip, and that is a refusal rather than an omission.
 * Trend and Target are per section: sections differ in length and in the clock
 * they are run against, so "−6:52 across 3 runs" spanning two different
 * sections would be a number about nothing. Each section row carries its own
 * attempts instead, and the four numbers appear on the section's own page,
 * where they mean something.
 */
export function SectionLibraryLaunch({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  children,
  onExit,
}: SectionLibraryLaunchProps) {
  return (
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={onExit}
      showBreadcrumbs={false}
      sectionsFirst
    >
      <div className="sl-runs">
        <div>
          <span className="sl-label">Your sections</span>
          <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>{children}</div>
        </div>

        <aside className="sl-aside">
          <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
          <p>
            Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own
            words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece,
            recall the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section
            is recorded in <strong>one take</strong>, and you cannot pause the
            recording.
          </p>
          <p>
            Every completed attempt stays here. If you run a section again, it
            is saved as a new attempt. Your earlier attempts are never replaced.
          </p>
        </aside>
      </div>
    </SectionLaunchFrame>
  );
}
=====SECTIONLAUNCH=====
import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import { ArrowRight } from "lucide-react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import {
  readMyRecentSectionGrades,
  type RecentSectionRun,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { SectionLaunchFrame } from "./SectionLaunchFrame";
import { clock, signedClock, statsFor, ticksFor } from "../recall/runStats";
import "./section-launch.css";
import { useNoKeyboard } from "../recall/noKeyboard";

/** How many past runs the history block shows. */
const RUNS_SHOWN = 3;

export interface SectionLaunchProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  /** Stimuli in this section, from the assignment snapshot. */
  itemCount?: number;
  /** The clock this section is run against. */
  timeGoalMinutes?: number | null;
  /** `PT105.S2`. Scopes the history read to this section. */
  sourceSection?: string | null;
  /**
   * Whose runs to read. Passed to the QUERY, not used as a display filter: the
   * admin SELECT policy on `activity_section_grades` returns every student's
   * rows, and no flag on this component can undo that. Only the `.eq` can.
   */
  studentId?: string | null;
  /**
   * Whether to show run history at all. False for a teacher reading a student's
   * page and for the authoring preview, where "her last three runs" is either
   * somebody else's record or nobody's.
   */
  showHistory?: boolean;
  onStart: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

type HistoryState =
  | { kind: "idle" }
  | { kind: "loading" }
  | { kind: "ready"; runs: RecentSectionRun[] }
  | { kind: "failed" };

/**
 * The door to a Read, Recall, Colour section.
 *
 * What it has to do that the editorial intro could not: fit. The page it
 * replaces ran 2778px, put its one button below the fold and grew a sticky dock
 * to compensate. This is one card, Start in the top right, and the long briefing
 * folded into a drawer that opens a beat at a time.
 *
 * Everything numeric on it is derived from runs she has already done -- see
 * `recall/runStats.ts`, which holds the arithmetic and the refusals. Nothing
 * here is scored, predicted, or filled in when the evidence is missing.
 */
export function SectionLaunch({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  itemCount,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  sourceSection,
  studentId,
  showHistory = false,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionLaunchProps) {
  const [history, setHistory] = useState<HistoryState>({ kind: "idle" });
  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();

  const canRead = showHistory && Boolean(sourceSection) && Boolean(studentId);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!canRead) {
      setHistory({ kind: "idle" });
      return;
    }
    // A resolution that arrives after this effect has been superseded, or after
    // the component is gone, must not land. Without the guard a slow first read
    // can overwrite a fast second one and show the wrong section's runs.
    let live = true;
    setHistory({ kind: "loading" });
    readMyRecentSectionGrades(RUNS_SHOWN, {
      sourceSection: sourceSection ?? undefined,
      studentId: studentId ?? undefined,
    })
      .then((result) => {
        if (!live) return;
        setHistory(result.failed ? { kind: "failed" } : { kind: "ready", runs: result.runs });
      })
      .catch(() => {
        if (live) setHistory({ kind: "failed" });
      });
    return () => {
      live = false;
    };
  }, [canRead, sourceSection, studentId]);

  // Derived inside the memo rather than above it. A `[]` literal computed on
  // every render is a new array every render, so hoisting it would give the
  // memo a dependency that never compares equal and recompute the stats each
  // pass -- a memo that memoises nothing.
  const runs = useMemo(
    () => (history.kind === "ready" ? history.runs : []),
    [history],
  );
  const stats = useMemo(
    () => statsFor(runs, timeGoalMinutes ?? null),
    [runs, timeGoalMinutes],
  );

  return (
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={onExit}
      meta={
        <>
          {sourceSection ? `${sourceSection} · ` : ""}
          {typeof itemCount === "number" ? `${itemCount} stimuli` : ""}
          {timeGoalMinutes ? ` · est ${timeGoalMinutes} min` : ""}
        </>
      }
      primaryAction={
        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
          Start the section
          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
        </button>
      }
      closingAction={
        <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
          Start now
          <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
        </button>
      }
      /* Warned, not refused: Joshua's call, 2026-09-04. She is told what the
         run needs and left free to make the call herself, which is why nothing
         here disables Start. The run itself carries an on-screen way to advance
         so that going ahead is a real choice rather than a trap. */
      notice={
        noKeyboard ? (
          <p className="sl-notice" role="status">
            This one is built for a laptop. It records you for the whole section
            in one take, so a call or a locked screen ends the run, and the
            microphone on a phone hears you less well. You can carry on if you
            want to.
          </p>
        ) : null
      }
      strip={showHistory ? <StatStrip state={history} stats={stats} /> : null}
    >
      {showHistory && <RunHistory state={history} />}
    </SectionLaunchFrame>
  );
}

/**
 * The four numbers, or an honest account of why there are not four numbers.
 *
 * A failed read and a student who has never run this section produce the same
 * empty array and must not produce the same sentence: one of them is being told
 * her work is gone.
 */
function StatStrip({
  state,
  stats,
}: {
  state: HistoryState;
  stats: ReturnType<typeof statsFor>;
}) {
  if (state.kind === "loading") {
    return (
      <div className="sl-stats" aria-hidden="true">
        {[0, 1, 2, 3].map((i) => (
          <div className="sl-stat" key={i}>
            <span className="sl-label">&nbsp;</span>
            <span className="sl-sv">
              <span className="sl-skel" style={{ width: "60%" }} />
            </span>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>
    );
  }
  if (state.kind === "failed" || state.kind === "idle") return null;
  if (stats.totalRuns === 0) return null;

  return (
    <div className="sl-stats">
      <div className="sl-stat">
        <span className="sl-label">Last run</span>
        <span className="sl-sv" data-empty={stats.lastRunSeconds === null || undefined}>
          {stats.lastRunSeconds === null ? "no runs yet" : clock(stats.lastRunSeconds)}
        </span>
        <span className="sl-sd">{stats.totalRuns === 1 ? "first run" : "most recent"}</span>
      </div>
      <div className="sl-stat">
        <span className="sl-label">Trend</span>
        <span
          className="sl-sv"
          data-dir={(stats.trendSeconds ?? 0) < 0 ? "down" : undefined}
          data-empty={stats.trendSeconds === null || undefined}
        >
          {stats.trendSeconds === null ? "first run" : signedClock(stats.trendSeconds)}
        </span>
        <span className="sl-sd">
          {stats.trendSeconds === null ? "nothing to compare yet" : `across ${stats.trendRuns} runs`}
        </span>
      </div>
      <div className="sl-stat">
        <span className="sl-label">Recalled</span>
        <span className="sl-sv" data-empty={stats.recalled === null || undefined}>
          {stats.recalled === null ? "not recorded" : stats.recalled}
          {stats.recalledOf !== null && <small>/{stats.recalledOf}</small>}
        </span>
        <span className="sl-sd">
          {stats.recalled === null ? "not recorded" : "stimulus by stimulus"}
        </span>
      </div>
      <div className="sl-stat">
        <span className="sl-label">Target</span>
        <span className="sl-sv" data-empty={stats.targetMinutes === null || undefined}>
          {stats.targetMinutes === null ? "no clock set" : `${stats.targetMinutes}:00`}
        </span>
        <span className="sl-sd">
          {stats.targetMinutes === null
            ? "no clock set"
            : stats.targetRuns === 0
              ? "no run on this clock yet"
              : `met on ${stats.metTarget} of ${stats.targetRuns}`}
        </span>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

/** Her last few runs, and what each one covered. */
function RunHistory({ state }: { state: HistoryState }) {
  if (state.kind === "idle") return null;

  return (
    <div className="sl-runs">
      <div>
        <span className="sl-label">
          {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0
            ? state.runs.length === 1
              ? "Your first run"
              : `Your last ${state.runs.length} runs`
            : "Your recent runs"}
        </span>
        {state.kind === "loading" && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            <span className="sl-skel" style={{ display: "block", width: "70%" }} />
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "failed" && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            Your run history is not available right now. The section still starts
            normally.
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length === 0 && (
          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
            No runs yet. The first one is the baseline, not the test.
          </p>
        )}
        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0 && (
          <div className="sl-scroll">
            <table className="sl-rtable">
              <thead>
                <tr>
                  <th>Date</th>
                  <th>Time</th>
                  <th>Recalled</th>
                  <th className="sl-r">Stimulus by stimulus</th>
                </tr>
              </thead>
              <tbody>
                {state.runs.map((run) => {
                  const t = ticksFor(run);
                  // `created_at` rather than `graded_at`: a regrade rewrites the
                  // second one, and the date beside a stable sort has to be as
                  // stable as the sort.
                  const when = run.created_at ?? run.graded_at;
                  return (
                    <tr key={`${run.assignment_id}-${run.attempt_no}`}>
                      <td>
                        {new Date(when).toLocaleDateString(undefined, {
                          day: "numeric",
                          month: "short",
                        })}
                      </td>
                      <td>{clock(run.section.total_seconds)}</td>
                      <td>{t ? `${t.recorded} of ${t.slots}` : "not recorded"}</td>
                      <td className="sl-r">
                        {t ? (
                          <span
                            className="sl-ticks"
                            aria-label={`${t.recorded} of ${t.slots} stimuli recorded`}
                          >
                            {t.ticks.map((s, i) => (
                              <span key={i} className="sl-tick" data-state={s} />
                            ))}
                          </span>
                        ) : (
                          <span className="sl-note">not recorded</span>
                        )}
                      </td>
                    </tr>
                  );
                })}
              </tbody>
            </table>
          </div>
        )}
      </div>

      <aside className="sl-aside">
        <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
        <p>
          Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own
          words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece, recall
          the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section is
          recorded in <strong>one take</strong>, and you cannot pause the
          recording.
        </p>
        <p>
          <span className="sl-key">Space</span> is the only key, and you press it twice
          per stimulus. Nothing advances on its own.
        </p>
      </aside>
    </div>
  );
}
=====BRIEFING=====
import type React from "react";
import { ArrowRight } from "lucide-react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { renderInlineMarks } from "../prompt-format";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionBriefingPageProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  onStart: () => void;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

/**
 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
 *
 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order, every step numbered and open,
 * with one Start button at the top and the same one at the foot. The written
 * instructions sit under it, closed. Nothing here begins the run.
 */
export function SectionBriefingPage({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];

  const startButton = (label: string) => (
    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
      {label}
      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
    </button>
  );

  return (
    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
      <header className="sl-bp-head">
        <div>
          {/* No chip. The title already names the drill, and on the door the
              chip earns its place because the H1 there is the bare name. */}
          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
          {(instructions.tagline || subtitle) && (
            <p className="sl-tagline">
              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
            </p>
          )}
        </div>
        <div className="sl-cta">
          {startButton("Start the section")}
          {onExit && (
            <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={onExit}>
              Back
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </header>

      {/* One fact, the one that lowers her shoulders before she reads. That the
          section records in one take with no pause is said at the foot, beside
          the Start she presses after reading; saying it here as well made the
          notice a paragraph she skipped. (An earlier version of this comment
          claimed Step 4 carried it. It did not: the sentence lived only in
          `instructions.rules`, which this page never renders, so for a day the
          page did not say it anywhere.) */}
      {/* No role="status". A live region is for state that changes without a page
          load; this sentence is the same every time, and announcing it as an
          update is noise. */}
      <p className="sl-notice">
        Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.
      </p>

      {steps.length > 0 && (
        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
            {steps.map((step, i) => (
              <li key={i} style={{ "--i": i } as React.CSSProperties}>
                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ol>
        </section>
      )}

      {/* The four steps above ARE the instruction. This is the same method told
          again at length, and rendering both open left her reading the method
          twice with no way to tell which one she was meant to follow. Closed by
          default, one click from anyone who wants the long version. */}
      {briefing && (
        <details className="sl-bp-more">
          {/* The summary carries a real heading rather than only looking like one.
              It read as a section title and was absent from the heading outline,
              which is what left the page jumping H2 to H4. */}
          <summary>
            <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Read the long version</h2>
          </summary>
          <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
            <section key={i}>
              <h3>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h3>
              {section.body.map((para, j) => (
                <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-bp-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>
              ))}
              {section.steps && (
                <ol>
                  {section.steps.map((step, j) => (
                    <li key={j}>
                      <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bp-sl-${i}-${j}`)}</strong>{" "}
                      {renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bp-sb-${i}-${j}`)}
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ol>
              )}
              {section.demo && (
                <ol>
                  {section.demo.beats.map((b, j) => (
                    <li key={j}>
                      <strong>{b.kind}</strong> {renderInlineMarks(b.text, `sl-bp-d-${i}-${j}`)}
                    </li>
                  ))}
                </ol>
              )}
            </section>
          ))}
          </section>
        </details>
      )}

      {/* The two facts she needs at the moment she presses Start, said where
          she presses it: it does not pause, and what the time is for. The
          closer is the authored last beat before Start and belongs here too. */}
      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
          It runs in one take and does not pause. Twenty-five minutes for the whole section is
          the goal you are working toward, not a limit.
        </p>
        {briefing?.closer && (
          <p className="sl-bp-closer">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.closer, "sl-bp-closer")}</p>
        )}
        {/* Says what happens to her, not how the clock works. "Counting up"
            described the mechanism and left her wondering what it counted
            toward. */}
        <p>
          When you are ready, press Start. Recording begins the moment you do, and the timer
          starts with it. The first time, your browser will ask to use your microphone. Allow it.
        </p>
        <div className="sl-cta">{startButton("Start the section")}</div>
      </footer>
    </div>
  );
}

import { useCallback, useMemo, useRef, useState, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { motion, useReducedMotion, useScroll, useTransform } from "framer-motion";
import { ArrowDown, ArrowRight, Mic } from "lucide-react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { renderInlineMarks } from "../prompt-format";
import { MeadowScene } from "./MeadowScene";
import { RecallDemo } from "./RecallDemo";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionLaunchFrameProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  /** Whether this page needs the breadcrumb line above its own heading. */
  showBreadcrumbs?: boolean;
  /** The right half of the crumb line: what this page is a page of. */
  meta?: ReactNode;
  /**
   * The page's own primary action, if it has one.
   *
   * A section has one: Start. The library does not; its actions are the
   * section rows, so its hero button carries her down to them instead.
   */
  primaryAction?: ReactNode;
  /**
   * The same action again at the foot of the page, worded for where it sits.
   * A section passes a second Start here; the library needs nothing, since its
   * default carries her back up to the sections.
   */
  closingAction?: ReactNode;
  /** A caution under the hero, where there is one. It never disables anything. */
  notice?: ReactNode;
  /** Between the hero and the method. The four numbers, where there are four. */
  strip?: ReactNode;
  /** The slab below the method: her runs on a section, her sections in the library. */
  children?: ReactNode;
  /**
   * Put the slab directly under the hero instead of at the foot of the page.
   *
   * The library's slab IS the point of the page — the sections she can start.
   * Burying it under the editorial meant "Choose a section" was a long scroll
   * away, and a student who did not scroll never found a way in.
   */
  sectionsFirst?: boolean;
  onExit?: () => void;

}

const EASE = [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] as const;

/** One authored entrance, shared by every block below the hero. */
const reveal = {
  initial: { opacity: 0, y: 28 },
  whileInView: { opacity: 1, y: 0 },
  viewport: { once: true, margin: "-12% 0px" },
  transition: { duration: 0.8, ease: EASE },
} as const;

const STEP_KINDS = ["read", "tell", "colour", "recall"] as const;

/**
 * The little painted scene above each step.
 *
 * Four small dioramas drawn in the door's own vocabulary: a page, a voice, a
 * stroke of colour, a page emptying. They move while the step is in view and
 * stop under reduced motion; none of them carries anything the words do not.
 */
function StepScene({ kind }: { kind: (typeof STEP_KINDS)[number] }) {
  if (kind === "read") {
    return (
      <div className="rd-scene rd-sc-read" aria-hidden="true">
        <div className="rd-sc-page">
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "88%" }} />
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "72%" }} />
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "94%" }} />
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "58%" }} />
          <span className="rd-sc-sweep" />
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  if (kind === "tell") {
    return (
      <div className="rd-scene rd-sc-tell" aria-hidden="true">
        <div className="rd-sc-bubble">
          <Mic size={13} strokeWidth={2.4} />
          <span className="rd-wave" data-live="true">
            {Array.from({ length: 7 }, (_, i) => (
              <i key={i} style={{ animationDelay: `${(i * 0.09).toFixed(2)}s` }} />
            ))}
          </span>
          <span className="rd-sc-bubble-text">in your own words</span>
        </div>
        <div className="rd-sc-page rd-sc-page-turned">
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "80%" }} />
          <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "64%" }} />
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  if (kind === "colour") {
    return (
      <div className="rd-scene rd-sc-colour" aria-hidden="true">
        <span className="rd-sc-stroke" />
        <div className="rd-sc-chip">
          <span className="rd-sc-chip-head">Colour it</span>
          “Who counted that?”
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  return (
    <div className="rd-scene rd-sc-recall" aria-hidden="true">
      <div className="rd-sc-page rd-sc-page-fading">
        <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "88%" }} />
        <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "72%" }} />
        <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "94%" }} />
        <i className="rd-sc-line" style={{ width: "58%" }} />
      </div>
      <span className="rd-sc-key">space</span>
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * The door to Read, Recall, Colour, drawn once for both of its pages.
 *
 * There are two doors and one design. The library is where she picks tonight's
 * section; the section page is where she reads its clock and starts it. They
 * share the hero, the demonstration, the four steps and the briefing, and
 * differ only in the slab underneath.
 *
 * The page is built to be understood in one viewport: the method is performed
 * in the hero before it is explained, the four steps are the next thing she
 * meets, and the sections she can run sit directly beneath. The long briefing
 * is one tab away and never in the way.
 */
export function SectionLaunchFrame({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  showBreadcrumbs = true,
  meta,
  primaryAction,
  closingAction,
  notice,
  strip,
  children,
  sectionsFirst = false,
  onExit,

}: SectionLaunchFrameProps) {
  const reduced = useReducedMotion();

  /**
   * The four beats are `steps` inside ONE briefing section, not the sections.
   * Only "How the method works" carries the procedure; rendering `sections`
   * here would put the essay headings in the strip and lose the method.
   */
  const beatSection = useMemo(
    () => instructions.briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0),
    [instructions.briefing],
  );
  const beats = beatSection?.steps ?? [];

  const [beat, setBeat] = useState(0);
  const [pane, setPane] = useState<"beat" | "briefing">("beat");

  const heroRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const drawerRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const sectionsRef = useRef<HTMLElement>(null);

  /**
   * The hero's scroll, 0 while it is fully on screen and 1 once it has left.
   * The scene planes and the headline all ride this one number, so they move
   * as one picture rather than as separate effects.
   */
  const { scrollYProgress } = useScroll({ target: heroRef, offset: ["start start", "end start"] });
  const copyY = useTransform(scrollYProgress, [0, 1], [0, 160]);
  const copyOpacity = useTransform(scrollYProgress, [0, 0.5], [1, 0]);
  const copyFilter = useTransform(scrollYProgress, [0, 0.5], ["blur(0px)", "blur(14px)"]);
  const copyScale = useTransform(scrollYProgress, [0, 0.6], [1, 0.94]);

  const beatRefs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const onBeatKey = useCallback(
    (e: React.KeyboardEvent) => {
      const last = beats.length - 1;
      let next: number | null = null;
      if (e.key === "ArrowRight" || e.key === "ArrowDown") next = beat === last ? 0 : beat + 1;
      if (e.key === "ArrowLeft" || e.key === "ArrowUp") next = beat === 0 ? last : beat - 1;
      if (e.key === "Home") next = 0;
      if (e.key === "End") next = last;
      if (next === null) return;
      e.preventDefault();
      setBeat(next);
      setPane("beat");
      beatRefs.current[next]?.focus();
    },
    [beat, beats.length],
  );

  const paneRefs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const onPaneKey = useCallback(
    (e: React.KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.key !== "ArrowRight" && e.key !== "ArrowLeft") return;
      e.preventDefault();
      const next = pane === "beat" ? "briefing" : "beat";
      setPane(next);
      paneRefs.current[next === "beat" ? 0 : 1]?.focus();
    },
    [pane],
  );

  const openBriefing = useCallback(() => {
    setPane("briefing");
    drawerRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: reduced ? "auto" : "smooth", block: "start" });
  }, [reduced]);

  const goToSections = useCallback(() => {
    const slab = sectionsRef.current;
    if (!slab) return;
    // The app can run inside an editor frame whose scrolling container swallows
    // smooth document scrolling. Jump first, then focus the destination without
    // starting a second browser-controlled scroll back toward the hero.
    slab.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "auto", block: "start" });
    slab.focus({ preventScroll: true });
  }, []);


  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const current = beats[beat];

  return (
    <div className="sl">
      {/* ---------------------------------------------------------------- hero */}
      <section className="rd-hero" ref={heroRef} aria-labelledby="rd-title">
        <div className="rd-hero-panel">
          <MeadowScene progress={scrollYProgress}>
            <RecallDemo />
          </MeadowScene>

          <div className="rd-hero-top">
            <span className="rd-crumbs">
              {showBreadcrumbs ? (
                <>
                  {onExit ? (
                    <button type="button" className="sl-crumb-back" onClick={onExit}>
                      Library
                    </button>
                  ) : (
                    "Library"
                  )}
                  <span className="rd-crumb-sep">/</span>
                  Out loud
                  <span className="rd-crumb-sep">/</span>
                  <strong>{title}</strong>
                </>
              ) : (
                <>
                  {onExit && (
                    <>
                      <button type="button" className="sl-crumb-back" onClick={onExit}>
                        Library
                      </button>
                      <span className="rd-crumb-sep">/</span>
                    </>
                  )}
                  <strong>Out loud</strong>
                </>
              )}
            </span>
            {meta && <span className="rd-meta">{meta}</span>}
          </div>

          <motion.div
            className="rd-hero-copy"
            style={reduced ? undefined : { y: copyY, opacity: copyOpacity, filter: copyFilter, scale: copyScale }}
          >
            <span className="sl-chip">
              <Mic size={11} strokeWidth={2.6} aria-hidden="true" />
              Read, Recall, Colour
            </span>
            <h1 className="sl-title" id="rd-title">
              {/* "Read, Recall, Colour · PT102 Section 3" is one title in the
                  record and two lines on the door: the drill, then the section
                  it is run on, in the same italic the library gives "back".
                  The library door's own title carries no " · ", so it renders
                  whole. It used to hardcode "Recall Rephrase Colour" here, a
                  name the product does not have, one line under a chip reading
                  "Read, Recall, Colour". */}
              {title.includes(" · ") ? (
                <>
                  {title.slice(0, title.indexOf(" · "))}
                  <br />
                  <em>{title.slice(title.indexOf(" · ") + 3)}</em>
                </>
              ) : (
                title
              )}
            </h1>
            <p className="sl-tagline">
              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-tagline") : subtitle}
            </p>
            <div className="sl-cta">
              {primaryAction ?? (
                <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={goToSections}>
                  Choose a section
                </button>
              )}
              {briefing && (
                <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={openBriefing}>
                  Read the full instructions
                </button>
              )}
            </div>
          </motion.div>

          <div className="rd-hero-space" aria-hidden="true" />
        </div>
      </section>

      {sectionsFirst && (
        <motion.section
          className="rd-slab"
          id="sl-sections"
          ref={sectionsRef}
          tabIndex={-1}
          aria-label="Choose a section"
          {...reveal}
        >
          {children}
        </motion.section>
      )}



      {(notice || strip) && (
        <div className="rd-band">
          {notice}
          {strip}
        </div>
      )}

      {/* -------------------------------------------------------------- method */}
      {beats.length > 0 && (
        <motion.section className="rd-method" aria-label="Read, Recall, Colour steps" {...reveal}>
          <div
            className="sl-beats"
            role="tablist"
            aria-label="The method, step by step"
            onKeyDown={onBeatKey}
            style={{ ["--sl-beat-count" as string]: String(beats.length) }}
          >
            {beats.map((step, i) => (
              <button
                key={i}
                ref={(el) => {
                  beatRefs.current[i] = el;
                }}
                type="button"
                role="tab"
                id={`sl-beat-tab-${i}`}
                aria-controls="sl-beat-panel"
                aria-selected={pane === "beat" && beat === i}
                tabIndex={beat === i ? 0 : -1}
                className="sl-beat rd-step"
                data-kind={STEP_KINDS[i] ?? "read"}
                onClick={() => {
                  setBeat(i);
                  setPane("beat");
                }}
              >
                <StepScene kind={STEP_KINDS[i] ?? "read"} />
                <span className="rd-step-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <span className="sl-l">{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bl-${i}`)}</span>
                <span className="sl-d">{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bd-${i}`)}</span>
              </button>
            ))}
          </div>

          {briefing && (
            <div className="sl-drawer" ref={drawerRef}>
              <div className="sl-dr-tabs" role="tablist" aria-label="Instruction detail" onKeyDown={onPaneKey}>
                <button
                  ref={(el) => {
                    paneRefs.current[0] = el;
                  }}
                  type="button"
                  role="tab"
                  id="sl-pane-tab-beat"
                  aria-selected={pane === "beat"}
                  aria-controls="sl-beat-panel"
                  tabIndex={pane === "beat" ? 0 : -1}
                  className="sl-dr-tab"
                  onClick={() => setPane("beat")}
                >
                  This step
                </button>
                <button
                  ref={(el) => {
                    paneRefs.current[1] = el;
                  }}
                  type="button"
                  role="tab"
                  id="sl-pane-tab-briefing"
                  aria-selected={pane === "briefing"}
                  aria-controls="sl-beat-panel"
                  tabIndex={pane === "briefing" ? 0 : -1}
                  className="sl-dr-tab"
                  onClick={() => setPane("briefing")}
                >
                  Full instructions
                </button>
              </div>

              <div
                className="sl-dr-view"
                id="sl-beat-panel"
                role="tabpanel"
                tabIndex={-1}
                data-pane={pane}
                aria-labelledby={pane === "beat" ? `sl-pane-tab-beat sl-beat-tab-${beat}` : "sl-pane-tab-briefing"}
              >
                {pane === "beat" && current ? (
                  <div className="rd-dr-step">
                    <span className="sl-label">
                      Step {beat + 1} of {beats.length}
                    </span>
                    <h3 className="sl-dr-title">{renderInlineMarks(current.label, `sl-dt-${beat}`)}</h3>
                    <p className="sl-dr-body">{renderInlineMarks(current.body, `sl-db-${beat}`)}</p>
                    <div className="rd-dr-nav">
                      {beat > 0 && (
                        <button type="button" className="sl-close" onClick={() => setBeat(beat - 1)}>
                          Previous step
                        </button>
                      )}
                      {beat < beats.length - 1 ? (
                        <button type="button" className="sl-close" onClick={() => setBeat(beat + 1)}>
                          Next step
                          <ArrowRight size={13} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
                        </button>
                      ) : (
                        <button type="button" className="sl-close" onClick={goToSections}>
                          Choose a section
                          <ArrowDown size={13} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
                        </button>
                      )}
                    </div>
                  </div>
                ) : (
                  <div className="sl-essay">
                    {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-lede")}</p>}
                    {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
                      <section key={i}>
                        <h4>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-h-${i}`)}</h4>
                        {section.body.map((para, j) => (
                          <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>
                        ))}
                        {section.steps && (
                          <ol>
                            {section.steps.map((step, j) => (
                              <li key={j}>
                                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-sl-${i}-${j}`)}</strong>{" "}
                                {renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-sb-${i}-${j}`)}
                              </li>
                            ))}
                          </ol>
                        )}
                        {section.demo && (
                          <ol>
                            {section.demo.beats.map((b, j) => (
                              <li key={j}>
                                <strong>{b.kind}</strong> {renderInlineMarks(b.text, `sl-demo-${i}-${j}`)}
                              </li>
                            ))}
                          </ol>
                        )}
                      </section>
                    ))}
                  </div>
                )}
                {pane === "briefing" && (
                  <div className="sl-dr-side">
                    <button type="button" className="sl-close" onClick={() => setPane("beat")}>
                      Back to the steps
                    </button>
                    <button type="button" className="sl-close" onClick={goToSections}>
                      Choose a section
                      <ArrowDown size={13} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
                    </button>
                  </div>
                )}
              </div>
            </div>
          )}
        </motion.section>
      )}

      {/* ------------------------------------------------------- what counts */}
      <motion.section className="rd-counts" aria-labelledby="rd-counts-title" {...reveal}>
        <div className="rd-counts-head">
          <h2 id="rd-counts-title" className="rd-h2">
            Your own words, <em>every</em> detail.
          </h2>
          <p className="rd-method-sub">
            Three ways to say the same stimulus back. Only one of them is the drill.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="rd-counts-grid">
          <div className="rd-count" data-tone="good">
            <span className="rd-count-tag">Retelling</span>
            <p className="rd-count-quote">
              “The planners want to widen the road, but the last three cities that tried it got a fifth
              more traffic in two years, so it will probably backfire.”
            </p>
            <p className="rd-count-note">Own words. Every detail. Held together as an argument. This is the drill.</p>
          </div>
          <div className="rd-count">
            <span className="rd-count-tag">Reciting</span>
            <p className="rd-count-quote">
              “City planners argue that widening Elm Street will ease congestion. But in the three cities
              that widened comparable roads…”
            </p>
            <p className="rd-count-note">Word for word. Coverage looks perfect and nothing was understood.</p>
          </div>
          <div className="rd-count">
            <span className="rd-count-tag">Listing</span>
            <p className="rd-count-quote">“Elm Street. Planners. Three cities. A fifth. Two years. Worse.”</p>
            <p className="rd-count-note">Every fact, no argument. Nothing holds it together.</p>
          </div>
        </div>
      </motion.section>

      {/* --------------------------------------------------------------- slab */}
      {!sectionsFirst && (
        <motion.section
          className="rd-slab"
          id="sl-sections"
          ref={sectionsRef}
          tabIndex={-1}
          aria-label="Choose a section"
          {...reveal}
        >
          {children}
        </motion.section>
      )}


      {/* -------------------------------------------------------------- close */}
      <section className="rd-close" aria-label="Start">
        <div className="rd-close-panel">
          <p className="rd-close-line">
            Twenty-five minutes. No pause button. <em>Every detail you can still hold.</em>
          </p>
          {closingAction ?? (
            <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={goToSections}>
              Choose a section
              <ArrowDown size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </section>
    </div>
  );
}

## user

Tool result:
/**
 * A Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run: what is on screen, what she is doing, and
 * what one key press does next.
 *
 * Pure on purpose, like `session.ts`, and deliberately not a variant of it. The
 * v1 session has a `feedback` phase and cumulative rounds; a section has
 * neither, and the reason is not that they were dropped for time. Nothing may
 * be judged, shown or scored until the whole section ends, because a live
 * verdict trains her to vet before speaking, which is the exact habit colouring
 * exists to break (spec `docs/specs/2026-08-31-rrc-section-mode.md` section 4).
 * Sharing a machine with v1 would mean carrying that phase and remembering not
 * to enter it.
 *
 * The unit of work is the section, not the stimulus
 * (`docs/adr/0010-rrc-section-is-the-unit-of-work.md`). She runs 22 to 26
 * stimuli end to end against one clock without stopping.
 *
 * The machine does not track which chunk she is on
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`). She may
 * not chunk the way the content is cut, and a cursor that expects otherwise
 * scores her for disagreeing with our line breaks. Silence carries no signal to
 * drive one with either: a pause while reading and a pause after finishing are
 * the same sound. So the grain here is the stimulus, and a stimulus has exactly
 * two states.
 */

import type { RecallSectionItem, TimeGoalMinutes } from "../api/types";

/**
 * Where the run is within one stimulus.
 *
 * `working` is the whole of steps 1 to 3: the full stimulus is on screen and
 * she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes. `recalling` is
 * The Recall: the text is gone and she says the whole argument back from
 * memory (`docs/adr/0012-the-recall-is-a-recap-from-memory.md`).
 */
export type SectionPhase = "working" | "recalling";

export interface SectionCursor {
  /** 0-based index into the section's items. */
  item: number;
  phase: SectionPhase;
}

/**
 * One stimulus, reduced to what the screen and the scorer need.
 *
 * `theRead` and `theReadType` are carried from the capture and read by nothing.
 * That is deliberate, not an oversight: the analytical takeaway is deferred to
 * a later level, and ADR 0012 records the unused key so nobody wires it in as
 * a fix.
 */
export interface SectionStimulus {
  slug: string;
  order: number;
  /**
   * The authored cuts, kept because the grader's Details live inside them and
   * they guide her eye on screen. Never a boundary she is held to.
   */
  chunks: { n: number; text: string; details: { n: number; text: string }[] }[];
  theRead: string;
  theReadType: string;
  modelRecording: string;
  modelTranscript: string;
}

export interface SectionRun {
  sourceSection: string;
  timeGoalMinutes: TimeGoalMinutes;
  stimuli: SectionStimulus[];
}

/** The three goals, by the accommodation the student runs under. */
export type Accommodation = "standard" | "accommodation_53" | "accommodation_70";

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Building a run
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

function isSectionItem(item: unknown): item is RecallSectionItem {
  const it = item as RecallSectionItem | undefined;
  return Boolean(
    it &&
      typeof it.slug === "string" &&
      typeof it.the_read === "string" &&
      it.the_read.trim() !== "" &&
      Array.isArray(it.chunks) &&
      it.chunks.length > 0 &&
      it.chunks.every(
        (c) =>
          typeof c?.text === "string" &&
          c.text.trim() !== "" &&
          Array.isArray(c.details) &&
          c.details.length > 0,
      ),
  );
}

/**
 * Build a run from an assignment's snapshot, or null if the snapshot is not a
 * section.
 *
 * Returning null rather than throwing is deliberate: the caller renders an
 * explanation instead of a blank screen, and a malformed snapshot is a content
 * bug that must not look like a crash to a student mid-assignment.
 */
export function buildSectionRun(
  items: unknown,
  sourceSection: string | null | undefined,
  timeGoalMinutes: unknown,
): SectionRun | null {
  if (!Array.isArray(items) || items.length === 0) return null;
  if (!sourceSection) return null;
  const goal = timeGoalMinutes as TimeGoalMinutes | null | undefined;
  if (
    !goal ||
    typeof goal.standard !== "number" ||
    typeof goal.accommodation_53 !== "number" ||
    typeof goal.accommodation_70 !== "number"
  ) {
    return null;
  }
  if (!items.every(isSectionItem)) return null;

  const stimuli: SectionStimulus[] = items.map((it) => ({
    slug: it.slug,
    order: it.order,
    chunks: it.chunks.map((c) => ({
      n: c.n,
      text: c.text,
      details: c.details.map((d) => ({ n: d.n, text: d.text })),
    })),
    theRead: it.the_read,
    theReadType: it.the_read_type,
    modelRecording: it.model_recording,
    modelTranscript: it.model_transcript,
  }));

  return { sourceSection, timeGoalMinutes: goal, stimuli };
}

export function startCursor(): SectionCursor {
  return { item: 0, phase: "working" };
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Advancing
// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * One key press.
 *
 * There is exactly one input in the loop and no mouse, so this is the whole
 * interaction model: the first press on a stimulus is her saying "Recall Full
 * Stimulus" -- the text goes and she recaps it from memory -- and the second
 * ends the Recall and loads the next stimulus. Those two moments are the only
 * ones not recoverable from the audio afterwards, which is why they are the
 * only ones asked of her.
 *
 * Returns `null` when the section is over. The caller stops the clock there;
 * this function does not know about time, which is what makes it testable.
 */
export function advanceCursor(run: SectionRun, at: SectionCursor): SectionCursor | null {
  if (!run.stimuli[at.item]) return null;

  if (at.phase === "working") return { item: at.item, phase: "recalling" };

  // The Recall is finished. The stimulus does not come back until the Report
  // Card, so there is nothing between here and the next one.
  const nextItem = at.item + 1;
  if (nextItem >= run.stimuli.length) return null;
  return { item: nextItem, phase: "working" };
}

/**
 * Whether she is inside the goal for her accommodation.
 *
 * Time is a scored criterion and the goal cannot be one number: a student with
 * 53% or 70% extra time is not slow when she takes longer, and grading her
 * against the standard goal would score her accommodation as a failure.
 */
export function goalSeconds(run: SectionRun, accommodation: Accommodation): number {
  return run.timeGoalMinutes[accommodation] * 60;
}

/** Elapsed time as `M:SS`, which is how a clock she glances at should read. */
export function formatElapsed(seconds: number): string {
  const safe = Math.max(0, Math.floor(seconds));
  const m = Math.floor(safe / 60);
  const s = safe % 60;
  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}
=====RECORDER=====
import { useCallback, useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";

/**
 * The section's recording: one unbroken file, plus an index of what is where.
 *
 * The drill's own rule, from `corpus/loophole-online/drills/translation-plus-clir-drill.md`:
 * press record at the very beginning and do not end the recording until the
 * whole drill is finished, never pausing or stopping between steps or stimuli.
 * So this records once, continuously, and stops once.
 *
 * That rule and "organise the recording per portion of each question" only look
 * like they conflict. They are reconciled by an index rather than by cutting:
 * every phase boundary the runner passes is stamped as an offset from the start
 * of the file, so any portion -- a single chunk's recall, one stimulus's Read,
 * the whole of stimulus 14 -- is a seek, not a separate recording. Nothing has
 * to be found by ear afterwards.
 *
 * There is exactly one break in that rule and it is the student's, not the
 * drill's: `pause` and `resume`. The rule above forbids stopping BETWEEN STEPS
 * -- pausing to think, to check a word, to compose herself before The Recall --
 * because a drill practised in pieces is not the drill. It was never a rule that
 * a twenty-five minute sitting may not be interrupted by a life, and enforced as
 * one it produced the only outcome worse than a paused take: a student who has
 * to leave, cannot, and throws away twenty-five minutes of work by walking away
 * from it. The recorder is paused rather than stopped, so it is still one file
 * and still one index. What the pause must never do is desynchronise the two,
 * which is why it is the RUNNER's clock that stops with it: every offset in this
 * file is measured with the `now` the runner injects, and a paused run's `now`
 * does not advance (`SectionRunner.tsx`). The break is therefore absent from the
 * audio and absent from the timeline by the same arithmetic, and how long she
 * was away is recorded on the run as its own number rather than hidden inside
 * her time.
 *
 * What this does NOT do is transcribe. Transcription happens off the machine
 * the drill is running on, against the finished file, so a slow or failed model
 * call can never touch a twenty-minute unbroken take.
 */

/**
 * Which portion of which stimulus a stretch of the file belongs to.
 *
 * Stimulus-grained: `working` is her time with the text up, `recalling` is The
 * Recall. There is no chunk field, because the app does not track which chunk
 * she is on (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`);
 * what she covered inside a stretch is worked out from the audio afterwards.
 */
export interface SegmentBody {
  /** The stimulus this belongs to, by its content slug. */
  slug: string;
  /** 1-based position of that stimulus in the section. */
  order: number;
  phase: "working" | "recalling";
}

/** One stretch of the file, in ms from the start of the recording. */
export interface RecordingSegment extends SegmentBody {
  startMs: number;
  endMs: number;
  /**
   * A segment is stored verbatim inside `activity_item_responses.rrc`, which is
   * a `jsonb` column typed as `Json`. The index signature is what lets this
   * interface satisfy that type without a cast at the insert, so the shape is
   * checked rather than asserted. It sits here and not on `SegmentBody` because
   * `Omit` over an index signature erases the named members with it.
   */
  [key: string]: string | number;
}

export interface FinishedRecording {
  blob: Blob;
  mimeType: string;
  durationMs: number;
  segments: RecordingSegment[];
}

/**
 * Pick a container the browser will actually produce. Safari has no webm/opus,
 * so asking for it there yields a recorder that starts and emits nothing, which
 * is the worst failure available: it looks like it worked.
 */
function pickMimeType(): string | undefined {
  const candidates = [
    "audio/webm;codecs=opus",
    "audio/webm",
    "audio/mp4",
    "audio/ogg;codecs=opus",
  ];
  const supported = (window as unknown as { MediaRecorder?: typeof MediaRecorder }).MediaRecorder;
  if (!supported?.isTypeSupported) return undefined;
  return candidates.find((t) => supported.isTypeSupported(t));
}

/**
 * What the microphone is asked for, and what it is asked NOT to do.
 *
 * `{ audio: true }` takes Chrome's defaults, which are tuned for a phone call:
 * automatic gain, noise suppression and echo cancellation, all on. Measured on
 * a live 21-minute run, all three were active. For a call that is right. For
 * this it is not, and in three separate ways.
 *
 * The take is twenty-odd minutes with long SILENT reads inside it, so automatic
 * gain spends those reads winding the input up and then pumps room noise into
 * the gap before she speaks again. Noise suppression is a speech/not-speech
 * judgement made by the browser, ahead of ours, and the quiet end of her
 * delivery -- a trailed-off clause, an aside, the half-swallowed word -- is
 * exactly what it is most likely to decide is not speech. And what comes off
 * this file is not listened to, it is transcribed and then graded Detail by
 * Detail: a word the browser removed is a Detail she is marked as having
 * dropped.
 *
 * So they are asked for explicitly, and off. Mono because the source is one
 * voice and a second channel is bytes against a bucket cap, not information.
 *
 * The constraints are not `exact`. A device that cannot honour one of them
 * should still record the section: a run with automatic gain is worse than a
 * run without it and far better than no run. Nothing here can fail the drill.
 */
const AUDIO_CONSTRAINTS: MediaTrackConstraints = {
  autoGainControl: false,
  noiseSuppression: false,
  echoCancellation: false,
  channelCount: 1,
};

/**
 * Open the microphone, preferring the untreated stream and accepting the
 * treated one.
 *
 * Older browsers reject an unknown constraint key outright rather than ignoring
 * it, so a single request would turn "cannot switch off noise suppression" into
 * "no microphone" -- trading a slightly worse recording for none at all. The
 * retry is what stops a preference from ever becoming a requirement.
 */
async function openMicrophone(): Promise<MediaStream> {
  // No microphone API at all -- an insecure origin, or a browser without it.
  // That is the refused-microphone case, not a constraint the device would not
  // take, and retrying it would only produce the same TypeError twice.
  if (!navigator.mediaDevices?.getUserMedia) throw new Error("no microphone on this device");
  try {
    return await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: AUDIO_CONSTRAINTS });
  } catch (e) {
    // A refusal is not a constraint failure and must not be retried into a
    // second permission prompt.
    const name = (e as DOMException)?.name;
    if (name === "NotAllowedError" || name === "SecurityError") throw e;
    console.warn("[rrc] microphone would not take the drill's constraints; using its defaults", e);
    return await navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({ audio: true });
  }
}

/**
 * How often the backup recorder hands over a piece.
 *
 * The most that can ever be lost to a crash. Ten seconds is short enough that
 * nothing meaningful goes missing and long enough that a twenty-minute section
 * costs about 130 small writes to her own disk, which is nothing.
 */
const BACKUP_SLICE_MS = 10_000;

export function useSectionRecorder(
  now: () => number = () => Date.now(),
  /**
   * Called with each piece of the parallel backup copy, in order.
   *
   * Optional, and the drill runs identically without it. The recorder does not
   * know or care where the pieces go; keeping the storage decision outside this
   * hook is what lets the backup be swapped or switched off without touching
   * the thing that produces the real recording.
   */
  onBackupChunk?: (blob: Blob, seq: number) => void,
) {
  const recorder = useRef<MediaRecorder | null>(null);
  const backup = useRef<MediaRecorder | null>(null);
  const backupSeq = useRef(0);
  const stream = useRef<MediaStream | null>(null);
  /** Kept in a ref so the callback can change without restarting a recorder. */
  const onChunk = useRef(onBackupChunk);
  onChunk.current = onBackupChunk;
  const parts = useRef<Blob[]>([]);
  const startedAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
  const segments = useRef<RecordingSegment[]>([]);
  const openAt = useRef<number>(0);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [recording, setRecording] = useState(false);
  const [paused, setPaused] = useState(false);

  /**
   * Ask for the microphone and start. Resolves either way, and reports the
   * refusal rather than throwing it.
   *
   * This used to say that a refused microphone must not stop the drill, because
   * the sitting is still valid against a handheld recorder. It is not: the
   * grader refuses a run with no recording, and no handheld file has any way to
   * reach the assignment. The runner now treats a reported refusal as a gate on
   * the ready beat (ADR 0029), so what this returns decides whether a section
   * begins at all.
   *
   * Safe to call again after a failure, and only after one: nothing has been
   * opened, so there is no recorder to restart.
   */
  const start = useCallback(async () => {
    if (recorder.current) return;
    try {
      const media = await openMicrophone();
      const mimeType = pickMimeType();
      const rec = new MediaRecorder(media, mimeType ? { mimeType } : undefined);
      parts.current = [];
      segments.current = [];
      rec.ondataavailable = (e) => {
        if (e.data.size > 0) parts.current.push(e.data);
      };
      rec.start();

      /*
       * The second recorder, on the same microphone.
       *
       * `rec` above is the real recording and is deliberately left alone: asked
       * for pieces as it goes, it can no longer write its own duration and the
       * file stops being seekable, which is what Clips are cut with. This one
       * exists only so a crash does not cost twenty minutes, and a recovered
       * copy only has to be good enough to grade.
       *
       * Its failure is swallowed whole. A browser that will not run two
       * recorders at once still runs the drill.
       */
      if (onChunk.current) {
        try {
          const copy = new MediaRecorder(media, mimeType ? { mimeType } : undefined);
          backupSeq.current = 0;
          copy.ondataavailable = (e) => {
            if (e.data.size > 0) onChunk.current?.(e.data, backupSeq.current++);
          };
          copy.start(BACKUP_SLICE_MS);
          backup.current = copy;
        } catch (e) {
          console.error("[rrc] no local backup copy for this run", e);
        }
      }

      stream.current = media;
      recorder.current = rec;
      startedAt.current = now();
      openAt.current = 0;
      setRecording(true);
      setError(null);
    } catch {
      // The name of the fault is not useful to her; the consequence is.
      setError("No microphone. The drill still runs, but nothing is being recorded.");
    }
  }, [now]);

  /**
   * Close the portion that just ended. Called on every phase boundary, from
   * inside the loop -- which is safe because it touches refs and no network.
   */
  const mark = useCallback(
    (segment: SegmentBody): RecordingSegment | null => {
      if (startedAt.current === null) return null;
      const at = now() - startedAt.current;
      const closed: RecordingSegment = { ...segment, startMs: openAt.current, endMs: at };
      segments.current.push(closed);
      openAt.current = at;
      // Handed back as well as kept, so a caller saving one stimulus as it
      // finishes can carry that stimulus's own portions with it. Without this
      // the index is only readable at `stop`, which is the one moment a crash
      // means it never arrives.
      return closed;
    },
    [now],
  );

  /**
   * Hold the file open and stop writing to it.
   *
   * `pause` is not `stop`: the recorder keeps its parts, its mime type and its
   * identity, so what resumes is the same take rather than a second one. That
   * distinction is the whole reason this is allowed at all -- the drill forbids
   * a second recording, not a gap in the first.
   *
   * It answers whether it actually paused, and the runner treats a false as a
   * refusal to pause rather than as a pause that failed quietly. A recorder that
   * kept running while the runner's clock stopped would put minutes of a break
   * inside the file and nowhere in the index, and every segment boundary after
   * it would name the wrong stretch of audio. Silence about that is worse than
   * telling her this browser will not do it.
   *
   * The backup copy is paused too, and its failure is swallowed like every other
   * failure of the backup copy: a recovered file with a break the index does not
   * know about is still a file worth having.
   */
  const pause = useCallback((): boolean => {
    const rec = recorder.current;
    if (!rec || rec.state !== "recording") return false;
    try {
      rec.pause();
    } catch {
      return false;
    }
    /*
     * Asked for, and then checked. A browser is allowed to implement `pause`
     * as a no-op, and a no-op here is the misalignment this returns false to
     * prevent.
     *
     * Read into a widened local because TypeScript narrowed `rec.state` to
     * "recording" at the guard above and does not model `pause()` as changing
     * it, so the comparison it needs to see is the one it calls impossible.
     */
    const afterPause: string = rec.state;
    if (afterPause !== "paused") return false;
    try {
      if (backup.current?.state === "recording") backup.current.pause();
    } catch {
      // The backup is best effort and always has been.
    }
    setPaused(true);
    return true;
  }, []);

  /** Start writing to the same file again. Answers whether it did. */
  const resume = useCallback((): boolean => {
    const rec = recorder.current;
    if (!rec || rec.state !== "paused") return false;
    try {
      rec.resume();
    } catch {
      return false;
    }
    // Widened for the same reason as `afterPause` above.
    const afterResume: string = rec.state;
    if (afterResume !== "recording") return false;
    try {
      if (backup.current?.state === "paused") backup.current.resume();
    } catch {
      // As above.
    }
    setPaused(false);
    return true;
  }, []);

  /** Stop once, at the end, and hand back the file with its index. */
  const stop = useCallback(async (): Promise<FinishedRecording | null> => {
    const rec = recorder.current;
    if (!rec || startedAt.current === null) return null;
    const durationMs = now() - startedAt.current;
    const mimeType = rec.mimeType || "audio/webm";
    const finished = new Promise<Blob>((resolve) => {
      rec.onstop = () => resolve(new Blob(parts.current, { type: mimeType }));
    });
    rec.stop();
    // Stopped before the tracks are, so its last piece is emitted rather than
    // cut off with the microphone.
    try {
      backup.current?.stop();
    } catch {
      // Already stopped, or never started. Neither is worth reporting.
    }
    backup.current = null;
    stream.current?.getTracks().forEach((t) => t.stop());
    recorder.current = null;
    stream.current = null;
    setRecording(false);
    setPaused(false);
    const blob = await finished;
    return { blob, mimeType, durationMs, segments: segments.current.slice() };
  }, [now]);

  /**
   * A recorder does not outlive the component that started it.
   *
   * `stop` is the run's own ending and the only place this used to close the
   * microphone. A section she LEFT -- the exit button, a route change, any
   * unmount before `done` -- left both recorders running with the microphone
   * open, invisible, for as long as the tab lived. On 2026-09-07 one such
   * orphan ran for two hours: through the student's real sitting of the same
   * section and past its end, feeding its backup the whole time. When the tab
   * finally reloaded, the recovery pass took that orphan's 116-minute file for
   * a crashed run and uploaded it over the 66-minute recording the real run
   * had stored, and the grader refused the section for a clock that sat 37
   * minutes off.
   *
   * So the tracks close with the hook. Nothing is handed back: whatever this
   * recorder held belongs to a run that is, by the drill's own rule, thrown
   * away (ADR 0023). A run that ended properly has already been through `stop`
   * and there is nothing here left to close.
   */
  useEffect(
    () => () => {
      for (const rec of [recorder.current, backup.current]) {
        try {
          if (rec && rec.state !== "inactive") rec.stop();
        } catch {
          // Already stopped. Nothing to close twice.
        }
      }
      stream.current?.getTracks().forEach((t) => t.stop());
      recorder.current = null;
      backup.current = null;
      stream.current = null;
    },
    [],
  );

  /**
   * The live input, for anything that needs to listen rather than store.
   *
   * A getter rather than state: the voice detector reads it inside an animation
   * frame loop, and re-rendering the whole runner when the microphone opens
   * would be a render in the middle of her first read.
   */
  const getStream = useCallback(() => stream.current, []);

  /**
   * The container the recorder settled on, readable from the first stimulus.
   *
   * The file name depends on it, and a row written before the section ends has
   * to name the file it will belong to. Falls back to the type the picker would
   * have chosen, so this answers even before the microphone opens.
   */
  const getMimeType = useCallback(
    () => recorder.current?.mimeType || pickMimeType() || "audio/webm",
    [],
  );

  /**
   * The instant the file begins, or null before the microphone opens.
   *
   * Every segment in the index is an offset from this, and the transcript is
   * cut against those segments -- so anything stamping the transcript has to
   * measure from the same instant or the two channels disagree by however long
   * the microphone took to answer. Recognition starts at mount and the recorder
   * starts when `getUserMedia` resolves, which on a first run is a permission
   * prompt: several seconds of skew, silently shifting every piece of her
   * transcript into a later segment than the one she said it in.
   */
  const getStartedAt = useCallback(() => startedAt.current, []);

  return { start, pause, resume, mark, stop, error, recording, paused, getStream, getMimeType, getStartedAt };
}

  return {
    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    item_order,
    carried: 3,
    total: 3,
    percent: 100,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,

    ...over,

}

const SLOTS = 25;
function run(seconds: number, createdAt: string, gaps: number[] = [], silent: number[] = []) {
  return {

    attempt_no: 1,

    graded_at: createdAt,
    created_at: createdAt,

      const n = i + 1;

}

import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { SectionLibraryPanel } from "@/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibraryPanel";
import "@/features/activity-centre/activity-centre.css";

/**
 * Read, Recall, Colour, reachable without waiting to be assigned it.
 *
 * The door used to exist only as an assignment: a student who had never been
 * given the section-library Activity had no address for it at all, even though
 * every section behind it is open to her and starting one is a server call that
 * builds its own Attempt. This is that same door at a permanent address, linked
 * from the Activities page.
 */
export default function ActivitySections() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  return <SectionLibraryPanel onExit={() => navigate("/activities")} />;
}
=====SectionLibraryPanel=====
import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { recoverPendingRecordings, recoverPendingRows } from "../recall/recoverRecordings";
import { useStudentActivities } from "../hooks/useStudentActivities";
import { SectionLibrary } from "./SectionLibrary";
import { SectionLibraryLaunch } from "./SectionLibraryLaunch";
import { SectionProgress } from "./SectionProgress";
import sectionInstructions from "../content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";

/**
 * The section list, wired to her own record and to the router.
 *
 * `SectionLibrary` stays pure so it can be rendered in a test without a session
 * or a router. This is the half that knows about both.
 *
 * Starting an Attempt navigates to that Attempt's own assignment and opens the
 * recorder immediately. The library already shows the complete instructions;
 * repeating them on a second page made Start look as though it had not opened
 * the drill at all.
 */
export function SectionLibraryPanel({
  onExit,
  title = "Read, Recall, Colour",
  subtitle = null,
  instructions,
}: {
  onExit?: () => void;
  title?: string;
  subtitle?: string | null;
  /**
   * The drill's own briefing. Defaults to the authored section instructions
   * because the door is not a section and carries no snapshot of its own, and
   * the method it describes is the same one every section is run by.
   */
  instructions?: ActivityInstructions;
}) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const { assignments } = useStudentActivities();

  /*
   * Send up anything a previous run left on this machine.
   *
   * The runner already does this on the way into an Attempt, which only helps
   * a student who starts another one. This is the door she actually comes back
   * through, so a recording or a set of answers stranded by a crash goes up as
   * soon as she opens the drill -- and is markable from that moment. Silent and
   * best-effort: a failed pass must never keep this page from opening.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!user?.id) return;
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      try {
        await recoverPendingRecordings(user.id);
        if (cancelled) return;
        await recoverPendingRows(user.id);
      } catch {
        // Nothing to say here. The next open tries again.
      }
    })();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [user?.id]);

  return (
    <SectionLibraryLaunch
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions ?? (sectionInstructions as unknown as ActivityInstructions)}
      onExit={onExit}
    >
      {/*
        * Starting and reopening are the same navigation, on purpose.
        *
        * An Attempt IS its assignment, so its Report Card is at the
        * assignment's own address -- which is what makes a past Attempt
        * reachable from any device at any later date rather than only from
        * the tab that produced it. A completed section routes itself to the
        * card rather than to the runner.
        */}
      <SectionProgress onOpen={(id) => navigate(`/activities/${id}`)} />

      <SectionLibrary
        attempts={assignments}
        onStart={(id) => navigate(`/activities/${id}`, { state: { startSection: true } })}
        onOpenAttempt={(id) => navigate(`/activities/${id}`)}
      />
    </SectionLibraryLaunch>
  );
}

Tool result:
/* The Read, Recall, Colour door.
 *
 * One evening, in two registers. The hero is the runner's own indigo lifted
 * into a dusk sky over a painted meadow, cut into depth planes that move at
 * their own rates as she scrolls; everything under it sits on cream paper in
 * the same light. The method is performed before it is explained, the four
 * steps are the next thing she meets, and the sections she can run sit
 * directly beneath.
 *
 * Every value is scoped to `.sl`. Nothing here touches the runner
 * (`recall/section.css`) or the shell outside this door. Class names beginning
 * `sl-` are the door's contract with its tests and its two wrappers; `rd-`
 * names are the design and may change freely.
 */

.sl {
  /* The hero panel's ink. Named so a rule that borrows it onto cream is
     visible as a mistake instead of reading as an ordinary colour. */
  --rd-on-dark: #fff;
  --rd-cream: #f6f3ea;
  --rd-paper: #fffdf8;
  --rd-paper-2: #f3efe4;
  --rd-ink: #16132b;
  --rd-ink-2: #46435f;
  --rd-ink-3: #6a6784;
  --rd-line: #e3ded2;
  --rd-line-2: #ede9df;
  --rd-indigo: #1a1635;
  --rd-indigo-2: #2a2450;
  --rd-lavender: #c9c3ec;
  --rd-apricot: #f3c9a0;
  --rd-apricot-light: #ffe8c8;
  --rd-marigold: #f2b544;
  --rd-marigold-ink: #6e4600;
  --rd-marigold-wash: rgba(242, 181, 68, 0.26);
  --rd-emerald: #0e6f4d;
  --rd-emerald-wash: rgba(14, 111, 77, 0.11);
  --rd-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rd-sans: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif;
  --rd-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;
  --rd-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
  --rd-radius: 22px;
  --rd-radius-panel: 28px;

  min-height: 100%;
  background: var(--rd-cream);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-family: var(--rd-sans);
  font-size: 16px;
  line-height: 1.5;
  -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;
  accent-color: var(--rd-ink);
  caret-color: var(--rd-marigold-ink);
  scrollbar-color: #c9c3b6 var(--rd-cream);
}
.sl ::selection {
  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl :focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
  box-shadow: none;
  border-radius: 6px;
}
.sl em {
  font-style: italic;
}

/* ---- the one micro-label style ---- */
.sl-label {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}

/* =============================================================== hero */

.rd-hero {
  padding: 14px 14px 0;
}
.rd-hero-panel {
  position: relative;
  isolation: isolate;
  overflow: hidden;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  min-height: calc(100svh - 28px);
  border-radius: var(--rd-radius-panel);
  background: #2a2450;
  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
}

/* The scene: five planes and a stage, each its own layer. */
.mw {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 0;
  overflow: hidden;
}
.mw-layer {
  position: absolute;
  inset: -6% 0 0 0;
  width: 100%;
  height: 106%;
  display: block;
  overflow: visible;
  will-change: transform;
}
.mw-sky {
  z-index: 1;
}
.mw-far {
  z-index: 2;
}
.mw-mid {
  z-index: 3;
}
