.rc {
  /* ── The console: a deep plum field ─────────────────────────────────── */
  --rc-console: oklch(0.245 0.058 312);
  --rc-console-raised: oklch(0.315 0.062 312);
  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.385 0.055 312);
  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.968 0.014 312);
  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);
  --rc-colour-lit: oklch(0.83 0.13 82);

  --rc-method-ink: oklch(0.455 0.09 278);
  --rc-method-soft: oklch(0.951 0.026 278);
  --rc-method-lit: oklch(0.79 0.085 280);

  /* Sound. Not a category, and the only thing on the page that moves. Held at
     328 rather than nearer the reds: the playhead lands on the same line as the
     dropped-Detail chips, and twenty degrees of hue is not enough separation
     between "this is playing" and "this is what you lost". */
  --rc-signal: oklch(0.545 0.225 328);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.715 0.2 328);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.957 0.032 328);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.995 0.005 328);

  /* The two faint inks are set where they are because that is where they clear
     4.5:1 on their own ground, measured in the browser rather than assumed.
     Every label on this card is 10px, so there is no large-text exemption to
     lean on. */
  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rc-body: "Lexend", "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --rc-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

  background: var(--rc-page);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
}

/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped
   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */
.rc__measure {
  margin-inline: auto;
  max-width: 72ch;
}

/* ── Micro-labels ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   One label style for the whole card. Every section of a stimulus wears it,
   which is what makes the order legible: she learns four labels once and then
   knows where she is on all twenty-two. */
.rc__label {
  color: var(--rc-ink-faint);
  font-family: var(--rc-mono);
  font-size: 10px;
  font-weight: 500;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.rc__label--onconsole {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);
}

.rc__display {
  font-family: var(--rc-display);
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 96;
  font-weight: 380;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  line-height: 1.02;
}

/* ── The console ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

.rc__console {
  background: var(--rc-console);
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
}

.rc__console-divider {
  border-color: var(--rc-console-hairline);
}

/* ── The ledger of twenty-two ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   The whole section as one row of marks, so the shape of the run is visible
   before any one stimulus is opened. Twenty-two accordion rows are a list;
   this is a picture. */
.rc__ledger {
  display: grid;
  gap: 4px;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(28px, 1fr));
}

.rc__tick {
  align-items: center;
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: 6px;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 5px;
  /* 44px of target, reached by being 44px rather than by wrapped text. */
  min-height: 44px;
  justify-content: center;
  padding: 4px 0;
  transition: background-color 160ms var(--rc-ease);
}

.rc__tick:hover {
  background: var(--rc-console-raised);
}

.rc__tick:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal-bright);

{"description": "Critique A: LLM design review of RRC", "subagent_type": "general-purpose", "run_in_background": true, "prompt": "You are Assessment A of an `$impeccable critique`: an independent LLM design review, done like a design director, of the whole \"Read, Recall, Colour\" (RRC) drill in the LSAT U web app. You must not see or ask for any other assessment's output. Do not edit any project files. Return structured findings as text.\n\n## What the interface is trying to accomplish\nRRC is a spoken LSAT drill. A student opens a door page, picks a section (22 to 26 Logical Reasoning stimuli), reads a briefing, presses Start, and then runs the whole section in one recorded take against a 25-minute goal: for each stimulus she reads it out loud in pieces, tells each piece back in her own words, \"colours it\" (says her own reaction), then presses Space, the text vanishes and she recalls the whole argument from memory, then presses Space for the next one. Nothing is judged live. After the run, an AI grader marks it and she gets a Report Card: what she carried, what she dropped, her own recording beside the standard, line by line, and what to do next time. The students are paying one-to-one coaching clients of Joshua (the sole instructor), mostly on laptops, in 25-minute focused sittings, often at night. There is one real student's incident on record: a run whose recorder started 37 minutes early and a grade stuck \"still being marked\" for 8 hours.\n\n## Repo and server\n- Repo root: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu. Read files with Bash (cat / sed -n) or Read.\n- Dev server ALREADY running at http://localhost:60738 . Do NOT start a server.\n- Browser: use mcp__Claude_Browser__* tools. FIRST call mcp__Claude_Browser__tabs_create to get your own tabId and pass it on EVERY browser call. After each navigation, run javascript_tool: document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title; so the owner can tell your tab apart. Never act on a tab you did not create. Only use the URLs listed here; never invent a path.\n\n## The four surfaces and where to see them\n1. Door (library and section pages): http://localhost:60738/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html?state=library and ?state=runs (also ?state=empty, ?state=failed). Code: src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx, SectionLaunch.tsx, SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx, SectionLibrary.tsx, SectionProgress.tsx, MeadowScene.tsx, RecallDemo.tsx, section-launch.css.\n2. Briefing page: src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx (+ `.sl-briefing-page` rules at the end of section-launch.css). Content JSON: src/features/activity-centre/content/read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json. Check src/dev/sectionLaunchPreview.tsx for a harness state; if none exists review from code and the JSON and say so.\n3. Runner (the drill itself): http://localhost:60738/activity-lab then click the \"section\" tab button (use mcp__Claude_Browser__find with query \"section\" to get its ref). Inside it there are \"Moment\" buttons: Running, Ready Beat, Paused, Finishing, plus stimulus number buttons, and a \"Leave\" button that opens the confirm dialog. Press the space key (computer action key \"space\") to move from working to recalling (text vanishes). Code: src/features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage.tsx, SectionRunner.tsx, VoiceLevel.tsx, coach.ts (first-run walkthrough card; NOT rendered in the lab, so read it), section.css. Note the lab has no microphone so the presence bar is flat, and the walkthrough aside is not shown in the lab.\n4. Report Card: http://localhost:60738/src/dev/report-card-preview.html?state=faults&frame=runner (also ?state=clean&frame=runner, ?state=partial&frame=runner, and &playing=2). Code: src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.tsx, report-card.css, SectionReport.tsx (the \"still being marked\" polling wrapper), SectionRunHistory.tsx.\n\n## Context to read first\n- C:/Users/Joshu/.claude/skills/impeccable/reference/critique.md, section \"Assessment A: LLM Design Review\" (your method), plus heuristics-scoring.md, cognitive-load.md, personas.md, product.md (this is PRODUCT register: the tool should disappear into the task), and SKILL.md \"Shared design laws\" + \"Absolute bans\" + \"The AI slop test\".\n- Repo PRODUCT.md (users, principles, brand commitments incl. no em dashes and \"the coach is not simulated\", WCAG AA floor) and DESIGN.md (two registers; sanctioned scoped worlds: the door `.sl`, the Report Card `.rc`; the runner deliberately wears the LawHub official-section look: indigo field, white paper, Fraunces 19px).\n- .impeccable/surfaces/src-features-activity-centre.md sections \"Read, Recall, Colour door\" and \"The Report Card\" (the owner's locked briefs; critique against their intent, and say where the build falls short of the brief or where the brief itself is the problem).\n- src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md, subsection \"Read, Recall, Colour\" (vocabulary the product uses and the words it avoids).\n- The instructions JSON above (what the student reads).\n\n## Evaluate (per critique.md)\n- AI slop detection, both registers: door is brand-ish (Persuade), runner and card are product (Operate). Run the category-reflex check at both altitudes (first-order and second-order). Would a design director say \"AI made this\"? Be specific about what tells and what earns its place.\n- Holistic review across the four surfaces AS ONE JOURNEY: hierarchy, IA, emotional resonance for a nervous student at 9pm, discoverability, composition, typography (four type systems across four surfaces: Fraunces/Inter/Plex on the door, Helvetica Neue + Fraunces in the runner, Fraunces/Lexend/Plex on the card; is that a seam problem?), colour, states and edge cases (mic refused, paused, dead input, upload failed, still being marked, ungraded, first run vs fourth run), microcopy (tone, consistency of the step names: the door says \"Read it, Tell it back, Colour it, Recall it\"; the runner's ready beat says \"once when you have read it and said it back, once when you have given your Read\"; the walkthrough says \"say what that piece is doing in the argument, the colour\"; are these the same method to a student?).\n- Cognitive load: the 8-item checklist on each surface, with the failure count. === instructions.json structure ===
[ 'task', 'coach', 'rules', 'tagline', 'briefing' ]
tagline: Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.
rules: [
 "Everything here is out loud. The whole section records in one take, and it never pauses.",
 "Read silently, about a sentence at a time. When you are not sure how much to take on, take less.",
 "Look away and tell it back in your own words. Then react to it. Both out loud.",
 "Keep going like that through the whole stimulus.",
 "Press space when you have got all of it. The stimulus vanishes and stays gone. Now say the whole argument back from memory, with every detail you can hold.",
 "Press space again and the next one loads. No breaks, no going back, all the way to the end."
]
lede: Worth reading properly once. After that, skim the rules above and go.
closer: One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.
## You can understand every sentence and still arrive with nothing.
## What you actually do.
  step: Read it. :: Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.
  step: Tell it back. :: Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.
  step: Colour it. :: React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.
  step: The Recall. :: Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.
## Simpler is not the same as shorter.
## You will think you have nothing to say. Everybody does.
## Look away. That part is on you.
## Twenty-five minutes, and what that number means.

## user

Tool result:
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  getSectionGrade,
  requestSectionGrading,
  startSectionFocusAttempt,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReportCard } from "./SectionReportCard";

/**
 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
 *
 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus
 * one judgement call per stimulus, run server-side after she stops. It does not
 * finish inside the request that ends the section, and it must not be tied to
 * this tab staying open.
 *
 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
 */

const POLL_MS = 4000;
/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
/**
 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
 *
 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
 *
 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
 */
const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;

interface SectionReportProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  onDone?: () => void;
}

export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    let askedAgain = false;

    const poll = async () => {
      try {
        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
        if (cancelled) return;
        if (found) {
          setGrade(found);
          return;
        }
      } catch {
        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
      }
      if (cancelled) return;

      const waited = Date.now() - startedAt.current;
      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
        askedAgain = true;
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
      }
      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
        setStillWaiting(true);
        return;
      }
      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
    };

    void poll();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [assignmentId]);

  if (grade)
    return (
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={grade}
        onDone={onDone}
        onStartFocusSet={async (slugs) => {
          // A fresh Attempt built server-side from this run's weakest stimuli,
          // entered straight into the runner the way the library enters it.
          const id = await startSectionFocusAttempt(assignmentId, slugs);
          navigate(`/activities/${id}`, { state: { startSection: true } });
        }}
      />
    );

  return (
    <div className="mx-auto max-w-2xl">
      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          Section finished
        </p>
        <p className="ac-display mt-3 text-4xl text-foreground">
          {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
        </p>
        <p className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
          {stillWaiting
            ? "This one is taking longer than usual. Your run is saved and your Report Card will be here when you come back to it."
            : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. This takes a minute."}
        </p>
        {onDone && (
          <div className="mt-8">
            <Button variant={stillWaiting ? "default" : "outline"} onClick={onDone}>
              Done
            </Button>
          </div>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
=====ActivityRunner section briefing block=====

  /*
   * The door. Joshua assigns Read, Recall, Colour; she picks which section to
   * run tonight, and she runs several. This Activity has no items of its own:
   * its content is the list.
   *
   * It sits ABOVE the `phase === "intro"` return rather than below it, because
   * the door has no intro to precede it -- it IS the first screen. Behind that
   * return it was unreachable except by pressing Start on an editorial page
   * written for the typed Activities, which is how a student met three screens
   * before her first run and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
   */
  if (assignment.mode === "section-library") {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen" data-runner="section-library">
        {adminBanner}
        <SectionLibraryPanel
          title={assignment.title}
          subtitle={assignment.subtitle}
          instructions={assignment.instructions}
          onExit={handleLeave}
        />
      </div>
    );
  }

  /*
   * A section's front door is its instructions, in full, on their own page.
   *
   * The editorial intro folded the method into tabs, so a student pressing
   * Start from the library either met a page she had to unfold or -- when the
   * launch skipped it -- a live microphone with no explanation at all.
   */
  if (phase === "intro" && assignment.mode === "section" && assignment.item_format === "spoken-recall") {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen" data-runner="section-briefing">
        {adminBanner}
        <SectionBriefingPage
          title={assignment.title}
          subtitle={assignment.subtitle}
          instructions={assignment.instructions}
          onStart={handleStart}
          onExit={handleLeave}
        />
      </div>
    );
  }

  if (phase === "intro") {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre act-home" data-world={world?.slug}>
        {adminBanner}
        <ActivityIntro
          title={assignment.title}
          subtitle={assignment.subtitle}
          instructions={assignment.instructions}
          itemCount={items.length}
          maxAttempts={MAX_ATTEMPTS}
          format={assignment.item_format}
          mode={assignment.mode}
          resumeAt={index + 1}
          /*
           * The goal is per accommodation, so there is no single "target" on the
           * assignment to read. A student on 70% extra time is not slow when she
           * takes longer, and showing her the standard clock would print a target
           * she is not being measured against.
           */
          timeGoalMinutes={assignment.time_goal_minutes?.[accommodation] ?? null}
          sourceSection={assignment.source_section}
          studentId={assignment.student_id}
          /*
           * Two gates, and they guard different things.
           *
           * `showHistory` keeps the block off a page Joshua is reading over a
           * student's shoulder, where "your last three runs" is not addressed to
           * the person looking at it. The `studentId` above is the one that makes
           * the READ safe: the admin SELECT policy on the grades table returns
           * every student's rows, so a flag here could never have been enough on
           * its own.
           */
          showHistory={!practice && !viewingSomeoneElse}
=====ActivityRunner 780-840=====
   */
  if (assignment.item_format === "spoken-recall") {
    if (!user) return null;
    /*
     * Section mode is its own player, not a mode flag on the v1 one.
     *
     * The v1 runner's unit is the item and it judges each chunk as she goes; a
     * section's unit is the whole section and nothing may be judged until it
     * ends (ADR 0010, spec section 4). Those are different machines, so the
     * branch is here rather than inside RecallRunner, where the forbidden
     * feedback phase would still exist and have to be remembered around.
     *
     * `mode` comes off the assignment's own snapshot, not the Activity: the
     * clock is what her time is scored against, so a later edit to the
     * definition must not be able to rescore a run she has finished.
     */
    if (assignment.mode === "section") {
      /*
       * A finished section goes to its Report Card, not back to the intro.
       * Grading is a post-section job, so the card polls for a result that is
       * still being produced rather than being handed one.
       */
      if (phase === "report") {
        return (
          <div
            className="activity-centre rc-ground ac-section-handover min-h-screen px-4 py-12"
            data-runner="recall-section-report"
          >
            <SectionReport assignmentId={assignment.id} onDone={handleLeave ?? handleBackToIntro} />
          </div>
        );
      }
      return (
        <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen" data-runner="recall-section">
          <SectionRunner
            assignmentId={assignment.id}
            studentId={user.id}
            items={assignment.items}
            sourceSection={assignment.source_section}
            /*
             * The section builder needs the complete set of goals to validate
             * and build the run. `accommodation` tells it which one applies to
             * this student when the clock and grade are calculated.
             */
            timeGoalMinutes={assignment.time_goal_minutes}
            accommodation={accommodation}
            onComplete={() => setPhase("report")}
            onExit={handleBackToIntro}
          />
        </div>
      );
    }

import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import {
  listMySectionGrades,
  listSectionChoices,
  startSectionAttempt,
  type SectionChoice,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { ActivityAssignmentSummary, SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";

/**
 * The sections she can run, and what she has already done.
 *
 * Read, Recall, Colour is one Activity now. Joshua assigns the drill; she picks
 * which section to work tonight, and she works several. That is why this list
 * exists at all: a section per library card meant he chose for her, and it grew
 * by one card every time a section was built.
 *
 * Two rules the drill puts on this screen:
 *
 * - **Nothing is hidden once done.** The regular section picker drops a section
 *   she has sat, which is right when there are two hundred of them and wrong
 *   here, where there are three. Repeating a section is legitimate: she is
 *   training recall, not measuring a score.
 * - **Every Attempt is kept.** Not a count, not the latest. Attempt 1, 2 and 3
 *   each keep their own date and their own Report Card, because the point of
 *   running a section twice is seeing what changed.
 *
 * Starting is a server call, not an insert. She chooses the section; the row
 * that holds her run is built under definer rights, because the snapshot
 * carries the Details her Coverage is scored against and must never be hers to
 * write.
 */
export interface SectionLibraryProps {
  /** Her assignments, used to show what she has already attempted. */
  attempts: ActivityAssignmentSummary[];
  /** Called with the new assignment id once an Attempt has been created. */
  onStart: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /**
   * Called with the assignment of an Attempt she wants to look at again.
   *
   * Its own Report Card is what opens: a completed section routes there rather
   * than to the generic summary, from any device and at any later date.
   */
  onOpenAttempt?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /** Injected in tests. */
  loadChoices?: () => Promise<SectionChoice[]>;
  loadGrades?: () => Promise<SectionGradeStub[]>;
  beginAttempt?: (activityId: string) => Promise<string>;
}

function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
}

/**
 * A section she was handed and has not opened.
 *
 * Not an Attempt: nothing was recorded, nothing was answered, and calling it
 * "Attempt 1 - not finished" tells her she abandoned a run she never began.
 * `started_at` is the honest test, because a teacher-assigned row and a
 * server-made one both arrive as rows and only one of them has a clock.
 */
function isWaiting(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): boolean {
  return a.status === "assigned" && !a.started_at;
}

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.
 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.
 */
function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;

=== ADR 0031 summary ===
# 0031 — Step two is the Retelling, and she is told to tell it back

Date: 2026-09-04
Status: accepted

Step 2 of Read, Recall, Colour was called **Reading Recall** in the docs and
labelled **"Say it back"** in the product, which meant two names for one step and
the word "Recall" doing duty on two different steps — step 2 and **The Recall**,
which is step 4 and the whole point of the drill. ADR 0012 already had to rename
step 4 away from "The Read" for colliding with step 1; this is the same collision
one step over, and it survived because the docs and the authored copy were fixed
in different places at different times.

Step 2 is now **the Retelling** internally and in code, and **Tell it back**
everywhere a student reads it. "Recall" is left to mean step 4 and nothing else,
and the two names are now one word apart rather than unrelated. The verb was
already in use where it mattered most: the coach rubric in
`read-recall-colour.json` judged "the retelling" before this ADR existed.

## Consequences

The authored student copy changed, so a student mid-course sees "Tell it back"
where she saw "Say it back". This is the cheapest moment it will ever be: the
section runner is not in the seed library yet (ADR 0009).

ADRs 0009, 0012, 0013, 0018, 0022, 0024 and the two RRC specs still say "Reading
Recall" in their prose. They are records of what was decided when, and are left
alone. Read "Reading Recall" in anything dated before today as the Retelling.

Work efficiently: batch browser actions with mcp__Claude_Browser__browser_batch; keep tool calls few.\n\n## What the interface does\nRRC is a spoken LSAT drill. A student opens a door page, picks a section (22 to 26 Logical Reasoning stimuli), reads a briefing, presses Start, then runs the whole section in one recorded take against a 25-minute goal: for each stimulus she reads it in pieces, tells each piece back in her own words, \"colours it\" (says her own reaction), then presses Space, the text vanishes and she recalls the whole argument from memory, then Space for the next one. Nothing is judged live. Afterwards an AI grader marks it and she gets a Report Card: what she carried, what she dropped, her recording beside the standard, line by line, and what to do next time. Students are paying one-to-one coaching clients of Joshua (sole instructor), mostly on laptops, 25-minute sittings, often at night. <result>## Assessment B: Automated Detection, RRC drill

Raw JSON saved in the scratchpad: `impeccable-rrc-files.json` (14 TSX files), `impeccable-rrc.json` (directory scan, 183 findings), `impeccable-rrc-noconfig.json`, `impeccable-rrc-url1..4.json` (Puppeteer URL scans). Summariser: `summarise-rrc.mjs`, `list.mjs`.

### (c) Tooling problems, precisely

1. **`impeccable live` in 4.1.0 is not an overlay server.** It is a "live variant mode" that requires `.impeccable/live/config.json`. Running it returned `{"ok":false,"error":"config_missing", ...}` and exited 0 without starting any server. The only file in `.impeccable/live/` is `roots.json`. There is no `detect.js` endpoint; creating the config would mean editing a project file, so I did not. **No browser overlay was produced and no `[Human]` tabs were opened.** Nothing was left running.
2. **`detect` does accept URL targets** (Puppeteer, viewport 1280x800) and worked against the dev server. This replaced the overlay. Limitation: it cannot click the "section" tab on `/activity-lab`, so that scan covers the default lab tab (Switchyard world), not the RRC runner.
3. **`section-launch.css` is listed in `.impeccable/config.json` `detector.ignoreFiles`**, so the directory scan produced zero findings for it. `--no-config` lifts the ignore but also drops DESIGN.md rules; under `--no-config` the file produced zero slop-rule findings. Running from a temp cwd with a trimmed config did not resolve linked CSS (CSS resolution is cwd-relative), so design-system findings for that file could not be produced without editing project config. The URL scans of the two launch pages cover it in rendered form instead.
4. Config `ignoreValues` already waive Fraunces (`overused-font`) in `recall/section.css`, `#050102`, `0.72rem`, `1.15rem`, `hsl(30 80% 72%)`, and the 10/11/12px `.sl` micro-labels, all with recorded reasons. With `--no-config` the waived RRC findings are 3x `overused-font` Fraunces (section.css:183, 507, 635) and the same `side-tab`.

### (a) CLI findings

**14-file TSX scan:** 19 findings, all `design-system-font-size` (advisory), all in `components/SectionReportCard.tsx`: lines 342 (28px), 495 (40px and 52px), 549 (28px), 689 (26px), and 15px at 511, 744, 752, 764, 786, 795, 823, 879, 899, 1035, 1045, 1061, 1070, 1247. Call: **likely false positive**. The card `.rc` is a sanctioned scoped world; the 40/52px band display and 15px reading body are its own ramp. Note, though, that these are not waived in config the way `.sl` and `section.css` are, so they will keep resurfacing until a waiver with a reason is recorded.

**Directory scan (RRC-relevant subset of 183):**

| Rule | File | Lines | Call |
|---|---|---|---|
| side-tab (warning) | report-card.css | 278 `.rc__line::before` 3px stripe, left:0 | **False positive.** It is the playhead: opacity 0 by default, 2px radius, only shown on the active line. Not a decorative accent border. |
| design-system-font-size (adv) | report-card.css | 147 (9px), 312 (15px) | 9px mono label at :147 is a **true positive**: below the 10px floor the `.sl` waiver argued for, and the URL scan confirms 10px labels are already at the edge. 15px body: false positive (scoped). |
| design-system-font-size (adv) | recall/section.css | 508 (26px), 636 (22px), 753 (0.68rem), 788 (0.66rem), 799 (0.78rem) | 26/22px: false positive (LawHub mirror). 0.68rem and 0.66rem (~10.6px, ~10.9px) uppercase coach-step labels: **true positive**, below the 11px floor. |
| design-system-font-size (adv) | prompt-format.tsx | 43 (32px) | False positive; shared helper, not RRC-specific. |
| design-system-radius (adv) | report-card.css | 161 (3px), 333 (5px), 436 (10px) | False positive (scoped). |
| design-system-radius (adv) | recall/section.css | 796 (0.3rem) | False positive. |
| design-system-color (adv) | report-card.css | 452 `oklch(0.925 0.014 312)` `.rc-ground` | False positive; the card's ground token. |
| design-system-color (adv) | recall/section.css | 47, 68, 220, 398 (#e2e8f0); 153, 481 (#cbd5e1); 244, 517, 645 (#64748b); 445, 477 (#475569); 523 (#94a3b8); 365 `oklch(0.58 0.2 27)`; 423 `rgb(10 8 24/.62)`; 433 `rgb(0 0 0/.45)` | The slate hexes are the LawHub mirror set and mostly false positives, but 13 of them are **not waived** while sibling values (#f1f5f9, #dbeafe) are. The record-pulse red at :365 and the overlay scrim at :423 are true positives for hex/oklch literals that could be tokens. |

Not surfaced by the CLI (outside its rules):
- **Em dashes in student-facing copy:** `SectionProgress.tsx:48` ("They are saved — try again in a moment."), `SectionProgress.tsx:68` ("is saved here — every run..."), `SectionReportCard.tsx:1273` ("Try again in a moment — nothing has been lost."). **True positives.** The test files assert no em dash in rendered body text, but these three lines are error/empty states the tests do not render.
- Hex literals in components: only `MeadowScene.tsx` (lines 64-65 palette arrays, 135-199 SVG gradient stops). Expected for an SVG scene; no other RRC TSX has hex literals.
- No `border-left` &gt; 1px, no gradient text (`background-clip: text`), in section-launch.css, report-card.css, or section.css.

### (b) URL scan findings (Puppeteer, in place of the overlay)

**1. `section-launch-preview.html?state=library` (18):** undersized-ui-text x4 ("01 · Read it" 10.5px; "Stimulus", "Out loud", "Demonstration. The stimulus and the tran..." 10px); cramped-padding (1px vertical for 16.5px text); all-caps-body (73 chars uppercase); hero-eyebrow-chip (`.sl-chip` "Read, Recall, Colour" directly above h1 "Read, Recall, Colour", SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:279-283); gpt-thin-border-wide-shadow (1px border + 60px blur, advisory); low-contrast x5: #ffffff on #a89fe0 2.4:1 and #ffe8c8 (`--rd-apricot-light`) on #a89fe0 2.0:1 (the bottom stop of `.rd-sc-tell` at section-launch.css:843 and `.rd-close-panel` at :1575), plus pixel-contrast on the SVG-underlay hero copy (1.6:1 on the quote, 1.1:1 median 2.5:1 on "Read it, tell it back..."); tight-leading 1.04 (`.rd-close-line` :1595, display size, arguable); overused-font Inter 40%; skipped-heading h1 -&gt; h3 (`.sl-dr-title` at SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:429 precedes the first h2 at :502); layout-transition (padding, margin); shape-assembled-illustration (MeadowScene, 30 primitives, advisory).

**2. `?state=runs` (22):** same 14 as above plus undersized-ui-text x4 more ("Date", "Time", "Recalled", "Stimulus by stimulus" at 10.5px, the run-history table headers) and a second all-caps-body (34 chars). The quote-underlay contrast finding did not recur.

**3. `/activity-lab` (16, default tab, NOT the RRC runner):** low-contrast x3 (#ffffff on #0d9668, 3.8:1), radial-spotlight-glow x2, dark-glow x6 (#fba82d), gpt-thin-border-wide-shadow x4, layout-transition (height). All belong to the Switchyard world; not RRC evidence.

**4. `report-card-preview.html?state=faults&amp;frame=runner` (34):** undersized-ui-text x15, every one a 10px label ("Stimulus 1 of 22", "The passage", "What you held", "What slipped", "Everything else that slipped (1)", "Your Read", "Line by line", "Held" x2, "Dropped", "Done right" x2, "What you said", "Next time", "1 of 22"); line-length x10 (89, 100, 94, 98, 93, 94, 93, 93, 117, 93 chars/line; `.rc__measure` caps at 72ch at report-card.css:70, so some column is escaping it); all-caps-body (32 chars); nested-cards x4; side-tab (`.rc__line::before`, false positive as above); layout-transition (padding, margin); gpt-thin-border-wide-shadow (1px + 32px blur, advisory); dark-glow (#e8d08b zero-offset shadow).

### (d) Most consequential true positives

1. **Contrast on the launch door's dark-to-lilac panels.** White and apricot text over the `#a89fe0` bottom gradient stop measures 2.4:1 and 2.0:1 (need 3:1), and the hero copy over the meadow SVG hits 1.1 to 1.6:1 median. section-launch.css:843, :1575, MeadowScene.tsx:138. Verified in the rendered scan, not just static.
2. **Report card body running 89 to 117 characters per line** despite the 72ch `.rc__measure` cap (report-card.css:70). Some transcript/passage column is not inside the measure. Ten instances on one page.
3. **Sub-11px functional labels everywhere the drill is read.** 15 instances at 10px on the report card, 4 to 8 on the launch pages (10.5px), and the 9px `.rc` mono label at report-card.css:147 plus 0.66/0.68rem coach-step labels in section.css:753, 788. The `.sl` waiver argued for 10 to 12px; the rendered result is at or below the floor throughout.
4. **Em dashes in three student-facing strings** (SectionProgress.tsx:48, :68; SectionReportCard.tsx:1273), in exactly the error/empty states the no-em-dash tests never render.
5. **Heading structure on the door: h1 -&gt; h3 with no h2**, and the eyebrow chip repeating the h1 text verbatim (SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:279-283, 429, 502). Screen-reader outline and a slop tell in one spot.

<result>All measurements are in and the viewport preset is back on desktop. Here is the audit.

# Technical Audit: RRC Report Card, wait screen, run history

Scope: `SectionReportCard.tsx` (1278 lines), `report-card.css` (474), `SectionReport.tsx`, `SectionRunHistory.tsx`, `section.css` from line 590, harness at `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html`, lab tabs "report" and "runs". Every figure below is tagged **[measured]** (browser, canvas-converted oklch, on the actual rendered ground) or **[code]** (read from source, computed by hand where a number is given). Limitation: the browser pane returned black frames for any scrolled screenshot under viewport emulation, so below-the-fold checks are DOM-measured, not visually confirmed.

## Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Accessibility | 2 | No heading in the card; ledger ticks expose no band; targets under 44px on 8 of 9 control types; run-history band colours at 3.79:1 and 3.85:1 |
| 2 | Performance | 3 | One stimulus rendered at a time (223 elements for 22 stimuli); no layout-property transitions; playhead tick re-renders the whole card 4x/s |
| 3 | Responsive | 3 | No overflow at 375 or 768; ledger wraps to 3 rows; ticks 29px wide at 375 |
| 4 | Theming | 3 | Sanctioned `.rc` world is clean; literal hsl/hex outside the token block in `SectionRunHistory.tsx` and `section.css` |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | 3 | One absolute-ban match (`border-l-2` signal stripe on the next-step line); nested bordered boxes inside the card |
| **Total** | | **14/20** | **Good (address weak dimensions)** |

## Anti-Patterns Verdict

**Pass, with two specific hits.** The surface does not read as AI-generated: violet-tinted neutrals, band-by-fill ledger, Fraunces at weight 380, prose figures. Specific verdicts requested:

- **Console header (hero-metric block?)**: Borderline, not a fail. Structurally it is band headline + sub-line + three label/number/note figures (`SectionReportCard.tsx:495-598`), which is the shape of the template. It escapes the cliché because the band leads and the number follows (ADR 0018), each figure carries a qualifying sentence rather than a delta badge, and there is no gradient or card per figure. Keep as is.
- **Ledger**: Clean. Band is carried by fill amount and shape (solid, 66%, 30%, hollow ring, dashed ring, dotted), survives grayscale [code `report-card.css:159-210`, measured mark contrasts 5.97 to 9.71:1].
- **Category chips**: Clean. Held and Dropped differ by inset border and weight and are each preceded by a text label (`:965-981`). Not colour-alone.
- **Line-by-line pairing**: Clean in structure (stacked, not columns), but the "Done right" box per line is a bordered, rounded, filled container inside the `.rc__line` inside the card (`:987`). That is a nested card by the shared design laws.
- **Absolute ban hit**: `SectionReportCard.tsx:511` `border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)] pl-4` on the next-step sentence. A 2px coloured left border on a callout is the side-stripe pattern verbatim.

## Executive Summary

- Audit Health Score: **14/20** (Good)
- Issues: **P0: 0, P1: 5, P2: 9, P3: 6**
- Top issues:
  1. Focus-set chips name the wrong stimulus: `(s.item_order ?? i) + 1` at `:1257` adds 1 to a 1-based `item_order` (authored `order: 1` in `content/read-recall-colour-pt102-s3.json:16`; ledger and header at `:668, :687` use it unshifted). "Stimulus 5" in the panel is stimulus 4 everywhere else.
  2. Run-history band colours fail AA: 3.79:1 and 3.85:1 on the lab's cream ground, computed 4.24:1 and 4.31:1 on Workroom `#fcfcfc` where `TeacherStudentDetail.tsx:368` renders it.
  3. Interactive targets under the product's 44px floor on ticks (30x45 at 1180, 29x45 at 375), transport (34px), Hear it / Previous / Next (26px), `&lt;summary&gt;` rows (16px), dispute link (16px).
  4. Side-stripe ban at `:511`.
  5. Card has zero headings and no named landmark; the 52px band label is a `&lt;p&gt;`.
- Next steps: fix the off-by-one, replace the run-history literals with AA tokens, lift targets, restructure the next-step line, add a heading outline.

## Detailed Findings by Severity

### P1

**[P1] Focus set chips are off by one on every real grade**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:1257`
- Category: Accessibility / Truthfulness (Product Principle 1)
- Impact: The panel that asks her to redo specific stimuli names the wrong ones. `item_order` is 1-based (`content/read-recall-colour-pt102-s3.json:16`, `SectionRunner.tsx:628` passes `r.order` through unchanged); the ledger (`:671`) and header (`:687`) print it directly, the panel adds 1. [code; the harness and lab never render `FocusSetPanel` because neither passes `onStartFocusSet`, so this is unrendered]
- WCAG: n/a (correctness)
- Recommendation: `Stimulus {s.item_order ?? i + 1}` to match `:668`.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P1] Run-history "Held" column fails text contrast, and carries the band by colour alone**
- Location: `SectionRunHistory.tsx:48-53` (`hsl(38_92%_34%)`, `hsl(24_85%_42%)`, `hsl(350_80%_45%)`), applied at `:144`
- Category: Accessibility / Theming
- Impact: [measured, lab ground rgb(244,239,231)] thin `rgb(198,89,16)` = **3.79:1**, nearly-there `rgb(166,108,7)` = **3.85:1**, emerald 5.12:1. [computed] On the Workroom page `#fcfcfc` where it ships: 4.24:1 and 4.31:1. All at 14px, so no large-text exemption. The band has no text label in the row; the number is the same in all four colours.
- WCAG: 1.4.3 (AA), 1.4.1
- Recommendation: Use the Activity Centre's tokened inks (`--ac-emerald-strong` is already a token; add darkened amber and rust equivalents measured at 4.5:1 on both grounds), and add a band word or sr-only text beside the percentage.

**[P1] Touch and pointer targets below the product's 44px floor**
- Location: `report-card.css:119-131` (`.rc__tick` min-height 44 but width from `minmax(28px,1fr)`), `:229-239` (`.rc__source` 34px), `:356-372` (`.rc__hear` 26px), `SectionReportCard.tsx:743, :804` (`&lt;summary&gt;`), `:1185-1192` (dispute button)
- Category: Responsive / Accessibility
- Impact: [measured at 1180] ticks 30.1x45.4 with 4px gap; Hear it back 101x34; The passage summary 665x16; Everything else summary 699x16; Hear it 58x26 (x2); This is not what I said 127x16; Previous 67x26; Next 47x26. [measured at 375] ticks 29x45, otherwise identical heights. WCAG 2.5.8 AA (24px with spacing) passes; PRODUCT.md "Accessibility &amp; Inclusion" sets 44x44 as its own binding floor, "by being that size".
- WCAG: 2.5.5 (AAA), project floor
- Recommendation: `min-width: 44px` on ticks (ledger becomes two rows at 820px, which the 375 layout already does), `min-height: 44px` on `.rc__source`, `.rc__hear`, the summaries and the dispute button, using padding rather than font size.

**[P1] Side-stripe callout on the next-step sentence**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:511`
- Category: Anti-Pattern
- Impact: Absolute-ban match in the impeccable laws ("border-left greater than 1px as a colored accent on callouts"). [measured] signal on console is 2.94:1, so it is decorative rather than a boundary.
- Recommendation: Drop the border; let the sentence carry weight by size (it is already 15px on a 14px sub-line) or set it in the display face. If a mark is wanted, a leading glyph or a `rc__label` above it.

**[P1] Handover subtitle ink fails contrast**
- Location: `section.css:645` `.ac-section-finished span { color: #64748b }` on the `is-finishing` ground `hsl(38 36% 93%)` (`:612`)
- Category: Accessibility / Theming
- Impact: [computed, not browser-measured] slate-500 on rgb(244,239,231) = **4.17:1** at 14px. This is the sentence she reads at the end of 25 minutes. Also an untokened hex inside a world that tokens everything else as `--sec-*`.
- WCAG: 1.4.3
- Recommendation: `var(--sec-chrome)` or another `--sec-*` ink measured at 4.5:1 on the warmed ground.
- Suggested: `$impeccable colorize`

### P2

**[P2] No heading outline, no named landmark**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:489-497` (`&lt;header&gt;` inside a `div`, band label is `&lt;p&gt;`), `:368` (`&lt;section&gt;` without accessible name), `:689`
- Category: Accessibility
- Impact: [measured] `h1..h6` count is 0; `&lt;header&gt;` is not top-level so it is not a banner; six unnamed `&lt;section&gt;` elements are not landmarks. A screen-reader user has no outline of a page with seven movements per stimulus.
- WCAG: 1.3.1, 2.4.6 (best practice)
- Recommendation: `h1` for the band headline, `h2` for "Stimulus N of 22", `h3` on each Movement label (`:369`), `aria-labelledby` on the sections.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P2] Ledger ticks expose no band or state to assistive tech**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:659-673`
- Category: Accessibility
- Impact: [measured] accessible name is "Stimulus N" only; the mark is `aria-hidden`; `aria-current="false"` is emitted on the other 21. The one piece of information the ledger exists to show (band, recited, ungraded) is invisible non-visually.
- WCAG: 1.1.1, 4.1.2
- Recommendation: extend the sr-only span: "Stimulus 4, nearly there" / "said back word for word" / "not marked"; emit `aria-current` only when true.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P2] Polling screen has no live region, no re-ask, and slow and dead runs look identical**
- Location: `SectionReport.tsx:64-91, :114-137`
- Category: Accessibility / Harden
- Impact: [code] 0s: "Marking your run" + "This takes a minute" + outline Done (`:121, :126, :130`). 30s: unchanged, no elapsed indicator. 120s: silent second grading request (`:79-85`). 180s: "Still being marked" + "Your run is saved and your Report Card will be here when you come back to it", polling stops (`:86-89`), Done becomes the primary button. No `aria-live`/`role=status` anywhere in the file, so the text swap and the arrival of the card are not announced (PRODUCT.md requires live updates to be announced). A grade that will never arrive (grader threw, auth expired so every `getSectionGrade` throws at `:72`) renders the same copy as a slow one; ADR 0019 makes absence the pending signal, so there is nothing to distinguish them, and the only exit is Done. No manual "ask again". Polling ignores `visibilitychange`.
- WCAG: 4.1.3
- Recommendation: `role="status"` on the copy block; after give-up, offer "Check again" that restarts the poll; consider surfacing the sweep's own state if one exists so a stuck run reads differently after the sweep window.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P2] `role="status"` elements are mounted conditionally**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:728, :1170, :1178, :1272`
- Category: Accessibility
- Impact: [code] Each status paragraph is inserted into the DOM with its text already present. Most screen readers only announce changes inside a pre-existing live region, so "Noted", "That did not save", "Following your recording" are likely silent.
- WCAG: 4.1.3
- Recommendation: keep one always-mounted `role="status"` container per site and swap its text.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P2] Nested bordered boxes inside the card**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:318` (Walkthrough), `:742` (passage `&lt;details&gt;`), `:987` ("Done right" per line), `:1004` (recall verdict box)
- Category: Anti-Pattern

- Recommendation: replace inner boxes with the existing `rc__panel` tone fill or a top hairline plus label; the card's own hairlines already do the separation.
- Suggested: `$impeccable distill`

**[P2] Raw grader enum rendered as student copy**

- Category: Copy

- Recommendation: map through a table the way `BAND` does (`:56-61`).
- Suggested: `$impeccable clarify`

**[P2] Vocabulary conflict: "run" and "try" in student copy**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:654` "The run", `:1226` "in this run", `:1251, :1264` "Run these N again", `:1267` "This run", `SectionReport.tsx:121` "Marking your run", `:125` "Your run is saved", `SectionRunHistory.tsx:79, :112` "sections run"; `SectionReportCard.tsx:1273` "Try again in a moment"
- Category: Copy
- Impact: CONTEXT.md "Attempt" lists run, try, retry, session as avoided. [measured] none of scorecard, verdict, results, grade, summary, paraphrase, parrot, shopping list, retry, session, failed, error, zero appear in rendered faults or clean text; "run" appears on every state; "Try" is in the unrendered failure branch. The header itself says "Attempt 2" (`:491`), so both words coexist on one screen.
- Recommendation: decide whether "run" is now sanctioned (then update CONTEXT.md) or replace with "this Attempt" / "the section".
- Suggested: `$impeccable clarify`

**[P2] Em dash in the focus-set failure copy**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:1273` "Try again in a moment — nothing has been lost."
- Category: Copy
- Impact: [measured] 0 U+2014 and 0 " -- " in faults, clean and lab rendered text; [code] one U+2014 in the unrendered failure branch. Global rule: no em dashes.
- Recommendation: "Try again in a moment. Nothing has been lost." (and see the "try" note above).
- Suggested: `$impeccable clarify`

**[P2] Control boundaries below 3:1 on the transport and pill buttons**
- Location: `report-card.css:220-227` (transport raised on console), `:356-359` (`.rc__hear` hairline border)
- Category: Accessibility
- Impact: [measured] transport pill vs console 1.25:1; `.rc__hear` border vs page 1.28:1. WCAG 1.4.11 exempts controls identified by their text (text passes at 5.63:1 and 7.33:1), but PRODUCT.md asks for 3:1 on "the visual boundaries of interactive controls".
- WCAG: 1.4.11 (exempt), project rule
- Recommendation: border `var(--rc-ink-faint)` on `.rc__hear` (5.63:1 available), raise the transport two more steps or drop the pill.
- Suggested: `$impeccable polish`

### P3

**[P3] Stop affordance lost when navigating while a clip plays**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:466, :710-713`
- Category: Harden
- Impact: [code] `key` is `s${openStimulus}`; moving to another stimulus while hers plays keeps the audio going and shows "Hear it back" instead of "Stop", since `clip.playing` still equals the old key. A second press seeks the same element rather than stopping.
- Recommendation: stop on stimulus change, or key the transport state on the file rather than the index.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P3] Whole-card re-render on every `timeupdate`, unmemoised derivations**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:138-139` (`setPositionMs` each tick), `:470` (`walkthroughPairs`), `:474` (`whatHeld`)
- Category: Performance
- Impact: [code] `timeupdate` fires about 4x/s; each tick re-renders the console, 22 ticks and the open stimulus, and re-parses the walkthrough markdown. [measured] 223 elements at 22 stimuli, 178 in clean state, 2 `.rc__line` nodes (only the open stimulus renders), 0 `&lt;audio&gt;` elements in the DOM (one `new Audio()` created lazily). No `backdrop-filter`, no `will-change`, no layout-property transitions (background-color, color, opacity, transform only). Cost is small; the fix is cheap.
- Recommendation: `useMemo` on `pairs` and `held` keyed by `s`; isolate the playhead into the line-by-line subtree.
- Suggested: `$impeccable optimize`

**[P3] Literal colours outside the token block**
- Location: `report-card.css:452` (`.rc-ground` oklch, documented), `SectionReportCard.tsx:487` (two rgba shadows), `SectionReport.tsx:116` (rgba shadow), `SectionRunHistory.tsx:50-52, :189` (four hsl literals), `section.css:612, :623, :645`
- Category: Theming
- Impact: The `.rc` world itself is clean: every colour inside it resolves from `--rc-*`. The literals are on the edges. [measured] `frame=runner` ground oklch(0.925 0.014 312) vs card page 0.979 = 1.18:1, one honest step; compound `.activity-centre.rc-ground` wins over the cream. No `.dark` or `prefers-color-scheme` handling exists in `report-card.css` or `activity-centre.css`, so the card is identical in dark mode, which is acceptable for a scoped light world (`color-scheme: light` at `activity-centre.css:13`).
- Recommendation: tokenise the run-history inks (see P1) and the shadows.
- Suggested: `$impeccable extract`

**[P3] shadcn `Button` inside the `.rc` world takes Activity Centre colours**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:1112, :1263`
- Category: Theming
- Impact: [code, not rendered in harness or lab] "Done" and "Run these N again" resolve `bg-primary` and the `.activity-centre` emerald ring from the corridor, not from `--rc-*`. A palette leak into the one world that explicitly bans emerald.
- Recommendation: an `rc__button` styled from `--rc-signal` / `--rc-ink`.
- Suggested: `$impeccable polish`

**[P3] Tab order walks all 22 ticks before any content**
- Location: `SectionReportCard.tsx:656-675`
- Category: Accessibility
- Impact: [measured] order is 22 ticks, Hear it back, The passage, Everything else, Hear it x2, This is not what I said, Next (Previous disabled). No trap; focus rings visible and measured (signal-bright ring 5.97:1 on console, signal ring 5.29:1 on page; the `.rc :focus-visible` box-shadow overrides both the emerald module ring and the tick's own outline). Reaching Next from the top is 29 Tabs. Note: the browser tool's synthetic Enter did not activate a focused tick, while a JS `click()` did; a native `&lt;button type="button"&gt;` activates on Enter in a real browser, so this is reported as unverified, not as a defect.
- Recommendation: roving tabindex on the ledger (arrow keys between ticks, one tab stop) so the row is one stop.
- Suggested: `$impeccable harden`

**[P3] Playhead bar is visually a side stripe**
- Location: `report-card.css:278-306`
- Category: Anti-Pattern (judgement call)
- Impact: A 3px signal-coloured bar on the left edge of the playing line. It is functional (it appears only while playing, and moves), so it is not the decorative ban; noted because it is the same visual as the banned pattern one screen up.
- Recommendation: leave, or carry the playing state by the `--rc-signal-soft` fill alone (already present).
- Suggested: none

## Patterns and Systemic Issues

- **Height is set, width is not, and vice versa.** Ticks meet 44 tall and miss wide; pills meet wide and miss tall. The floor is treated per axis.
- **Status is inserted rather than updated.** Four `role="status"` nodes mount with their text; the wait screen has none. One pattern would fix all five.
- **The world is clean, its neighbours are not.** Every contrast pair inside `.rc` clears 4.5:1 [measured: lowest 5.63:1 for `--rc-ink-faint` 10px labels on page; 6.07:1 for `--rc-console-ink-faint` labels and 9px tick digits on console; chips 5.94 and 6.16 on their soft fields; playing line 5.23 to 14.67 on `--rc-signal-soft`; clean state lowest 5.63]. The failures are in the two files that share the vocabulary but not the tokens: `SectionRunHistory.tsx` and the `section.css` handover.
- **Two numbering conventions for `item_order`.** Three sites read it directly, one adds 1.
- **"Attempt" in the schema, "run" on the screen.** Ten student-facing uses across three files.

## Positive Findings

- Contrast inside the card is measured, documented and true: the surface brief's claim that every text node clears 4.5:1 holds in faults, playing and clean states [measured independently].
- Band is fill, not hue; recital is dashed, ungraded is dotted, lost is a hollow ring: the ledger survives grayscale and category owns colour.
- Only the open stimulus renders; 22 stimuli cost 223 elements and one lazily created audio element.
- Reduced motion is fully covered: `report-card.css:392-399` blankets every `.rc` transition and animation; `section.css:662-672` covers `.is-finishing` and `:697-701` covers `.ac-section-handover` [code].
- No layout-property transitions anywhere; exponential ease, no bounce.
- Responsive holds: no horizontal overflow at 375 or 768 [measured]; figures stack to one column at 375 and three at 768; ledger wraps to 3 rows at 375 and 2 at 768; measure is 699px at 1180 (under 72ch), 293px at 375; Hear it stays beside each line; hers-then-standard is stacked by design so nothing has to reflow.
- The focus ring override for the emerald module ring is correct and compounded for specificity (`report-card.css:460-474`).
- Held and Dropped chips are distinguished by shape, weight and a text label, never colour alone.
- Ungraded stimuli are excluded from tallies and named as "ours, not yours" (`SectionRunHistory.tsx:94`, `SectionReportCard.tsx:753`).

## Recommended Actions

1. **[P1] `$impeccable harden`**: fix `:1257` off-by-one; add heading outline, tick band names, an always-mounted status region on the card and on `SectionReport.tsx`; a "Check again" after give-up.
2. **[P1] `$impeccable colorize`**: tokenise and re-measure the run-history band inks and `section.css:645` at 4.5:1 on both cream and `#fcfcfc`.
3. **[P1] `$impeccable adapt`**: 44px on ticks (width), transport, pills, summaries and the dispute control.
4. **[P1] `$impeccable distill`**: remove the `border-l-2` stripe at `:511`; flatten the four inner bordered boxes.
5. **[P2] `$impeccable clarify`**: map `recall.verdict` to words; settle "run" vs "Attempt"; drop the em dash and "Try" at `:1273`.
6. **[P3] `$impeccable optimize`**: memoise `pairs` and `held`, isolate the playhead re-render.
7. **[P3] `$impeccable polish`**: `.rc`-styled buttons, 3:1 control borders, stop-on-navigate.

Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see the score move; the five P1 items alone would lift Accessibility to 3 and Anti-Patterns to 4.</result>
