<result>## Assessment A: LLM design review of Read, Recall, Colour as one journey

Method note first. I read the critique framework, PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, the two locked briefs, CONTEXT.md, and all listed source files. In the browser (my own tab, `tab-7`, labelled `[LLM]`) I rendered the door in `library`, `runs` and `failed` states, the runner in Running, Recall, Ready Beat, Paused, Finishing and Leave-dialog moments, and the Report Card in `faults`, `clean` and `partial&amp;playing=2`. The pane's capture tool returned blank frames for most scrolled positions, so mid-page visuals of the door and the runner's finishing screen were verified from computed styles and DOM text rather than pixels. The briefing page has no harness and was reviewed from code. Back-button behaviour and phone layout were not exercised.

---

### AI slop verdict

**Door (Persuade register): not first-order slop, but caught by the second-order reflex.** Nobody would guess "LSAT drill → lavender dusk over a painted meadow" from the category. But "ed-tech landing that is not SaaS-cream, so: editorial serif with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, pastel sky gradient, a product-demo card floating in the hero, a closing italic line above a pill button" is exactly the heylemon/fora lane the brief names, and that lane is the saturated one right now. Specific tells, by element:

- `.sl-chip` "Read, Recall, Colour" sits directly above `h1.sl-title` "Read, Recall, Colour". A restated heading, the copy law's named failure.
- `.sl-beats`: four same-sized cards, small drawn scene + "Step N" + heading + text. The identical-card grid. The tablist semantics are real and earn some of it; the composition does not.
- `.rd-counts-grid`: three same-sized quote cards (Retelling / Reciting / Listing). Same pattern again one screen later.
- `.rd-close-line` at 58px italic Fraunces over a pill CTA: the "big italic closer" template.
- The ground is cream, `--rd-cream: #f6f3ea`. The Report Card brief, by the same owner, calls cream "overdone and too AI looking". The journey's front door is built on the colour the journey's last page was forbidden to use. That is a brief-level contradiction, not a code one.

What earns its place: `RecallDemo` (the method performed before it is explained, with a real dropped detail "within two years" and the honest footnote "Demonstration. The stimulus and the transcript are written for this page."), the `MeadowScene` grass crossing in front of the demo card, the runs table with per-stimulus ticks, and the phone notice copy.

**Runner (Operate): clean.** It is a deliberate impersonation of the LawHub section (indigo field `#1a1635`, white paper, Fraunces 19px / 32px leading at 640px measure, 13px chrome). Familiarity is the feature here. Two tells under the surface: `section.css` hard-codes Tailwind slate literals (`#e2e8f0`, `#f1f5f9`, `#64748b`, `#475569`, `#cbd5e1`) against DESIGN.md's Named Ground Rule, and `--sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial` renders as Arial on the Windows laptops these students actually use, so the impersonation is of LawHub on a Mac.

**Report Card (Operate): two banned patterns inside an otherwise well-argued world.** The plum console `oklch(0.245 0.058 312)` with category colours is neither the first-order (ed-tech teal on white) nor the second-order (cream editorial) reflex; good. But `header.rc__console` is the hero-metric template: a 52px display label ("At the standard"), a one-line stat sentence, then three Figures with 10px mono labels (Held 82% / Understood 15 of 22 / Time 28:32). And `SectionReportCard.tsx` ~line 496 renders a conditional console paragraph with `border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)] pl-4`: a side-stripe border, in the magenta the brief reserves for "sound, not a category". Two rule breaks on one element. The Fraunces + Lexend + Plex Mono triad is also the door's recipe with Inter swapped for Lexend; two rooms of one product share a formula.

**Would someone say "AI made this"?** Hero: no. Step-card row and counts row: yes. Runner: no. Card console: probably.

---

### Nielsen heuristics (journey score; per-surface divergence noted)

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 3 | Runner is exemplary (clock, "Recording"/"Paused" in words, rail, live key hint). The marking screen polls silently for 3 minutes then says "Still being marked" with no elapsed time, no ETA beyond "a minute", and the library row it sends her to ("still being marked / Check back shortly") is inert text. The ready beat shows "Recording now" beside a clock reading 0:00 and "Starting in 3". |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 2 | "Read" means step 1 (silent reading) on the door, step 4 on the runner's ready beat ("once when you have given your Read"), and "found the move" on the card ("Your Read", "On 3 you did not give a Read at all"). "The move" is never defined anywhere she reads before the card. |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 2 | A Pause exists and works well, but every pre-run surface says it does not. No resume after refresh or tab close. After the run, "Done" is the only action on the marking screen. |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 1 | Three definitions of Colour, two names for step 4, "silently" vs "out loud in pieces", "No pause button" vs a Pause button, "91% carried" on the door vs "no arithmetic" on the card, "Recalled 73/75" meaning recorded not recalled, four type systems, "Start the section" / "Start now" / "Start" / "Run it again" for one action. |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Leave confirm with the cost named and Keep going as default; mic refusal holds the beat; dead-input detection; phone warning; `beforeunload`. Gaps: the walkthrough coaches the fault the judge penalises (see P0-2), and the recorder opens before the clock starts. |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | The runner footer always says what Space does next. But the card asks her to recognise "a Read", "the move", "coloured", none of which the door taught under those names; the walkthrough retires after three stimuli and there is no way to re-open the method mid-run. |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Space-only is the design and correct. No skip for the 3s beat; the door's method (step cards, counts, close band) is re-read every visit on the section page (only the library is `sectionsFirst`); the card's 22 ledger ticks are 22 tab stops before any content. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist design | 3 | Every element on the door is argued for in comments, but the chip restates the h1, the counts row restates the drawer's "Simpler is not the same as shorter", and the card console is a stat block. Runner is close to perfect here. |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Upload-failed and mic-refused copy is truthful and specific ("still on this computer... Open this section again from the same browser"). The slow or refused grade has no recovery path at all: no notification, no way to tell Joshua, no link back to the marking state. That is the exact shape of the 8-hour incident. |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 3 | Briefing, drawer, walkthrough, "This is not what I said". The help contradicts itself across surfaces. |
| **Total** | | **24/40** | Functional with a consistency problem at the core |

---

### Cognitive load (8-item checklist, failures counted)

**Door, library state: 3 failures (moderate).** Single focus fails: looping demo, chip, crumbs, meta, two CTAs compete in one hero. Minimal choices fails: the slab alone exposes up to four actions per section (two attempt links, "Run it again", plus "Start"), and the page carries 17 buttons including four step tabs, two drawer tabs, Previous/Next. Working memory fails: she leaves carrying three rules (Space twice per stimulus, look away, one take) into a screen that shows one hint. Chunking, grouping, hierarchy, one-thing, progressive disclosure pass.

**Door, section (`runs`) state: 3 failures (moderate).** Same three. Adds four stats and a table 2,300px below the hero Start.

**Briefing page: 1 failure (low).** Working memory only. Note it is the third rendering of the same four steps she meets in two minutes (door cards, briefing list, then the walkthrough card), and the fourth if she opened the drawer.

**Runner: 1 failure (low).** Working memory: she must hold the look-away rule and, during the first three stimuli, reconcile the walkthrough's instructions with the door's. Everything else passes. Decision points: two buttons (Pause, Leave) plus one key. This is the strongest surface.

**Marking screen: 0 failures, but nothing to decide and nothing to learn.**

**Report Card: 4 failures (critical, partly intrinsic).** Single focus fails (console figures, "Worth working on" list, 22-tick ledger, and the open stimulus all in one column). Chunking fails (six movements per stimulus plus three figures plus a five-item list). Minimal choices fails (22 ticks, "Hear it back", per-line "Hear it", "This is not what I said", Previous/Next, "Run these N again", Done). Working memory fails (terms from the card that the door never used). The density of a per-stimulus report is intrinsic; the console's numbers and the undefined terms are extraneous.

---

### Emotional journey (nervous student, 9pm, laptop)

- **Door:** calm and high. Dusk, meadow, "One a day holds you steady." Then, directly above the Start she is about to press, the largest type below the hero: `.rd-close-line` "Twenty-five minutes. No pause button. *Every detail you can still hold.*" at 58px. The anxiety spike is authored, sits at the decision point, and its central claim is false.
- **Briefing:** good interventions. "Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start." and "Allow it." lower the shoulders. The footer repeats "does not pause".
- **Start:** browser mic prompt (spike, unavoidable) then the ready beat: "Recording now" at clock 0:00, a 30-word paragraph, "Starting in 3". Thirty words in a three-second window is unreadable; and "Recording now" while the briefing said "the timer starts with it" is the first moment the product contradicts itself in front of her. This is also the class of the 37-minute incident: the recorder is open before the run is.
- **Running:** the quietest and best part. The Recall phase leaves an entirely blank white paper with only the footer line; deliberate, and it reads as a void. Pause copy is kind ("Your work so far is safe"). Leave copy is honest.
- **Finish (peak-end):** "Section finished. 23 stimuli, end to end. Your Report Card comes next." Then three grounds in ten seconds: `.is-finishing` warms the field to `hsl(38 36% 93%)` (Activity Centre cream, per a SectionRunner comment that still calls the card "warm paper"), the marking screen wears the shell's chrome (`rounded-[28px]`, `--ac-surface`, `ac-display`, shadcn Button), then the plum card lands. At 9:26pm she waits on a static card that says "This takes a minute." If the grade is slow, at 9:29pm it says "Still being marked... will be here when you come back to it" with a button labelled "Done". Done drops her at the library where the row reads "Attempt 1 · Aug 31 · still being marked / Check back shortly" as flat text. No time, no estimate, no notification, no way to tell Joshua. After 25 unbroken minutes the end of the curve is a shrug. In the recorded incident this was the whole ending for eight hours.
- **Card (when it arrives):** "At the standard" in 52px is a good ending sentence. "Next time: Say the quantifier on purpose as you read: all employees, not the staff." is the best line in the journey. But "Held 82%" reintroduces the score the per-stimulus copy carefully refuses, and the `clean` fixture (22 of 22, nothing dropped) gets the identical headline as the `faults` fixture (15 of 22). A perfect run has no distinct high.

---

### What's working

1. **The runner's footer contract.** Left: what she is doing. Right: `&lt;kbd&gt;space&lt;/kbd&gt;` plus what it does, always true for the phase. Verified in the lab: "recall full stimulus" → "next stimulus" → "in a moment" (beat) → "back to it" (paused), and "Recording"/"Paused" in words in the rail. A student never has to remember the one key.
2. **The Leave dialog.** "Stopping now throws away 12:34 of work and the recording with it." names the cost in her own elapsed time; "Keep going" is filled, autofocused, and the default the keyboard lands on; "Leave and lose it" is an outline button. Verified: focus lands on Keep going.
3. **Failure copy that tells the truth about whose fault it is.** Mic refused: "Nothing has started, and your time has not begun." Upload failed: "Your recording did not reach us, so it is still on this computer." Card: "If they are not what you said, the marking is wrong, not you. Play it back and say so." with a "This is not what I said" button. This is the coaching relationship showing through the product.

---

### Priority issues

**[P0] The product says she cannot pause, and then gives her a Pause button.**
What: `.rd-close-line` "No pause button.", `.sl-aside` "you cannot pause the recording", `.sl-bp-fact` "It runs in one take and does not pause.", and `instructions.rules[0]` "it never pauses" all deny it. `SectionStage` renders `button.ac-section-pause` "Pause" beside Leave, and it works well (clock stops, recording pauses, text hides, `pause_count` recorded).
Why: The 9pm client's second question ("can I stop if my roommate walks in?") is answered "no" four times before the run and "yes" during it. Either the door is lying to sell intensity, or the runner is breaking a rule the method depends on. Both cost trust.
Fix: Decide which is true. If Pause stays: door and briefing say "You can pause; the text hides while you do, and the clock stops. You cannot go back to a stimulus." and the close line becomes about the one-take recording, not the absence of a button. If Pause goes: remove the button and keep the copy.
Command: `$impeccable clarify`

**[P0] Four surfaces teach three different methods.**
What: Step 1 is "Read silently" (door `sl-l`, JSON rules) but `coach.ts` `WORKING[0].title` is "Read it, out loud in pieces", and the card then says "You read 2 of them out loud before recalling... reading silently is the harder, better version." Step 3 is "React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke" (door, briefing) and the judge's rubric penalises "a reaction that could be pasted onto any other sentence"; `coach.ts` defines it as "what that piece is doing in the argument... Evidence, conclusion, someone else's view, a concession", which is precisely the pasteable label the judge marks as "no reaction of your own". Step 4 is "The Recall" on the door, "your Read" on the ready beat, and "Your Read" / "given a Read" / "found the move" on the card; CONTEXT.md lists "The Read" under Avoid.
Why: The walkthrough is shown only to first-timers, on their first three stimuli, and it coaches them into the two faults the Report Card will then list under "Worth working on". Jordan follows the card, gets "3 with no reaction of your own", and concludes she is bad at this.
Fix: One source of truth (the instructions JSON) renders the step labels and one-line definitions on all four surfaces. Rewrite `WORKING[0]` to "Read it silently, a piece at a time. Look away, tell it back, then react to it." Rewrite the ready-beat sentence to "once when you have read it all, once when you have recalled it." Replace "Read" on the card with "The Recall" for the step and "the move" only after one definition line in the console.
Command: `$impeccable clarify` (then `$impeccable document` to put the glossary where the code reads it)

**[P0] The ending has no design when the grade is slow or missing.**
What: `SectionReport.tsx` polls every 4s for `GIVE_UP_MS = 3 min`, then "Still being marked / This one is taking longer than usual. Your run is saved and your Report Card will be here when you come back to it." with a Button labelled "Done". `SectionLibrary` then shows the attempt as `data-flat` text "still being marked / Check back shortly" with no date-time and no link.
Why: This is the exact face of the 8-hour incident. The Sweep exists in the domain model but is invisible to her and, as far as these surfaces show, to Joshua. A paying client's 25-minute effort ends on "Done" with nothing done.
Fix: The marking screen shows minutes elapsed and "usually 1 to 3 minutes". After the give-up: "We will keep marking it in the background. When it is ready it appears on your library row and in your inbox." backed by an actual inbox message on grade completion, plus a "Tell Joshua" affordance that drafts (never sends) a message with the attempt id. The library row becomes a link that reopens the marking state and shows "run at 21:03, marking since 21:29". Rename "Done" to "Back to the library".
Command: `$impeccable harden`

**[P1] Three grounds in ten seconds at the handover, and a stale comment driving one of them.**
What: `.ac-section.is-finishing` transitions the field to `hsl(38 36% 93%)` (a copy of the Activity Centre canvas), the marking screen is shell chrome, the card is plum on violet-white. The SectionRunner comment says the card is "warm paper", which was true before 2026-09-08.
Why: The one frame she is most likely to remember (the runner's own comment says so) is a flash of the colour the card brief vetoed, followed by a card in a third register.
Fix: `is-finishing` warms toward `--rc-page` (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`); the marking screen is rendered inside `.rc` using its console and display type, so the wait is the card arriving rather than the shell interrupting. Update the comment.
Command: `$impeccable polish`

**[P1] Numeracy policy differs by surface.**
What: The card brief bans Detail arithmetic and `howMuch()` says "Almost all of it came back". But the same card's console leads with "Held 82%", the library says "Attempt 2 · Sep 2 · 91% carried", and the section door's `sl-stats` show "Recalled 73/75" (which counts recordings, not recall) and "Target 25:00 met on 1 of 3".
Why: She is told the number on the door and denied it on the stimulus. Either the number matters or it does not; the product currently argues with itself, and "Recalled" as a column name for "recorded" mislabels the one number she will read first.
Fix: Pick one: either the section-level figure is allowed (then say it identically on door and card, and rename "Recalled" to "Recorded") or it is not (then the console leads with the band and "Next time", and the library row says the band). Also reconcile the Time verdict: in the harness the `faults` fixture shows 28:32 with "Inside the time" and `clean` shows 24:00 with "Over the goal"; the copy keys off `section.within_time` while the number is elapsed. Derive one from the other or show the clock the verdict used.
Command: `$impeccable distill`

**[P1] Em dashes in student copy, and hard-coded palette literals.**
What: `coach.ts` lines 62, 67, 80, 85 and `SectionReportCard.tsx` line 1273 ("Try again in a moment — nothing has been lost.") contain em dashes; PRODUCT.md makes this a binding rule for computed and utility copy alike. `section.css` uses raw slate hex in nine places.
Why: The em dash rule exists because it reads as machine-written; the walkthrough is the first thing a first-timer reads inside the drill.
Fix: Rewrite with commas and full stops; lift the slate literals into `--sec-*` tokens.
Command: `$impeccable polish`

**[P2] The ready beat asks her to read 30 words in 3 seconds while claiming to record before the run starts.**
What: `.ac-section-ready-title` "Recording now", body "One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus: once when you have read it and said it back, once when you have given your Read.", `.ac-section-ready-count` "Starting in 3", clock 0:00, and no Pause during the beat.
Why: Unreadable at that speed, uses the banned term, and contradicts the briefing's "the timer starts with it". The recorder being open before the clock is the mechanism behind an early-start recording.
Fix: The beat says "Microphone on. Starting in 3." and the level bar; the sentence moves to the briefing footer where she has time. Either start the clock with the recorder or say plainly "the recorder is on so nothing you say is lost; the clock starts at zero."
Command: `$impeccable distill`

**[P2] Card console: hero-metric block, side-stripe in the signal colour, and 22 tab stops before content.**
What: `header.rc__console` (display label, stat sentence, three Figures); the `border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)]` paragraph; `.rc__ledger` buttons at `minmax(28px, 1fr)` against PRODUCT.md's 44px target floor; each tick is a Tab stop, so keyboard users cross 22 buttons to reach the open stimulus.
Fix: Replace the stripe with a tinted field or nothing; make the ledger a roving-tabindex toolbar (one Tab stop, arrows move); raise tick hit areas to 44px; lead the console with the band and "Next time" and demote the three figures to a single line.
Command: `$impeccable layout` then `$impeccable audit`

---

### Persona red flags

**Alex (fourth section):** On the section door the method (step cards, drawer, counts, close band) is re-scrolled every visit; only the library is `sectionsFirst`. "Read the full instructions" remains a hero CTA on run four. The 3s beat cannot be skipped. On the card, 22 ledger ticks are 22 Tab stops; there is no arrow-key ledger navigation and no "Run it again" on the card except the focus set. Door stats trend Time but not Held, so the number she cares about on run four has no trend anywhere.

**Jordan (first-timer):** "Read it" silently on the door, "Read it, out loud in pieces" on the walkthrough, "reading silently is the harder, better version" on the card. Colour defined as a reaction on the door and as "Evidence, conclusion" on the walkthrough, then graded as "no reaction of your own". "given your Read" (beat) and "Your Read", "the move", "coloured" (card) undefined. Ready-beat paragraph unreadable in 3 seconds. "Check back shortly" with no time reference. She will assume she did it wrong.

**Sam (screen reader, keyboard only):** Runner is good: Space on `window`, only Pause and Leave are focusable, the instruction is `role="status"` so the phase change is announced, the clock is `aria-live="off"`, the paused card autofocuses "Back to it", the stimulus leaves the DOM on recall so nothing is read that she is meant to have looked away from. Red flags: `RecallDemo` has `aria-live="polite"` on `.rd-demo-step` (RecallDemo.tsx line 163) inside a loop that plays for as long as she is on the door, so the step name is announced every few seconds indefinitely. Card labels are 10px mono (contrast measured, but 10px). Ledger ticks are 28px targets. Focus indicators on `.ac-section-pause` / `.ac-section-exit` were not verified.

**Casey (phone):** Warned by `sl-notice` and let through (correct per Joshua's 2026-09-04 call). `useNoKeyboard` (`pointer: coarse`) renders `.ac-section-advance` "Recall full stimulus" / "Next stimulus" in the paper footer, reachable. Pause and Leave sit in the top rail, out of thumb reach, which is arguably right for Leave and wrong for Pause. A call ends the run and nothing resumes. Note that `pointer: coarse` also matches touch laptops, so a Surface user with a keyboard gets the phone warning and the button.

**Riley (stress tester):** Refresh or close mid-run: generic `beforeunload` prompt only, then the run and recording are gone with no resume. Browser back button: `beforeunload` does not fire on SPA navigation, and I found no router blocker; the runner may unmount without the Leave dialog. Not verified, must be. Mic denied: handled, with "Ask again" and an embedded-preview branch. Upload failed: handled with truthful copy. Grade never arrives: unhandled after 3 minutes. Two tabs: the grader refuses a concurrent grading of one Attempt (good); two live runners were not tested. Library fixture shows "PT102.S3 · Attempt 1 · Sep 1 · not finished" although `SectionLibrary.tsx` line 5 says a never-begun run must not be called an Attempt; confirm the fixture represents a begun-then-abandoned run.

**The 9pm paying client:** "How long will this take?" The door says "est 25 min" and "Twenty-five minutes"; the briefing says go slower while it is new; the fixtures ran 24:00 and 28:32; add the mic prompt, the beat, 1 to 3 minutes of marking, and ten minutes reading a 22-stimulus card. Nobody says "budget 40 minutes tonight". "Can I stop if my roommate walks in?" Four surfaces say no; the button says yes; she finds it only by scanning the rail while panicking.

---

### Minor observations

- "est 25 min" in `.rd-meta` is a goal, not an estimate; the briefing itself says so.
- Hero "Start the section" and close band "Start now" are two labels for one action; the library uses "Start" and "Run it again".
- `.sl-aside` "Before you start" sits below the section rows, so she reads it after choosing.
- "Done" on the marking screen while nothing is done.
- The `clean` card lists "What slipped" as a movement even when nothing slipped ("Nothing slipped on this one.").
- `Movement` labels "What you held" and "What slipped" are excellent; "Your Read" and "What you said" are the two she cannot decode.
- The runner's Recall paper is a blank white rectangle; a single faint rule or the stimulus number would keep it from reading as a broken render.
- `--rd-ink-soft` is referenced in `section-launch.css` (line ~1923 comment) as a token the file never defines; the same class of defect DESIGN.md records for the focus ring.
- Section page `rd-band` stats use a real minus sign in "−6:52": good.

---

### Questions to consider

1. If the runner honestly has a Pause, why is "No pause button" the largest line above the Start button? Which one is the drill?
2. What if the door's step cards, the briefing list and the walkthrough card were one component fed by one JSON, so the method could not drift between them?
3. Does the console need "Held 82%" at all, when the per-stimulus copy already decided arithmetic is the wrong unit? What does the card look like if it opens with "Next time"?
4. What should she see at 9:31pm when marking has not come back, what should Joshua see, and is the Sweep visible to either of them?
5. Is the marking wait the last screen of the drill or the first screen of the shell? Right now it is dressed as the shell, in the middle of the drill's world.

# RRC door and briefing page: technical audit (2026-09-09)

Method: `$impeccable audit` format. "Measured" = read from the live DOM at http://localhost:60738 in my own tab (tab-6), desktop 1280x720, mobile 375x812, tablet 768x1024. "Code" = read from source. Contrast was computed from `getComputedStyle` colours converted through a 1x1 canvas against the first non-transparent ancestor background; the hero's sky was interpolated from the seven `MeadowScene` gradient stops at the element's fractional height (the radial sun glow is not included in that estimate). The briefing page has no harness state, so it is audited from code and from token maths run in the browser.

Two limitations. Frame-rate sampling was impossible: `document.hidden` stayed true in the Browser pane even after fronting the tab, so `requestAnimationFrame` never fired (probe timed out twice). Second, a lavender-and-dark seam at the hero's foot appeared in one scrolled mobile screenshot; on re-shoot the framer transforms read `none` because the same rAF stall had frozen the scroll-linked planes. I could not reproduce it with a live compositor, so it is logged as unverified, not as a defect.

## Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Accessibility | 2 | Three targets under 44px, one invisible focus ring, a perpetual `aria-live` loop, broken heading outline |
| 2 | Performance | 2 | 191 to 196 animations running at rest, 170 of them SVG path transforms; scroll-interpolated `blur(14px)` on the headline |
| 3 | Responsive Design | 3 | No overflow at 375/768/1280; hero is 1216px tall on a 812px phone and cuts the demo at the fold |
| 4 | Theming | 2 | 30 hex literals and 28 rgba() outside the token block, several duplicating existing tokens |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | 2 | Two identical card grids, traffic-light window dots, glass chip, hover lifts on three controls |
| **Total** | | **11/20** | **Acceptable (significant work needed)** |

## Anti-Patterns Verdict

Does it read as AI-made? Not at a glance, and that is the meadow's doing: a seeded, generative SVG scene with 170 drawn blades and 30 flowers, four hand-drawn dioramas, and a dictation demo with authored copy are things a template does not produce. Underneath, the tells are there and countable:

1. **Identical card grids, twice.** Four same-size step cards (scene + mono label + serif heading + text) at `section-launch.css:772-830`, and a three-card comparison (tag + quote + note) at `:1206-1253`. Absolute ban.
2. **Traffic-light window dots** on the demo card (`:362-380`, three hard-coded hex fills). The "fake browser chrome" landing cliché.
3. **Glass chip**: `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` with translucent fill and a 1.57:1 border (`:211-225`). One instance; the only backdrop filter on the page.
4. **Hero-metric strip**: `.sl-stats` is big Fraunces number, small mono label, small supporting line, times four in a bordered card (`:1436-1482`). Mitigated: every number is derived from real runs and the empty and failed states are honest.
5. **Decorative hover lifts**: `.sl-start:hover` translateY(-2px) plus a deeper shadow (`:286-292`), `.sl-beat:hover` translateY(-4px) plus `0 30px 44px` shadow (`:795-798`), `.sl-sec-go:hover` translateY(-1px) (`:1426-1429`). The product register bans these.
6. **Drenched CTA band**: the closing panel is an indigo-to-lavender gradient with a radial apricot glow (`:1575-1583`), the standard landing-page closer.

Not present: side stripes (the only `border-left` values are 1px hairlines in the stat strip; the 1.5px `border-right` at `:1911` is a drawn chevron), gradient text, modals, em dashes in rendered copy. The cream/serif/mono editorial lane is the sanctioned brief for this door, so it is not counted, though it is the same lane Joshua vetoed for the report card as "overdone and too AI looking".

## Executive Summary

- Audit Health Score: **11/20** (Acceptable)
- Issues: **P0 0, P1 5, P2 12, P3 6**
- Top issues:
  1. The door tells her in five places that the section cannot pause; the runner has had a Pause control since d3c096f (confirmed: `SectionRunner.tsx:264-292` holds `paused` state, `SectionRunner.pause.test.tsx` clicks a button named "Pause").
  2. The demo's pause button is 28x28 and its focus ring is white on paper (1.05:1), and it is the first Tab stop on the page.
  3. `aria-live="polite"` on the demo's step label announces a new phase every 1.5 to 4.2 seconds for as long as the page is open.
  4. 196 animations run continuously at rest, 170 of them CSS transforms on individual SVG paths, plus a scroll-driven `blur(14px)` on the 96px headline.
  5. Heading outline is H1, H3, H2 on the door, and H1, H4 x6, H2 with the briefing pane open.
- Next steps: `$impeccable clarify` for the pause copy, `$impeccable harden` for targets, focus, live region and outline, `$impeccable optimize` for the animation budget, `$impeccable quieter` for the lifts and tells, `$impeccable polish` last.

## Detailed Findings by Severity

### P1

**[P1] Stale "cannot pause" copy in five places** (code, confirmed)
- Location: `SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx:58`, `SectionLaunch.tsx:329` ("you cannot pause the recording"), `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:553` ("No pause button."), `SectionBriefingPage.tsx:143` ("does not pause"), and `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json` `rules[0]` ("it never pauses"; seeded to Postgres, not rendered on the door). The noKeyboard notice at `SectionLaunch.tsx:148` ("a call or a locked screen ends the run") may also be wrong now.
- Category: Copy / Accuracy. Impact: a student who needs to stop is told she cannot; the closing panel's 58px headline is the wrong fact.
- Recommendation: one sentence, one source. Say what a pause does (stimulus hides, clock stops, one file) rather than that it does not exist. Fix the JSON too, since `content:push` is what students read.
- Command: `$impeccable clarify`

**[P1] Demo play/pause button is 28x28 with an invisible focus ring** (measured)
- Location: `RecallDemo.tsx:170-178`, `section-launch.css:423-437` (28px), `:330-332` (`.rd-hero :focus-visible { outline-color: var(--rd-on-dark) }`).
- Measured: 28x28 on desktop and mobile; focused outline `solid #ffffff` on ground `#fbf9f3`, ratio 1.05:1.
- WCAG: 2.5.8 / PRODUCT.md 44x44 binding; 2.4.7 and 1.4.11 (ring needs 3:1).
- Recommendation: 44px hit area (padding, not a bigger glyph); give the demo card its own `:focus-visible` colour (`--rd-ink`), since the hero rule was written for the dark panel and the card is paper.
- Command: `$impeccable harden`

**[P1] Breadcrumb "Library" button is 55x17** (measured)
- Location: `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:247`, `section-launch.css:185-197` (`padding: 0`).
- WCAG: 2.5.8 / PRODUCT.md 44 binding. It is the exit control.
- Recommendation: `min-height: 44px; padding: 0 6px; margin: -13px -6px` so the text stays put and the target grows.
- Command: `$impeccable harden`

**[P1] `aria-live="polite"` on a perpetual loop** (measured, `live: [{rd-demo-step, polite}]`)
- Location: `RecallDemo.tsx:163-165`; beats cycle 1.5 to 4.2 s, forever, in every state.
- Impact: a screen reader user hears "02 · Tell it back", "03 · Colour it", "Space", "04 · Recall it", "Carried" on a loop while trying to read the page. Reduced motion stops it; nothing else does.
- WCAG: 4.1.3 (status messages are for changes the user caused or needs), 2.2.2.
- Recommendation: drop `aria-live`; the demo is labelled "Demonstration" and is not state. If a live label is wanted, announce once on manual play only.
- Command: `$impeccable harden`

**[P1] Animation budget: 191 to 196 running animations at rest** (measured `document.getAnimations().length`: library 196, runs 187, after scroll 191)
- Location: `section-launch.css:147-157` (`.mw-blades path` sway, 170 paths, `MeadowScene.tsx:203-216`), `:416`, `:602`, `:619`, `:880`, `:936`, `:976`, `:1018` (all `infinite`), plus framer loops in `RecallDemo`.
- Impact: 170 individually transformed SVG paths inside one `will-change: transform` layer repaint that layer every frame; the four dioramas and the demo keep running when scrolled off screen; `RecallDemo` also holds a 1 s `setInterval` and a per-frame `setState` typewriter. Could not sample fps (pane hidden). On a phone this is a battery and heat cost on a page she reads for 25 minutes before pressing Start.
- Recommendation: pause every loop with an `IntersectionObserver` (framer `whileInView` or `animation-play-state`), sway a handful of `&lt;g&gt;` groups rather than 170 paths, and stop the demo after N loops or when off screen.
- Command: `$impeccable optimize`

**[P2] Drawer tabs are 43px tall** (measured 56x43 and 101x43 at every viewport)
- Location: `section-launch.css:1063-1077` (`padding: 8px 0 14px`, 14px text).
- Recommendation: `min-height: 44px`. Command: `$impeccable harden`

The method section, the slab ("Your sections" is a `span.sl-label`) and the closing panel have no headings; `aria-label` on `&lt;section&gt;` does not enter the outline.
- Location: `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:346, 429, 455, 502, 541, 550`.
- WCAG: 1.3.1, 2.4.6, 2.4.10. Recommendation: H2 for the method, slab and close; drawer title H3 under it; essay headings H3.

- `.sl-close` (Previous/Next step, Back to the steps), `.sl-sec-attempt`, `.rd-demo-play` all rely on a `--rd-line` border for their edge on a paper fill. PRODUCT.md asks 3:1 for interactive boundaries. Mitigation: each carries text (and the attempt rows underline their action).

- Impact: the "signature moment" is cut at the fold and the hero takes 1.5 screens; page height 6186px (runs) and 5930px (library). Tablet: 1077px hero on a 1024px viewport. Start is inside the first viewport on both (356 to 408 on mobile), which is the thing that matters most.

Keyboard behaviour is correct (roving tabindex, Arrow/Home/End, `aria-controls` target exists, `aria-labelledby` updates: all measured). Two tablists controlling one `tabpanel` id is non-standard but functional.

Move `will-change` off `.mw-grain`. `$impeccable optimize`

- **[P3] Stylesheet cruft** (code): `.sl-briefing-page .sl-title` declared twice (`:1863`, `:2055`), `.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3` twice (`:1859`, `:2102`), `.sl-bp-more &gt; summary` twice (`:1886`, `:1895`); `.sl-dr-note` and `.rd-method-head` are defined and never rendered; `.sl-briefing-page .sl-chip` (`:1843-1848`) styles a chip the page no longer renders; `.sl-bp-meta` is asserted by the test but has no CSS. `$impeccable polish`

## Measured numbers not otherwise listed

**Contrast (all pass; lowest first).** `.rd-demo-label` "Out loud" `#6a6784` on `#f4f1ea` 4.79:1 at 10px; ink-3 on cream 4.87 (briefing `.sl-bp-n`); `.sl-label`, `.sl-sd`, `th`, `small`, `.rd-step-n`, `.rd-count-tag`, `.sl-note`, `.sl-sec-clock`, `.rd-demo-foot` 5.32 on paper at 10.5 to 14px; `.sl-sv[data-dir=down]` emerald 6.08 at 34px; `.rd-demo-step` 6.85 on the marigold wash; `.rd-method-sub`, `.sl-dr-body`, `.sl-d`, aside body 8.49 to 9.27; crumbs and meta `#d0cfd9` 9.28 on `#2a2450`, 10.9 to 11.3 on the sky's top; `.sl-brief` 8.4 vs interpolated sky; tagline 12.6; H1 14.5; chip 16.0; Start 16.3; closing headline white 15.8 (top of line) to 9.8 (bottom), apricot `em` 13.3 to 8.2; cream closing button on `#564b89` 6.85, its white focus ring 7.61. Note: 10px and 10.5px mono runs (`:459`, `:387`, `:705`, `:1497`) pass the ratio but sit below the 11px the rest of the world uses.

**Focus.** Every stop reached had a visible 2px ring with offset 3px: ink on cream/paper 16.3 to 17.8:1, cream on the hero 12.9:1, white on the crumb ground 14.3:1. The one failure is the demo button (1.05:1, above). Order after the first two stops is logical; Tab from the section rows lands on Step 1 and then skips to the drawer tab (roving tabindex correct); ArrowRight moved selection and focus to Step 2 and relabelled the panel `sl-pane-tab-beat sl-beat-tab-1`.

**aria-hidden.** All seven `MeadowScene` SVGs carry `aria-hidden` and `focusable=false`; the four `StepScene` dioramas are `aria-hidden` (50 chars of decorative text hidden); `.rd-hero-space` hidden. `.mw` itself is not hidden when the demo is inside it, by design, so the demo's 459 characters are read. The `.rd-keyrow` is hidden; the demo dots are hidden.

**Reduced motion (code).** `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` at `:1760-1772` kills every animation and transition under `.sl` and resets `will-change`; the briefing page's `sl-bp-rise` is stopped at `:2028-2040`. `SectionLaunchFrame` drops the scroll transforms and blur entirely (`:277`), `MeadowScene` pins every plane to `y: 0` (`:128`), `RecallDemo` holds the final "tally" frame, disables its own button and skips the typewriter (`:126, :143, :149-150, :175`). `scrollIntoView` uses `auto` when reduced (`:216`). Not verified in the browser (no emulation available in this pane); the code paths are complete.

**Responsive.** `scrollWidth` never exceeded `innerWidth` at 375, 768 or 1280 (the 15px desktop gap is the scrollbar). Only the run table overflows, inside `.sl-scroll` (client 310, scroll 370 at 375px). Grids: beats 4/2/1, stats 4/2/1, comparison 3/1, runs two-column to one under 900, demo two-column to one under 900. Notice (`role="status"`) rendered on the mobile emulation via `(pointer: coarse)`; absent at 768 (pointer fine there). `calc(100svh - 28px)` only applies above 900px width, so a short landscape phone never hits it; on desktop 720px high the hero measured 844px, driven by the 470px stage reservation rather than by svh.

**Theming.** `?theme=dark` adds `class="dark"` to `&lt;html&gt;`; the door renders identically (`.sl` background `#f6f3ea`, hero `#2a2450`) and the body stays `#fcfcfc`, so the door is single-look by construction. Six gradients (four dioramas, closing panel, glow). 29 `box-shadow` declarations; two are hover lifts (`.sl-start:hover`, `.sl-beat:hover`), the rest depth on the demo card (three-layer, 70px), bubbles, keys, dioramas and the inverted comparison card (`0 34px 60px`).

**States.** `oneRun`: strip reads "first run / nothing to compare yet / 25/25 / met on 1 of 1", table one row. `empty`: no strip, "No runs yet. The first one is the baseline, not the test." `failed`: no strip, "Your run history is not available right now. The section still starts normally." `noHistory`: no band, no runs, but an empty `.rd-slab` still renders with 72px of top padding (`:735-739`). Console clean in all but `failed`.

## Prior critique (2026-09-08): what shipped

Shipped and verified in code: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot (`:141-145`, now wrong about pausing), `closer` rendered (`:146-148`), comment corrected (`:64-70`), notice un-boxed (`:1796-1805`), 62ch measure on steps and foot (`:1966-1975`), one mono label style for `.sl-bp-h2` and `.sl-bp-n` (`:1929-1958`), lede specificity fix (`:1156-1163`), Start lift and shadow removed on the briefing page (`:1808-1813`), drawn chevron replacing " +" (`:1907-1918`), microphone warning in the foot (`:153-155`), chip removed (`:45-46`), head stacked on purpose (`:1787-1792`), a rem type scale (`:2048-2105`), Back at 44 min-width (`:1837-1839`), per-control focus rules (`:1827-1835`). Not shipped: the `h2`-in-`summary` exposure, "Press space" on a phone, and the "The Recall." label beside three imperatives.

## Patterns and Systemic Issues

1. **Hero rules written for the dark panel leak onto light children.** `.rd-hero :focus-visible { outline-color: #fff }` produced the 1.05:1 ring on the demo; the briefing page needed 60 lines of overrides for the same reason (`:1814-1884`). Colour-by-ground should be a token per surface, not a cascade fix per page.
2. **Unscoped rules with a shared prefix.** Fifty-seven bare `.sl-*` selectors in a global bundle, one already shared with Specimen Lab.
3. **Nothing stops when it leaves the screen.** Meadow, dioramas, demo, rec dot, caret, key press: 190+ loops with no visibility gating.
4. **Facts about the runner live in five copies of prose.** The pause change proves it; the next runner change will do it again. One sentence in one place, rendered everywhere.
5. **Named greys without names.** `#cfc9bd` x7, `#d9d3c6` x3, `#e3ded2` x4 (already `--rd-line`), `#2a2450` x3 (already `--rd-indigo-2`).

## Positive Findings

- Text contrast passes everywhere measured, including the interpolated sky and the closing gradient; the worst ratio on the page is 4.79:1.
- Keyboard model is correct: roving tabindex, Arrow/Home/End, `aria-controls` targets exist, `aria-selected` and `aria-labelledby` update, `sl-beat-panel` has `tabIndex=-1`, `goToSections` focuses the slab with `preventScroll`.
- Reduced motion is handled at three layers (CSS, framer transforms, the demo's state machine) and the demo's play button disables itself under it rather than pretending.
- Honest empty, failed and loading states, distinguished from each other; the failed read never disables Start.
- No horizontal overflow at any tested width; the only wide element scrolls in its own box.
- Zero em dashes in rendered copy, in the JSON, and in student-facing strings of the door's components.
- The meadow and dioramas ship as geometry, not rasters; the demo is labelled synthetic on the card itself.
- The 2026-09-08 critique's P1s and P2s all landed, with comments explaining why.

## Recommended Actions

1. **[P1] `$impeccable clarify`**: replace the five "cannot pause" statements and `rules[0]` with one accurate sentence about what Pause does; add the on-screen-control sentence to the briefing page for coarse pointers.
2. **[P1] `$impeccable harden`**: 44px demo button, 44px crumb, 44px drawer tabs; ink focus ring inside the demo card; remove `aria-live` from the demo; restore the H2 outline; move the scene after the copy in DOM order; scope every rule under `.sl`; lift the H2 out of `summary`.
3. **[P1] `$impeccable optimize`**: gate the meadow, dioramas and demo on visibility; sway groups instead of 170 paths; drop or cap the headline blur; take `will-change` off the grain layer.
4. **[P2] `$impeccable quieter`**: remove the three hover lifts, the traffic-light dots and the glass chip; give `.sl-close` and the attempt rows a 3:1 edge.
5. **[P2] `$impeccable colorize`**: name the recurring greys and shadow rgba values as `--rd-*` tokens; replace the four literals that already have tokens.
6. **[P2] `$impeccable adapt`**: shorten the phone hero, keep the meta line under 640px, and make step selection visible on single-column layouts.
7. **[P3] `$impeccable polish`**: dedupe the briefing selectors, delete `.sl-dr-note`, `.rd-method-head`, the briefing `.sl-chip` rule; downgrade the failed-history log to warn; fix the two em dashes in `SectionProgress.tsx`.

You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer. Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see your score improve.</result>

The full audit and critique of Read, Recall, Colour is done and saved to [RRC-DRILL-AUDIT-CRITIQUE-2026-09-09.md](wiki/RRC-DRILL-AUDIT-CRITIQUE-2026-09-09.md). The first run of the four sub-agent passes was killed by a session rate limit; the retry completed all four.

**Scores.** Audit for the journey: 13/20 (door 11, runner 14, Report Card 14). Critique: 24/40. The runner is the strongest surface; the door and the ending are the weakest.

**Three P0s, all copy and flow rather than pixels:**
- **Pause contradiction.** Six student-facing statements say the section cannot pause (door close line, both asides, briefing foot, the seeded rules, the walkthrough) while the runner has a working Pause button since commit d3c096f.
- **Three methods on four surfaces.** The door says read silently; the walkthrough says read out loud in pieces; the card scolds her for reading out loud. Colour is "a reaction, a joke" on the door and "evidence, conclusion" in the walkthrough, which is exactly what the judge marks as no reaction. Step 4 is "The Recall" on the door and "your Read" in the ready beat, a retired name.
- **No design for a slow or refused grade.** After three minutes she gets "Still being marked" and a button labelled Done, then a flat library row. That is the face of the 8-hour incident, and a refused grade looks identical to a slow one.

**Verified live in the drill:** Space advances correctly; Pause blanks the paper and freezes the clock; the Leave dialog opens but Escape does not close it and Tab walks out behind it; Pause and Leave are 30px tall; the focus ring measures 2.17:1 on white and 1.0:1 on the indigo field.

**Two real bugs found in code:** the focus-set chips name the wrong stimulus (off by one at `SectionReportCard.tsx:1257`), and the upload-failed recovery sentence is overwritten within one frame because completion fires regardless.

**The plan** is 12 ordered steps in the note, copy truth first, then runner and card accessibility, then the door's animation budget and tells, then records. Two decisions are yours before steps 1 and 7: does Pause stay, and is a section-level percentage allowed anywhere a student reads.

Not verified: below-the-fold and phone visuals were DOM-measured because the browser pane returned black frames; the browser Back button mid-run was not exercised; the briefing page has no harness.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
